# Section I: Verbal & Symbolic Analogy / Series

## Chapter 1: Word & GK Analogies

### Q001 · Instrument : Measurement

Ammeter : Electric Current :: Voltmeter : ?

A. Resistance
B. Capacitance
C. Electric Charge
D. Voltage

**Answer:** D. Voltage
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Voltage (Correct):** An ammeter measures electric current, and similarly a voltmeter measures voltage (potential difference) across two points in an electric circuit.
- **A — Resistance:** Electrical resistance is measured using an ohmmeter, not a voltmeter.
- **B — Capacitance:** Capacitance is measured using a capacitance meter or LCR meter rather than a voltmeter.
- **C — Electric Charge:** Electric charge is detected or measured with an electroscope or coulometer, not a voltmeter.
- **Exam Trick:** An ammeter is always connected in series (low resistance), whereas a voltmeter is connected in parallel (very high resistance).

### Q002 · Instrument : Measurement

Thermometer : Temperature :: Barometer : ?

A. Atmospheric Pressure
B. Humidity
C. Rainfall
D. Altitude

**Answer:** A. Atmospheric Pressure
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Atmospheric Pressure (Correct):** A thermometer measures temperature, and a barometer measures atmospheric pressure.
- **B — Humidity:** Humidity is measured by a hygrometer rather than a barometer.
- **C — Rainfall:** Rainfall is measured using a rain gauge (pluviometer), not a barometer.
- **D — Altitude:** Altitude is measured using an altimeter rather than a barometer.
- **Exam Trick:** Evangelista Torricelli invented the mercury barometer in 1643; a sudden drop in barometric pressure indicates an approaching storm.

### Q003 · Instrument : Measurement

Seismograph : Earthquake Waves :: Rain Gauge : ?

A. Wind Speed
B. Rainfall
C. Atmospheric Pressure
D. Solar Radiation

**Answer:** B. Rainfall
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Rainfall (Correct):** A seismograph records earthquake waves, and a rain gauge measures the amount of rainfall at a place over a given time.
- **A — Wind Speed:** Wind speed is measured by an anemometer, not a rain gauge.
- **C — Atmospheric Pressure:** Atmospheric pressure is measured by a barometer rather than a rain gauge.
- **D — Solar Radiation:** Solar radiation is measured using a pyranometer or pyrheliometer, not a rain gauge.
- **Exam Trick:** A rain gauge is also called a pluviometer, udometer, or ombrometer in meteorological terminology.

### Q004 · Instrument : Measurement

Compass : Direction :: Anemometer : ?

A. Relative Humidity
B. Ocean Depth
C. Wind Speed
D. Blood Pressure

**Answer:** C. Wind Speed
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Wind Speed (Correct):** A compass indicates direction, and an anemometer measures wind speed.
- **A — Relative Humidity:** Relative humidity is measured by a hygrometer, not an anemometer.
- **B — Ocean Depth:** Ocean depth is measured using a fathometer rather than an anemometer.
- **D — Blood Pressure:** Blood pressure is measured by a sphygmomanometer, not an anemometer.
- **Exam Trick:** While an anemometer measures wind speed, a wind vane (weather vane) indicates wind direction.

### Q005 · Instrument : Measurement

Odometer : Distance :: Speedometer : ?

A. Acceleration
B. Speed
C. Time
D. Fuel Level

**Answer:** B. Speed
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Speed (Correct):** An odometer measures the total distance travelled by a vehicle, and a speedometer indicates its instantaneous speed.
- **A — Acceleration:** Acceleration is measured by an accelerometer rather than a speedometer.
- **C — Time:** Time intervals are measured by a clock or chronometer, not a speedometer.
- **D — Fuel Level:** Fuel quantity in a tank is indicated by a fuel gauge, not a speedometer.
- **Exam Trick:** In metric vehicles, odometers record distance in kilometres and speedometers show speed in km/h.

### Q006 · Instrument : Measurement

Clock : Time :: Hygrometer : ?

A. Purity of Milk
B. Temperature
C. Electric Power
D. Humidity

**Answer:** D. Humidity
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Humidity (Correct):** A clock measures time, and a hygrometer measures humidity (water vapour content in the air).
- **A — Purity of Milk:** Purity and density of milk are tested using a lactometer, not a hygrometer.
- **B — Temperature:** Temperature is measured by a thermometer rather than a hygrometer.
- **C — Electric Power:** Electric power is measured by a wattmeter, not a hygrometer.
- **Exam Trick:** Do not confuse hygrometer (humidity) with hydrometer (specific gravity/relative density of liquids).

### Q007 · Instrument : Measurement

Lactometer : Purity of Milk :: Sphygmomanometer : ?

A. Heart Sound
B. Lung Capacity
C. Body Temperature
D. Blood Pressure

**Answer:** D. Blood Pressure
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Blood Pressure (Correct):** A lactometer checks the purity of milk based on its density, and a sphygmomanometer measures arterial blood pressure.
- **A — Heart Sound:** Heart and lung sounds are heard using a stethoscope, not a sphygmomanometer.
- **B — Lung Capacity:** Lung capacity and airflow are measured with a spirometer rather than a sphygmomanometer.
- **C — Body Temperature:** Body temperature is measured using a clinical thermometer, not a sphygmomanometer.
- **Exam Trick:** Normal human blood pressure is typically expressed as 120/80 mmHg (systolic/diastolic).

### Q008 · Instrument : Measurement

Altimeter : Altitude :: Fathometer : ?

A. Sound Intensity
B. Heat Radiation
C. Ocean Depth
D. Light Intensity

**Answer:** C. Ocean Depth
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ocean Depth (Correct):** An altimeter measures altitude above a fixed level, and a fathometer measures ocean depth using sound waves.
- **A — Sound Intensity:** Sound intensity or hearing threshold is measured with an audiometer or decibel meter, not a fathometer.
- **B — Heat Radiation:** Heat radiation is measured using a bolometer or pyrometer rather than a fathometer.
- **D — Light Intensity:** Light intensity is measured by a lux meter or photometer, not a fathometer.
- **Exam Trick:** One fathom equals 6 feet (about 1.8288 metres), historically used for measuring water depth.

### Q009 · Instrument : Measurement

Galvanometer : Small Electric Current :: Pyrometer : ?

A. High Temperature
B. Specific Gravity
C. Atmospheric Pressure
D. Wind Direction

**Answer:** A. High Temperature
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — High Temperature (Correct):** A galvanometer detects and measures small electric currents, and a pyrometer measures very high temperatures such as those in furnaces.
- **B — Specific Gravity:** Specific gravity of liquids is measured using a hydrometer, not a pyrometer.
- **C — Atmospheric Pressure:** Atmospheric pressure is measured by a barometer rather than a pyrometer.
- **D — Wind Direction:** Wind direction is indicated by a wind vane, not a pyrometer.
- **Exam Trick:** Pyrometers work without physical contact by measuring thermal radiation emitted by the hot object.

### Q010 · Quantity : Unit

Kilogram : Mass :: Litre : ?

A. Length
B. Volume
C. Density
D. Area

**Answer:** B. Volume
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Volume (Correct):** Kilogram is a standard unit of mass, and litre is a standard metric unit of volume.
- **A — Length:** Length is measured in metres rather than litres.
- **C — Density:** Density is measured in kilograms per cubic metre, not litres.
- **D — Area:** Area is measured in square metres or hectares rather than litres.
- **Exam Trick:** One litre equals 1,000 cubic centimetres (1 dm³), while the base SI unit of volume is the cubic metre (m³).

### Q011 · Quantity : Unit

Force : Newton :: Work : ?

A. Watt
B. Pascal
C. Joule
D. Hertz

**Answer:** C. Joule
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Joule (Correct):** Newton is the SI unit of force, and joule is the SI unit of work and energy.
- **A — Watt:** Watt is the SI unit of power (rate of doing work), not work.
- **B — Pascal:** Pascal is the SI unit of pressure rather than work.
- **D — Hertz:** Hertz is the SI unit of frequency, not work.
- **Exam Trick:** One joule equals the work done when a force of 1 newton displaces an object by 1 metre (1 J = 1 N·m).

### Q012 · Quantity : Unit

Power : Watt :: Electrical Resistance : ?

A. Ohm
B. Ampere
C. Coulomb
D. Volt

**Answer:** A. Ohm
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Ohm (Correct):** Watt is the SI unit of power, and ohm is the SI unit of electrical resistance.
- **B — Ampere:** Ampere is the SI unit of electric current, not resistance.
- **C — Coulomb:** Coulomb is the SI unit of electric charge rather than resistance.
- **D — Volt:** Volt is the SI unit of electric potential difference, not resistance.
- **Exam Trick:** By Ohm's law, resistance equals voltage divided by current (1 ohm = 1 volt/ampere).

### Q013 · Quantity : Unit

Frequency : Hertz :: Pressure : ?

A. Tesla
B. Weber
C. Candela
D. Pascal

**Answer:** D. Pascal
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Pascal (Correct):** Hertz is the SI unit of frequency, and pascal is the SI unit of pressure.
- **A — Tesla:** Tesla is the SI unit of magnetic flux density, not pressure.
- **B — Weber:** Weber is the SI unit of magnetic flux rather than pressure.
- **C — Candela:** Candela is the SI unit of luminous intensity, not pressure.
- **Exam Trick:** One pascal equals one newton of force applied per square metre (1 Pa = 1 N/m²).

### Q014 · Quantity : Unit

Electric Charge : Coulomb :: Magnetic Flux : ?

A. Henry
B. Weber
C. Farad
D. Siemens

**Answer:** B. Weber
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Weber (Correct):** Coulomb is the SI unit of electric charge, and weber is the SI unit of magnetic flux.
- **A — Henry:** Henry is the SI unit of electrical inductance, not magnetic flux.
- **C — Farad:** Farad is the SI unit of electrical capacitance rather than magnetic flux.
- **D — Siemens:** Siemens is the SI unit of electrical conductance, not magnetic flux.
- **Exam Trick:** Magnetic flux density (magnetic field strength) is measured in tesla, where 1 tesla equals 1 weber per square metre.

### Q015 · Quantity : Unit

Temperature : Kelvin :: Luminous Intensity : ?

A. Mole
B. Radian
C. Candela
D. Lumen

**Answer:** C. Candela
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Candela (Correct):** Kelvin is the SI base unit of thermodynamic temperature, and candela is the SI base unit of luminous intensity.
- **A — Mole:** Mole is the SI base unit for amount of substance, not luminous intensity.
- **B — Radian:** Radian is the SI derived unit for plane angle rather than luminous intensity.
- **D — Lumen:** Lumen is the SI unit of luminous flux, whereas candela measures luminous intensity.
- **Exam Trick:** The seven SI base units are metre, kilogram, second, ampere, kelvin, mole, and candela.

### Q016 · Quantity : Unit

Capacitance : Farad :: Inductance : ?

A. Henry
B. Tesla
C. Joule
D. Becquerel

**Answer:** A. Henry
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Henry (Correct):** Farad is the SI unit of capacitance, and henry is the SI unit of electrical inductance.
- **B — Tesla:** Tesla is the SI unit of magnetic flux density rather than inductance.
- **C — Joule:** Joule is the SI unit of energy or work, not inductance.
- **D — Becquerel:** Becquerel is the SI unit of radioactivity rather than inductance.
- **Exam Trick:** Both farad (Michael Faraday) and henry (Joseph Henry) are named after 19th-century pioneers of electromagnetism.

### Q017 · Study : Subject

Ornithology : Birds :: Entomology : ?

A. Fishes
B. Reptiles
C. Snakes
D. Insects

**Answer:** D. Insects
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Insects (Correct):** Ornithology is the scientific study of birds, and entomology is the scientific study of insects.
- **A — Fishes:** The scientific study of fishes is ichthyology, not entomology.
- **B — Reptiles:** The scientific study of reptiles and amphibians is herpetology rather than entomology.
- **C — Snakes:** The specialized study of snakes is ophiology (serpentology), not entomology.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice the root 'entomon' (Greek for insect), while 'ornis' means bird.

### Q018 · Study : Subject

Mycology : Fungi :: Phycology : ?

A. Mosses
B. Algae
C. Viruses
D. Bacteria

**Answer:** B. Algae
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Algae (Correct):** Mycology is the branch of biology dealing with fungi, and phycology (algology) is the study of algae.
- **A — Mosses:** The scientific study of mosses and liverworts is bryology, not phycology.
- **C — Viruses:** The study of viruses is virology rather than phycology.
- **D — Bacteria:** The study of bacteria is bacteriology, not phycology.
- **Exam Trick:** Lichens are studied as symbiotic associations between fungi (mycobiont) and algae or cyanobacteria (phycobiont).

### Q019 · Study : Subject

Cardiology : Heart :: Nephrology : ?

A. Liver
B. Lungs
C. Kidneys
D. Brain

**Answer:** C. Kidneys
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kidneys (Correct):** Cardiology is the medical study and treatment of the heart, and nephrology deals with the kidneys.
- **A — Liver:** The medical specialty concerned with the liver is hepatology, not nephrology.
- **B — Lungs:** The medical specialty dealing with the lungs and respiratory tract is pulmonology rather than nephrology.
- **D — Brain:** The medical specialty focused on the brain and nervous system is neurology, not nephrology.
- **Exam Trick:** The functional filtration unit of the kidney is the nephron, which shares the Greek root 'nephros' (kidney).

### Q020 · Study : Subject

Pedology : Soil :: Seismology : ?

A. Earthquakes
B. Mountains
C. Glaciers
D. Oceans

**Answer:** A. Earthquakes
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Earthquakes (Correct):** Pedology is the scientific study of soil in its natural environment, and seismology is the study of earthquakes and seismic waves.
- **B — Mountains:** The study of mountains is orology, not seismology.
- **C — Glaciers:** The study of glaciers and ice sheets is glaciology rather than seismology.
- **D — Oceans:** The scientific study of oceans is oceanography, not seismology.
- **Exam Trick:** Seismology derives from the Greek 'seismos' (earthquake); do not confuse pedology (soil) with paediatrics (children's medicine).

### Q021 · Study : Subject

Ophthalmology : Eyes :: Dermatology : ?

A. Bones
B. Teeth
C. Ears
D. Skin

**Answer:** D. Skin
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Skin (Correct):** Ophthalmology is the branch of medicine concerned with the eyes, and dermatology deals with the skin.
- **A — Bones:** The study of bones is osteology, not dermatology.
- **B — Teeth:** The scientific study of teeth is odontology rather than dermatology.
- **C — Ears:** The medical study of ears is otology, not dermatology.
- **Exam Trick:** Derma derives from the Greek word for skin, which also appears in terms like epidermis and hypodermic.

### Q022 · Study : Subject

Paleontology : Fossils :: Anthropology : ?

A. Coins
B. Humans
C. Inscriptions
D. Stamps

**Answer:** B. Humans
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Humans (Correct):** Paleontology is the study of prehistoric life through fossils, and anthropology is the study of humans, human societies, and cultures.
- **A — Coins:** The study and collection of coins and currency is numismatics, not anthropology.
- **C — Inscriptions:** The study of ancient inscriptions is epigraphy rather than anthropology.
- **D — Stamps:** The collection and study of postage stamps is philately, not anthropology.
- **Exam Trick:** 'Anthropos' in Greek means human being, giving us words like anthropology and philanthropy.

### Q023 · Study : Subject

Oncology : Cancer :: Haematology : ?

A. Blood
B. Hormones
C. Tissues
D. Cells

**Answer:** A. Blood
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Blood (Correct):** Oncology is the study and treatment of cancer (tumours), and haematology is the study of blood and blood-forming organs.
- **B — Hormones:** The medical study of hormones and endocrine glands is endocrinology, not haematology.
- **C — Tissues:** The microscopic study of biological tissues is histology rather than haematology.
- **D — Cells:** The study of cells is cytology, not haematology.
- **Exam Trick:** Haemoglobin is the iron-containing oxygen-transport protein in red blood cells; 'haema' means blood in Greek.

### Q024 · Study : Subject

Botany : Plants :: Zoology : ?

A. Rocks
B. Stars
C. Animals
D. Microbes

**Answer:** C. Animals
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Animals (Correct):** Botany is the scientific study of plants, and zoology is the scientific study of animals.
- **A — Rocks:** The study of rocks and their origin is petrology, not zoology.
- **B — Stars:** The study of stars and celestial bodies is astronomy rather than zoology.
- **D — Microbes:** The study of microscopic organisms is microbiology, not zoology.
- **Exam Trick:** Aristotle is widely regarded as the Father of Zoology, while Theophrastus is known as the Father of Botany.

### Q025 · Country : Capital & Currency

India : New Delhi :: Japan : ?

A. Osaka
B. Kyoto
C. Hiroshima
D. Tokyo

**Answer:** D. Tokyo
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Tokyo (Correct):** New Delhi is the capital of India, and Tokyo is the capital of Japan.
- **A — Osaka:** Osaka is a major port and commercial city in Japan, not its capital.
- **B — Kyoto:** Kyoto is a historic cultural city and former capital of Japan, not the present capital.
- **C — Hiroshima:** Hiroshima is a major Japanese city on Honshu island, not the national capital.
- **Exam Trick:** Kyoto served as Japan's imperial capital for over a millennium before the capital moved to Tokyo in 1868.

### Q026 · Country : Capital & Currency

Australia : Canberra :: Canada : ?

A. Ottawa
B. Toronto
C. Vancouver
D. Montreal

**Answer:** A. Ottawa
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Ottawa (Correct):** Canberra is the capital of Australia, and Ottawa is the capital of Canada.
- **B — Toronto:** Toronto is Canada's largest city and the capital of Ontario province, not the national capital.
- **C — Vancouver:** Vancouver is a major Pacific port city in British Columbia, not Canada's capital.
- **D — Montreal:** Montreal is the largest city in Quebec province, not the capital of Canada.
- **Exam Trick:** Both Canberra and Ottawa were chosen as compromise capitals between rival larger cities (Sydney/Melbourne and Toronto/Montreal).

### Q027 · Country : Capital & Currency

Switzerland : Bern :: Brazil : ?

A. Rio de Janeiro
B. Brasilia
C. Sao Paulo
D. Salvador

**Answer:** B. Brasilia
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Brasilia (Correct):** Bern serves as the de facto capital (federal city) of Switzerland, and Brasilia is the capital of Brazil.
- **A — Rio de Janeiro:** Rio de Janeiro was Brazil's capital until 1960, not the current capital.
- **C — Sao Paulo:** Sao Paulo is Brazil's largest city and financial centre, not its capital.
- **D — Salvador:** Salvador was Brazil's first colonial capital until 1763, not the modern capital.
- **Exam Trick:** Brasilia was inaugurated in 1960 as a planned capital designed by Oscar Niemeyer and Lúcio Costa, replacing Rio de Janeiro.

### Q028 · Country : Capital & Currency

Egypt : Cairo :: Thailand : ?

A. Phuket
B. Chiang Mai
C. Bangkok
D. Pattaya

**Answer:** C. Bangkok
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Bangkok (Correct):** Cairo is the capital of Egypt, and Bangkok is the capital of Thailand.
- **A — Phuket:** Phuket is an island province and tourist destination in southern Thailand, not the capital.
- **B — Chiang Mai:** Chiang Mai is a major cultural city in northern Thailand, not the national capital.
- **D — Pattaya:** Pattaya is a coastal resort city in eastern Thailand, not the capital.
- **Exam Trick:** Bangkok's official ceremonial Thai name (Krung Thep...) is listed as the longest city place-name in the world.

### Q029 · Country : Capital & Currency

South Korea : Seoul :: Turkey : ?

A. Istanbul
B. Izmir
C. Antalya
D. Ankara

**Answer:** D. Ankara
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Ankara (Correct):** Seoul is the capital of South Korea, and Ankara is the capital of Turkey.
- **A — Istanbul:** Istanbul is Turkey's largest city and historical capital of the Ottoman Empire, not its current capital.
- **B — Izmir:** Izmir is a major Aegean port city in western Turkey, not the capital.
- **C — Antalya:** Antalya is a Mediterranean resort city in southern Turkey, not the national capital.
- **Exam Trick:** Ankara became the capital of the Republic of Turkey in 1923 under Mustafa Kemal Atatürk, replacing Istanbul.

### Q030 · Country : Capital & Currency

Japan : Yen :: Bangladesh : ?

A. Taka
B. Rupee
C. Kyat
D. Ngultrum

**Answer:** A. Taka
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Taka (Correct):** Yen is the official currency of Japan, and taka is the official currency of Bangladesh.
- **B — Rupee:** Rupee is the currency of India, Pakistan, Nepal, and Sri Lanka, not Bangladesh.
- **C — Kyat:** Kyat is the official currency of Myanmar rather than Bangladesh.
- **D — Ngultrum:** Ngultrum is the official currency of Bhutan, not Bangladesh.
- **Exam Trick:** The taka was introduced in Bangladesh in 1972 following its independence in 1971.

### Q031 · Country : Capital & Currency

Russia : Ruble :: South Africa : ?

A. Naira
B. Rand
C. Shilling
D. Cedi

**Answer:** B. Rand
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Rand (Correct):** Ruble is the currency of Russia, and rand is the currency of South Africa.
- **A — Naira:** Naira is the currency of Nigeria, not South Africa.
- **C — Shilling:** Shilling is used in East African countries such as Kenya, Tanzania, and Uganda, not South Africa.
- **D — Cedi:** Cedi is the official currency of Ghana rather than South Africa.
- **Exam Trick:** The South African rand (ZAR) takes its name from the Witwatersrand ridge, where most of South Africa's gold deposits were found.

### Q032 · Country : Capital & Currency

United Kingdom : Pound Sterling :: Malaysia : ?

A. Baht
B. Rupiah
C. Ringgit
D. Dong

**Answer:** C. Ringgit
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Ringgit (Correct):** Pound sterling is the currency of the United Kingdom, and ringgit is the currency of Malaysia.
- **A — Baht:** Baht is the official currency of Thailand, not Malaysia.
- **B — Rupiah:** Rupiah is the official currency of Indonesia rather than Malaysia.
- **D — Dong:** Dong is the official currency of Vietnam, not Malaysia.
- **Exam Trick:** All Southeast Asian currencies frequently appear in SSC GK questions: Malaysia (Ringgit), Thailand (Baht), Indonesia (Rupiah), Vietnam (Dong).

### Q033 · Country : Capital & Currency

UAE : Dirham :: Saudi Arabia : ?

A. Dinar
B. Lira
C. Shekel
D. Riyal

**Answer:** D. Riyal
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Riyal (Correct):** Dirham is the currency of the United Arab Emirates (UAE), and riyal is the currency of Saudi Arabia.
- **A — Dinar:** Dinar is the currency of Kuwait, Bahrain, Iraq, and Jordan, not Saudi Arabia.
- **B — Lira:** Lira is the official currency of Turkey rather than Saudi Arabia.
- **C — Shekel:** Shekel is the official currency of Israel, not Saudi Arabia.
- **Exam Trick:** Note the distinction in Middle Eastern currencies: Saudi Arabia uses the Riyal, UAE uses the Dirham, and Kuwait uses the Dinar.

### Q034 · State : Dance & Festival

Kerala : Kathakali :: Tamil Nadu : ?

A. Bharatanatyam
B. Kuchipudi
C. Odissi
D. Manipuri

**Answer:** A. Bharatanatyam
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Bharatanatyam (Correct):** Kathakali is a classical dance form of Kerala, and Bharatanatyam is the classical dance form originating from Tamil Nadu.
- **B — Kuchipudi:** Kuchipudi is a classical dance form from Andhra Pradesh, not Tamil Nadu.
- **C — Odissi:** Odissi is a classical dance form originating in Odisha rather than Tamil Nadu.
- **D — Manipuri:** Manipuri is the classical dance form of Manipur, not Tamil Nadu.
- **Exam Trick:** Sangeet Natak Akademi recognizes eight classical dance forms of India: Bharatanatyam, Kathak, Kathakali, Kuchipudi, Odissi, Manipuri, Mohiniyattam, and Sattriya.

### Q035 · State : Dance & Festival

Assam : Bihu :: Punjab : ?

A. Garba
B. Bhangra
C. Lavani
D. Ghoomar

**Answer:** B. Bhangra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Bhangra (Correct):** Bihu is the signature folk dance (and harvest festival) of Assam, and Bhangra is a famous folk dance of Punjab.
- **A — Garba:** Garba is a traditional folk dance of Gujarat, not Punjab.
- **C — Lavani:** Lavani is a popular folk dance form of Maharashtra rather than Punjab.
- **D — Ghoomar:** Ghoomar is a traditional folk dance of Rajasthan, not Punjab.
- **Exam Trick:** Assam celebrates three Bihu festivals a year: Bohag (Rongali) Bihu, Kati (Kongali) Bihu, and Magh (Bhogali) Bihu.

### Q036 · State : Dance & Festival

Andhra Pradesh : Kuchipudi :: Uttar Pradesh : ?

A. Sattriya
B. Mohiniyattam
C. Kathak
D. Chhau

**Answer:** C. Kathak
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Kathak (Correct):** Kuchipudi is a classical dance of Andhra Pradesh, and Kathak is the classical dance form traditionally associated with Uttar Pradesh and North India.
- **A — Sattriya:** Sattriya is a classical dance form from Assam, not Uttar Pradesh.
- **B — Mohiniyattam:** Mohiniyattam is a classical dance form of Kerala rather than Uttar Pradesh.
- **D — Chhau:** Chhau is a semi-classical dance tradition of eastern India (Odisha, Jharkhand, and West Bengal), not Uttar Pradesh.
- **Exam Trick:** Famous gharanas of Kathak include the Lucknow, Jaipur, and Banaras gharanas.

### Q037 · State : Dance & Festival

Gujarat : Garba :: Rajasthan : ?

A. Rouf
B. Nautanki
C. Yakshagana
D. Ghoomar

**Answer:** D. Ghoomar
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Ghoomar (Correct):** Garba is a folk dance of Gujarat, and Ghoomar is a traditional folk dance of Rajasthan.
- **A — Rouf:** Rouf is a traditional folk dance of Jammu and Kashmir, not Rajasthan.
- **B — Nautanki:** Nautanki is a folk theatre and dance tradition of Uttar Pradesh rather than Rajasthan.
- **C — Yakshagana:** Yakshagana is a traditional theatre-dance form of coastal Karnataka, not Rajasthan.
- **Exam Trick:** Ghoomar was traditionally performed by the Bhil tribe and later adopted by Rajput women during auspicious occasions.

### Q038 · State : Dance & Festival

Kerala : Onam :: Tamil Nadu : ?

A. Pongal
B. Lohri
C. Ugadi
D. Hornbill

**Answer:** A. Pongal
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Pongal (Correct):** Onam is the major harvest festival of Kerala, and Pongal is the major harvest festival of Tamil Nadu.
- **B — Lohri:** Lohri is a winter harvest festival celebrated primarily in Punjab, not Tamil Nadu.
- **C — Ugadi:** Ugadi is the New Year festival of Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, and Karnataka rather than Tamil Nadu.
- **D — Hornbill:** Hornbill Festival is a cultural festival celebrated in Nagaland, not Tamil Nadu.
- **Exam Trick:** Pongal is a four-day Tamil harvest festival celebrated in mid-January around Makar Sankranti.

### Q039 · State : Dance & Festival

Bihar : Chhath Puja :: Maharashtra : ?

A. Nuakhai
B. Ganesh Chaturthi
C. Losar
D. Wangala

**Answer:** B. Ganesh Chaturthi
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Ganesh Chaturthi (Correct):** Chhath Puja is a major festival of Bihar, and Ganesh Chaturthi is one of the most prominent festivals of Maharashtra.
- **A — Nuakhai:** Nuakhai is an agricultural festival celebrated mainly in western Odisha, not Maharashtra.
- **C — Losar:** Losar is the Tibetan New Year festival celebrated in Ladakh, Sikkim, and Arunachal Pradesh rather than Maharashtra.
- **D — Wangala:** Wangala is the post-harvest festival of the Garo tribe in Meghalaya, not Maharashtra.
- **Exam Trick:** Public celebration of Ganesh Chaturthi in Maharashtra was popularized by Bal Gangadhar Tilak in the 1890s to foster unity.

### Q040 · State : Dance & Festival

Nagaland : Hornbill Festival :: Odisha : ?

A. Thrissur Pooram
B. Baisakhi
C. Rath Yatra
D. Hemis

**Answer:** C. Rath Yatra
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Rath Yatra (Correct):** Hornbill Festival is the flagship cultural festival of Nagaland, and Rath Yatra (Puri) is a world-famous festival of Odisha.
- **A — Thrissur Pooram:** Thrissur Pooram is a famous temple festival of Kerala, not Odisha.
- **B — Baisakhi:** Baisakhi is a major spring harvest festival celebrated in Punjab and North India rather than Odisha.
- **D — Hemis:** Hemis Festival is a celebrated monastic festival of Ladakh, not Odisha.
- **Exam Trick:** The Hornbill Festival is held every December in Kisama Heritage Village near Kohima and is called the 'Festival of Festivals'.

### Q041 · State : Dance & Festival

Assam : Sattriya :: Kerala : ?

A. Gotipua
B. Kummi
C. Bhavai
D. Mohiniyattam

**Answer:** D. Mohiniyattam
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Mohiniyattam (Correct):** Sattriya is a classical dance of Assam, and Mohiniyattam is a classical dance of Kerala.
- **A — Gotipua:** Gotipua is a traditional dance form of Odisha and a precursor to Odissi, not from Kerala.
- **B — Kummi:** Kummi is a folk dance popular in Tamil Nadu rather than Kerala's classical tradition.
- **C — Bhavai:** Bhavai is a folk theatre and dance form of Gujarat and Rajasthan, not Kerala.
- **Exam Trick:** Sattriya was introduced in the 15th century by Vaishnava saint Srimanta Sankardev in Assam's monasteries (Sattras).

### Q042 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Puppy : Dog :: Kitten : ?

A. Cat
B. Fox
C. Rabbit
D. Bear

**Answer:** A. Cat
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Cat (Correct):** A puppy is the young one of a dog, and a kitten is the young one of a cat.
- **B — Fox:** The young one of a fox is called a kit, pup, or cub, not a kitten.
- **C — Rabbit:** The young one of a rabbit is called a kit or bunny rather than a kitten.
- **D — Bear:** The young one of a bear is called a cub, not a kitten.
- **Exam Trick:** Young-animal names (such as puppy, kitten, foal, cygnet, and leveret) are high-frequency vocabulary questions in SSC CHSL/CGL.

### Q043 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Cub : Lion :: Foal : ?

A. Deer
B. Horse
C. Sheep
D. Goat

**Answer:** B. Horse
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Horse (Correct):** A cub is the young one of a lion, and a foal is the young one of a horse.
- **A — Deer:** The young one of a deer is called a fawn, not a foal.
- **C — Sheep:** The young one of a sheep is called a lamb rather than a foal.
- **D — Goat:** The young one of a goat is called a kid, not a foal.
- **Exam Trick:** Specifically, a young male horse is called a colt and a young female horse is called a filly, while either is a foal.

### Q044 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Butterfly : Caterpillar :: Frog : ?

A. Maggot
B. Nymph
C. Tadpole
D. Cygnet

**Answer:** C. Tadpole
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Tadpole (Correct):** A caterpillar is the larval stage of a butterfly, and a tadpole is the aquatic larval stage of a frog.
- **A — Maggot:** A maggot is the larva of a housefly, not a frog.
- **B — Nymph:** A nymph is the immature stage of insects like cockroaches, grasshoppers, and dragonflies rather than a frog.
- **D — Cygnet:** A cygnet is a young swan, not a frog larva.
- **Exam Trick:** Both caterpillars and tadpoles undergo complete metamorphosis to reach their adult forms.

### Q045 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Swan : Cygnet :: Sheep : ?

A. Kid
B. Calf
C. Leveret
D. Lamb

**Answer:** D. Lamb
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Lamb (Correct):** A cygnet is the young one of a swan, and a lamb is the young one of a sheep.
- **A — Kid:** A kid is the young one of a goat, not a sheep.
- **B — Calf:** A calf is the young one of a cow, elephant, or whale rather than a sheep.
- **C — Leveret:** A leveret is a young hare, not a young sheep.
- **Exam Trick:** A group of sheep is called a flock, while a young goose is called a gosling.

### Q046 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Bird : Nest :: Bee : ?

A. Burrow
B. Hive
C. Web
D. Kennel

**Answer:** B. Hive
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Hive (Correct):** A bird builds and lives in a nest, and a bee lives in a hive.
- **A — Burrow:** A burrow is an underground tunnel dug by animals such as rabbits or foxes, not bees.
- **C — Web:** A web is spun by a spider rather than a bee.
- **D — Kennel:** A kennel is a shelter built for a dog, not a bee.
- **Exam Trick:** An apiary is a place where beehives are kept, whereas an aviary is a large enclosure for keeping birds.

### Q047 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Horse : Stable :: Pig : ?

A. Coop
B. Hutch
C. Sty
D. Den

**Answer:** C. Sty
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Sty (Correct):** A stable is the shelter for a horse, and a sty (pigsty) is the enclosure for a pig.
- **A — Coop:** A coop is a cage or pen for poultry such as hens, not pigs.
- **B — Hutch:** A hutch is a cage for keeping domestic rabbits rather than pigs.
- **D — Den:** A den is the natural shelter of wild carnivores such as lions or bears, not pigs.
- **Exam Trick:** Animal shelters frequently tested in SSC exams include Horse:Stable, Dog:Kennel, Cow:Byre/Shed, and Pig:Sty.

### Q048 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Army : Soldiers :: Fleet : ?

A. Bees
B. Wolves
C. Birds
D. Ships

**Answer:** D. Ships
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Ships (Correct):** An army is a collective group of soldiers, and a fleet is a collective group of ships.
- **A — Bees:** A group of bees is called a swarm or colony, not a fleet.
- **B — Wolves:** A group of wolves is called a pack rather than a fleet.
- **C — Birds:** A group of birds is called a flock, not a fleet.
- **Exam Trick:** When referring to aircraft operated together, 'fleet' or 'squadron' is used, whereas for warships or vessels, 'fleet' is standard.

### Q049 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Owl : Parliament :: Crow : ?

A. Murder
B. Pride
C. Pod
D. School

**Answer:** A. Murder
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Murder (Correct):** A parliament is the collective noun for a group of owls, and a murder is the collective noun for a group of crows.
- **B — Pride:** A pride is a group of lions, not crows.
- **C — Pod:** A pod is a group of whales or dolphins rather than crows.
- **D — School:** A school is a group of fish, not crows.
- **Exam Trick:** Distinctive collective nouns for birds often tested in vocabulary sections include Murder (crows), Parliament (owls), and Exaltation (larks).

### Q050 · Animal : Young, Group & Habitat

Snake : Hiss :: Elephant : ?

A. Howl
B. Trumpet
C. Neigh
D. Bleat

**Answer:** B. Trumpet
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Trumpet (Correct):** Hiss is the characteristic sound made by a snake, and trumpet is the characteristic sound made by an elephant.
- **A — Howl:** Howl is the characteristic sound of a wolf or jackal, not an elephant.
- **C — Neigh:** Neigh is the sound produced by a horse rather than an elephant.
- **D — Bleat:** Bleat is the sound made by a sheep or goat, not an elephant.
- **Exam Trick:** Keep these animal sounds handy: Lion (roar), Elephant (trumpet), Horse (neigh), Wolf (howl), and Frog (croak).

### Q051 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Doctor : Hospital :: Teacher : ?

A. Courtroom
B. Observatory
C. School
D. Studio

**Answer:** C. School
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — School (Correct):** A doctor's primary workplace is a hospital, and a teacher's primary workplace is a school.
- **A — Courtroom:** A courtroom is the workplace of a judge or lawyer, not a school teacher.
- **B — Observatory:** An observatory is the workplace of an astronomer rather than a teacher.
- **D — Studio:** A studio is the workplace of an artist, photographer, or broadcaster, not a teacher.
- **Exam Trick:** Worker-and-workplace analogies pair a profession directly with its institutional setting (e.g., Judge:Courtroom, Chef:Kitchen).

### Q052 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Doctor : Patient :: Teacher : ?

A. Client
B. Passenger
C. Customer
D. Student

**Answer:** D. Student
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Student (Correct):** A doctor treats a patient, and similarly a teacher instructs a student.
- **A — Client:** A client is served by a lawyer, architect, or consultant rather than a school teacher.
- **B — Passenger:** A passenger is carried by a driver, pilot, or conductor, not taught by a teacher.
- **C — Customer:** A customer buys goods from a shopkeeper or vendor rather than a teacher.
- **Exam Trick:** This analogy tests the Professional : Recipient/Beneficiary relationship, distinct from Professional : Workplace.

### Q053 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Painter : Brush :: Writer : ?

A. Pen
B. Chisel
C. Scalpel
D. Anvil

**Answer:** A. Pen
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Pen (Correct):** A brush is the primary tool used by a painter, and a pen is the primary tool used by a writer.
- **B — Chisel:** A chisel is used by a sculptor or carpenter, not a writer.
- **C — Scalpel:** A scalpel is a surgical knife used by a surgeon rather than a writer.
- **D — Anvil:** An anvil is the heavy iron block used by a blacksmith, not a writer.
- **Exam Trick:** Tool-user pairs are classic SSC Reasoning questions: Painter:Brush, Writer:Pen, Sculptor:Chisel, Surgeon:Scalpel.

### Q054 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Carpenter : Wood :: Mason : ?

A. Leather
B. Stone
C. Iron
D. Cloth

**Answer:** B. Stone
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Stone (Correct):** A carpenter shapes and builds with wood, and a mason builds with stone or brick.
- **A — Leather:** Leather is the primary material worked by a cobbler or saddler, not a mason.
- **C — Iron:** Iron is the metal forged by a blacksmith rather than a mason.
- **D — Cloth:** Cloth is the material stitched by a tailor, not a mason.
- **Exam Trick:** Worker-and-material pairs focus on the raw material worked upon rather than the tool used.

### Q055 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Author : Book :: Composer : ?

A. Painting
B. Statue
C. Music
D. Building

**Answer:** C. Music
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Music (Correct):** An author creates a book, and a composer creates music.
- **A — Painting:** A painting is created by a painter, not a music composer.
- **B — Statue:** A statue is carved or cast by a sculptor rather than a composer.
- **D — Building:** A building is designed by an architect, not a composer.
- **Exam Trick:** Make sure to check whether the second term is the creator's final product (Author:Book) or tool (Author:Pen).

### Q056 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Chalk : Blackboard :: Paint : ?

A. Stage
B. Loom
C. Anvil
D. Canvas

**Answer:** D. Canvas
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Canvas (Correct):** Chalk is applied to write or draw on a blackboard, and paint is applied to create artwork on a canvas.
- **A — Stage:** A stage is the platform where actors or dancers perform, not a surface for paint.
- **B — Loom:** A loom is the machine used by a weaver to make cloth, not a surface for painting.
- **C — Anvil:** An anvil is the iron block on which a blacksmith hammers heated metal, not for paint.
- **Exam Trick:** Canvas is a heavy, closely woven fabric (traditionally linen or cotton) stretched over a wooden frame for oil or acrylic painting.

### Q057 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Blacksmith : Anvil :: Cobbler : ?

A. Awl
B. Trowel
C. Spade
D. Sickle

**Answer:** A. Awl
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Awl (Correct):** An anvil is a signature tool of a blacksmith, and an awl is a pointed tool used by a cobbler to pierce leather.
- **B — Trowel:** A trowel is a flat-bladed hand tool used by a mason or gardener, not a cobbler.
- **C — Spade:** A spade is a digging tool used by a gardener or labourer rather than a cobbler.
- **D — Sickle:** A sickle is a curved-blade tool used by a farmer to harvest crops, not a cobbler.
- **Exam Trick:** Keep these distinctive tools in mind: Cobbler (awl), Mason (trowel), Tailor (needle/shears), Lumberjack (axe).

### Q058 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Chef : Kitchen :: Astronomer : ?

A. Laboratory
B. Observatory
C. Conservatory
D. Granary

**Answer:** B. Observatory
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Observatory (Correct):** A chef works in a kitchen, and an astronomer works in an observatory to study celestial objects.
- **A — Laboratory:** A laboratory is the workplace of a chemist, physicist, or biologist rather than specifically an astronomer.
- **C — Conservatory:** A conservatory is a school for music/arts or a glasshouse for plants, not an astronomer's workplace.
- **D — Granary:** A granary is a storehouse for threshed grain, not a workplace for an astronomer.
- **Exam Trick:** India's major astronomical observatories include the Indian Astronomical Observatory at Hanle (Ladakh) and Vainu Bappu Observatory (Tamil Nadu).

### Q059 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Tailor : Scissors :: Surgeon : ?

A. Plough
B. Harrow
C. Scalpel
D. Chisel

**Answer:** C. Scalpel
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Scalpel (Correct):** Scissors are used by a tailor to cut cloth, and a scalpel is used by a surgeon to make precise incisions.
- **A — Plough:** A plough is a farm implement pulled across soil by a farmer, not a surgeon.
- **B — Harrow:** A harrow is an agricultural tool used to break up clods and level soil, not used by a surgeon.
- **D — Chisel:** A chisel is a cutting tool used on wood or stone by carpenters and sculptors, not a surgeon.
- **Exam Trick:** A scalpel has a very sharp detachable blade designed for surgical dissection.

### Q060 · Worker : Tool, Workplace & Product

Baker : Bread :: Potter : ?

A. Shoes
B. Furniture
C. Garments
D. Earthenware

**Answer:** D. Earthenware
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Earthenware (Correct):** A baker makes bread, and a potter shapes and fires earthenware (clay pots and vessels).
- **A — Shoes:** Shoes are made and repaired by a cobbler, not a potter.
- **B — Furniture:** Furniture is crafted by a carpenter rather than a potter.
- **C — Garments:** Garments are stitched by a tailor, not a potter.
- **Exam Trick:** A potter uses a potter's wheel to shape wet clay and a kiln to bake and harden the earthenware.

### Q061 · Part, Function & Relationship

Foot : Shoe :: Hand : ?

A. Glove
B. Helmet
C. Belt
D. Scarf

**Answer:** A. Glove
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Glove (Correct):** A shoe is worn to cover and protect the foot, and a glove is worn to cover and protect the hand.
- **B — Helmet:** A helmet is worn on the head for protection, not on the hand.
- **C — Belt:** A belt is worn around the waist rather than on the hand.
- **D — Scarf:** A scarf is worn around the neck or head, not on the hand.
- **Exam Trick:** Clothing-and-body-part pairs are straightforward: Foot:Shoe, Hand:Glove, Head:Helmet/Hat, Waist:Belt.

### Q062 · Part, Function & Relationship

Fish : Water :: Bird : ?

A. Land
B. Air
C. Soil
D. Cave

**Answer:** B. Air
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Air (Correct):** A fish moves and lives in water, and a bird flies and moves through air.
- **A — Land:** Land is the primary medium for terrestrial animals like lions or deer, not flying birds.
- **C — Soil:** Soil is the habitat of burrowing organisms like earthworms rather than birds.
- **D — Cave:** Caves serve as shelters for bats or bears, not the general medium of movement for birds.
- **Exam Trick:** This analogy pairs an organism with its primary medium of locomotion (Fish:Water :: Bird:Air).

### Q063 · Part, Function & Relationship

Pen : Write :: Knife : ?

A. Erase
B. Stitch
C. Cut
D. Measure

**Answer:** C. Cut
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Cut (Correct):** The primary function of a pen is to write, and the primary function of a knife is to cut.
- **A — Erase:** Erasing pencil marks is the function of an eraser, not a knife.
- **B — Stitch:** Stitching fabric is the function of a needle rather than a knife.
- **D — Measure:** Measuring length is the function of a ruler or measuring tape, not a knife.
- **Exam Trick:** Object-to-action analogies match an everyday tool directly with its core verb function.

### Q064 · Part, Function & Relationship

Eye : See :: Ear : ?

A. Smell
B. Taste
C. Touch
D. Hear

**Answer:** D. Hear
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Hear (Correct):** The eye is the sense organ for seeing, and the ear is the sense organ for hearing.
- **A — Smell:** Smelling is the sensory function of the nose, not the ear.
- **B — Taste:** Tasting is the sensory function of the tongue rather than the ear.
- **C — Touch:** Touching or feeling pressure/temperature is the sensory function of the skin, not the ear.
- **Exam Trick:** The five traditional sense organs map cleanly: Eye:See, Ear:Hear, Nose:Smell, Tongue:Taste, Skin:Touch.

### Q065 · Part, Function & Relationship

Seed : Plant :: Egg : ?

A. Bird
B. Nest
C. Feather
D. Shell

**Answer:** A. Bird
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Bird (Correct):** A seed develops into a plant, and similarly a fertilized egg develops into a bird.
- **B — Nest:** A nest is the shelter built by a bird, not the organism that grows out of an egg.
- **C — Feather:** A feather is an external body covering of a bird rather than the organism itself.
- **D — Shell:** A shell is the protective outer casing of the egg, not what the egg develops into.
- **Exam Trick:** This tests the Origin/Precursor : Mature Organism relationship; ensure both sides follow the same developmental direction.

### Q066 · Part, Function & Relationship

Pond : Water :: Orchard : ?

A. Sand
B. Trees
C. Grass
D. Rocks

**Answer:** B. Trees
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Trees (Correct):** A pond is a body characterized by containing water, and an orchard is a cultivated area characterized by containing fruit trees.
- **A — Sand:** Sand is characteristic of a desert or beach, not an orchard.
- **C — Grass:** Grass characterizes a meadow or lawn rather than an orchard.
- **D — Rocks:** Rocks characterize a quarry or rocky terrain, not an orchard.
- **Exam Trick:** Similarly, a vineyard is a plantation of grapevines, and an apiary is a collection of beehives.

### Q067 · Part, Function & Relationship

Page : Book :: Brick : ?

A. Curtain
B. Carpet
C. Wall
D. Chandelier

**Answer:** C. Wall
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Wall (Correct):** Pages are bound together to form a book, and bricks are laid together to build a wall.
- **A — Curtain:** A curtain is a fabric hanging placed over a window, not constructed from bricks.
- **B — Carpet:** A carpet is a textile floor covering rather than a brick structure.
- **D — Chandelier:** A chandelier is a decorative hanging light fixture, not built out of bricks.
- **Exam Trick:** This is a classic Part : Whole structural analogy; check that the first term is the repeating building unit of the second.

### Q068 · Part, Function & Relationship

Smile : Happiness :: Tears : ?

A. Pride
B. Courage
C. Wisdom
D. Grief

**Answer:** D. Grief
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Grief (Correct):** A smile is an outward expression of happiness, and tears are an outward expression of grief.
- **A — Pride:** Pride is a feeling of self-satisfaction or dignity, not characteristically expressed by tears of sorrow.
- **B — Courage:** Courage is bravery in facing danger rather than an emotion directly symbolized by tears.
- **C — Wisdom:** Wisdom is sound judgement from knowledge and experience, not an emotion expressed through tears.
- **Exam Trick:** Expression-to-emotion pairs also include Frown:Anger/Displeasure and Blush:Embarrassment.

### Q069 · Part, Function & Relationship

January : Month :: Monday : ?

A. Day
B. Week
C. Year
D. Season

**Answer:** A. Day
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Day (Correct):** January is an example of a month, and Monday is an example of a day.
- **B — Week:** A week is a period of seven days, not the category to which Monday belongs as a unit.
- **C — Year:** A year is a 12-month period rather than the unit category for Monday.
- **D — Season:** A season is a climatic division of the year such as summer or winter, not a day.
- **Exam Trick:** This is an Instance : Category relationship; do not confuse it with Part : Whole (such as Day : Week).

### Q070 · Part, Function & Relationship

Leaf : Tree :: Petal : ?

A. Root
B. Flower
C. Stem
D. Branch

**Answer:** B. Flower
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Flower (Correct):** A leaf is a characteristic part of a tree, and a petal is a characteristic part of a flower.
- **A — Root:** The root is the underground anchoring and absorbing part of a plant, not made up of petals.
- **C — Stem:** The stem is the main supporting stalk of a plant rather than the structure formed by petals.
- **D — Branch:** A branch is a woody limb growing from a tree trunk, not the part composed of petals.
- **Exam Trick:** Collectively, all the petals of a flower form the corolla, while all the sepals form the calyx.

### Q071 · Synonym & Antonym

Abundant : Plentiful :: Scarce : ?

A. Ample
B. Copious
C. Rare
D. Excessive

**Answer:** C. Rare
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Rare (Correct):** Abundant and plentiful are synonyms meaning existing in large quantities, just as scarce and rare are synonyms meaning hard to find or insufficient.
- **A — Ample:** Ample means plentiful or more than enough, making it an antonym rather than a synonym of scarce.
- **B — Copious:** Copious means abundant in supply, which is opposite in meaning to scarce.
- **D — Excessive:** Excessive means more than necessary, contrasting with the shortage implied by scarce.
- **Exam Trick:** In vocabulary analogies, always check first whether the given pair shows a synonym or an antonym relationship before choosing.

### Q072 · Synonym & Antonym

Benevolent : Cruel :: Optimist : ?

A. Pessimist
B. Idealist
C. Realist
D. Altruist

**Answer:** A. Pessimist
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Pessimist (Correct):** Benevolent (kind) and cruel (unkind) are antonyms, just as optimist (one who expects positive outcomes) and pessimist (one who expects negative outcomes) are antonyms.
- **B — Idealist:** An idealist pursues noble principles or ideals, which is not the opposite of an optimist.
- **C — Realist:** A realist views situations practically as they are rather than serving as the antonym of an optimist.
- **D — Altruist:** An altruist acts selflessly for others' welfare, which contrasts with an egoist rather than an optimist.
- **Exam Trick:** Optimist comes from Latin 'optimus' (best), while pessimist comes from Latin 'pessimus' (worst).

### Q073 · Synonym & Antonym

Zenith : Nadir :: Ascend : ?

A. Climb
B. Descend
C. Elevate
D. Soar

**Answer:** B. Descend
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Descend (Correct):** Zenith (highest point) and nadir (lowest point) are antonyms, just as ascend (go up) and descend (go down) are antonyms.
- **A — Climb:** Climb means to move upward, which is a synonym rather than the antonym of ascend.
- **C — Elevate:** Elevate means to raise or lift up, matching the upward direction of ascend.
- **D — Soar:** Soar means to rise high into the air, making it a synonym of ascend rather than its opposite.
- **Exam Trick:** Zenith and nadir are frequently tested one-word substitutions and antonyms in SSC CGL Tier-I and Tier-II.

### Q074 · Synonym & Antonym

Frugal : Extravagant :: Hostile : ?

A. Aggressive
B. Bitter
C. Friendly
D. Antagonistic

**Answer:** C. Friendly
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Friendly (Correct):** Frugal (economical/sparing) and extravagant (wasteful/lavish) are antonyms, just as hostile (unfriendly) and friendly are antonyms.
- **A — Aggressive:** Aggressive means hostile or ready to attack, which aligns with hostile instead of opposing it.
- **B — Bitter:** Bitter describes resentful or harsh feelings, similar in tone to hostile rather than its opposite.
- **D — Antagonistic:** Antagonistic means actively opposing or unfriendly, making it a synonym of hostile.
- **Exam Trick:** Useful synonyms for frugal include thrifty and economical, while prodigal and spendthrift are synonyms for extravagant.

### Q075 · Science & General Knowledge

Vitamin C : Scurvy :: Vitamin D : ?

A. Rickets
B. Beriberi
C. Goitre
D. Night Blindness

**Answer:** A. Rickets
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Rickets (Correct):** Deficiency of Vitamin C causes scurvy, and deficiency of Vitamin D causes rickets (softening and weakening of bones in children).
- **B — Beriberi:** Beriberi is caused by a deficiency of Vitamin B1 (thiamine), not Vitamin D.
- **C — Goitre:** Goitre is caused by a deficiency of iodine rather than Vitamin D.
- **D — Night Blindness:** Night blindness is caused by a deficiency of Vitamin A, not Vitamin D.
- **Exam Trick:** In adults, severe Vitamin D deficiency causes osteomalacia, whereas in children it causes rickets.

---

## Chapter 2: Number, Letter & Triplet Analogies

### Q076 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
12 : 156 :: 15 : ?

A. 225
B. 240
C. 210
D. 256

**Answer:** B. 240
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 240 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 + n. Here 12^2 + 12 = 144 + 12 = 156. Similarly, 15^2 + 15 = 225 + 15 = 240.
- **A — 225:** The value 225 is simply 15^2, omitting the +15 step.
- **C — 210:** The value 210 equals 15^2 - 15 (i.e., n^2 - n) instead of n^2 + n.
- **D — 256:** The value 256 is 16^2, which is +16 above 240 and does not follow n^2 + n.
- **Exam Trick:** Recognize n(n + 1) = n^2 + n quickly by factoring: 156 = 12 * 13, so 15 * 16 = 240.

### Q077 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
14 : 182 :: 19 : ?

A. 342
B. 361
C. 380
D. 324

**Answer:** A. 342
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 342 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 - n (or n(n - 1)). For 14: 14^2 - 14 = 196 - 14 = 182. For 19: 19^2 - 19 = 361 - 19 = 342.
- **B — 361:** The value 361 is 19^2 without subtracting 19.
- **C — 380:** The value 380 equals 19^2 + 19 (n^2 + n) rather than n^2 - n.
- **D — 324:** The value 324 is 18^2, which is 18 less than the required 342.
- **Exam Trick:** Memorizing n(n - 1) products up to n = 25 helps spot numbers like 182 (14*13) and 342 (19*18) in seconds.

### Q078 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
7 : 350 :: 9 : ?

A. 720
B. 729
C. 738
D. 748

**Answer:** C. 738
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 738 (Correct):** The rule is n : n^3 + n. Thus 7^3 + 7 = 343 + 7 = 350, and 9^3 + 9 = 729 + 9 = 738.
- **A — 720:** The value 720 equals 9^3 - 9 (729 - 9), using subtraction instead of addition.
- **B — 729:** The value 729 is just 9^3 without adding 9.
- **D — 748:** The value 748 equals 9^3 + 19, adding 10 more than n.
- **Exam Trick:** Whenever a second number sits just above a perfect cube (e.g., 350 near 343), test n^3 + n or n^3 + n^2 first.

### Q079 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
6 : 210 :: 8 : ?

A. 520
B. 512
C. 496
D. 504

**Answer:** D. 504
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 504 (Correct):** The rule is n : n^3 - n. Here 6^3 - 6 = 216 - 6 = 210. Applying the same rule to 8 gives 8^3 - 8 = 512 - 8 = 504.
- **A — 520:** The value 520 equals 8^3 + 8, adding n instead of subtracting n.
- **B — 512:** The value 512 is 8^3 alone, missing the -8 subtraction.
- **C — 496:** The value 496 equals 8^3 - 16, subtracting 2n instead of n.
- **Exam Trick:** Note that n^3 - n = (n - 1) * n * (n + 1) is always a product of three consecutive integers (7 * 8 * 9 = 504).

### Q080 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
5 : 150 :: 7 : ?

A. 343
B. 392
C. 400
D. 384

**Answer:** B. 392
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 392 (Correct):** The relationship is n : n^3 + n^2. For 5: 5^3 + 5^2 = 125 + 25 = 150. For 7: 7^3 + 7^2 = 343 + 49 = 392.
- **A — 343:** The value 343 is only 7^3, omitting +7^2 (+49).
- **C — 400:** The value 400 equals 20^2 and is +8 above the required 392.
- **D — 384:** The value 384 equals 7^3 + 41, which is 8 short of 7^3 + 7^2.
- **Exam Trick:** Factoring n^3 + n^2 as n^2 * (n + 1) makes calculation fast: 49 * 8 = 392.

### Q081 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
13 : 196 :: 17 : ?

A. 324
B. 289
C. 306
D. 361

**Answer:** A. 324
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 324 (Correct):** The pattern is n : (n + 1)^2. For 13: (13 + 1)^2 = 14^2 = 196. For 17: (17 + 1)^2 = 18^2 = 324.
- **B — 289:** The value 289 is 17^2 (n^2) rather than (n + 1)^2.
- **C — 306:** The value 306 equals 17 * 18 (n^2 + n) instead of 18^2.
- **D — 361:** The value 361 is 19^2, which corresponds to (n + 2)^2.
- **Exam Trick:** Always memorize squares up to 30^2 so numbers like 196 (14^2) and 324 (18^2) jump out immediately.

### Q082 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
18 : 289 :: 22 : ?

A. 484
B. 420
C. 441
D. 462

**Answer:** C. 441
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 441 (Correct):** The pattern is n : (n - 1)^2. For 18: (18 - 1)^2 = 17^2 = 289. For 22: (22 - 1)^2 = 21^2 = 441.
- **A — 484:** The value 484 is 22^2 (n^2), failing to subtract 1 before squaring.
- **B — 420:** The value 420 equals 21^2 - 21 (21 * 20) instead of 21^2.
- **D — 462:** The value 462 equals 22 * 21 (n(n - 1)) rather than (n - 1)^2.
- **Exam Trick:** Check whether the square root of the second term is n + 1 or n - 1 before assuming a complex polynomial.

### Q083 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
9 : 162 :: 12 : ?

A. 264
B. 296
C. 312
D. 288

**Answer:** D. 288
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 288 (Correct):** The relation is n : 2n^2. Thus 2 * 9^2 = 2 * 81 = 162, and 2 * 12^2 = 2 * 144 = 288.
- **A — 264:** The value 264 equals 2 * 12^2 - 24 (24 less than 288).
- **B — 296:** The value 296 is 8 more than 288 and has no valid 2n^2 relation.
- **C — 312:** The value 312 equals 12 * 26, which is 24 more than 288.
- **Exam Trick:** When the second number is even and twice a familiar square (162 = 2 * 81), test 2n^2 immediately.

### Q084 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
16 : 128 :: 22 : ?

A. 220
B. 242
C. 264
D. 252

**Answer:** B. 242
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 242 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 / 2. For 16: 16^2 / 2 = 256 / 2 = 128. For 22: 22^2 / 2 = 484 / 2 = 242.
- **A — 220:** The value 220 equals 22 * 10, missing one factor of 11 (22 / 2 = 11).
- **C — 264:** The value 264 equals 22 * 12, which is +22 above 242.
- **D — 252:** The value 252 is 10 more than 242 and is not divisible by 11.
- **Exam Trick:** An equivalent way to view n^2 / 2 for even n is n * (n / 2): 16 * 8 = 128 and 22 * 11 = 242.

### Q085 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
23 : 25 :: 45 : ?

A. 81
B. 72
C. 90
D. 64

**Answer:** A. 81
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 81 (Correct):** The second number is the square of the sum of digits of the first number: (2 + 3)^2 = 5^2 = 25. Similarly, (4 + 5)^2 = 9^2 = 81.
- **B — 72:** The value 72 equals (4 + 5) * 8 instead of (4 + 5)^2.
- **C — 90:** The value 90 equals 45 * 2, which would give 46 for 23 instead of 25.
- **D — 64:** The value 64 is 8^2, corresponding to a digit sum of 8 rather than 9.
- **Exam Trick:** If the first number is a two-digit number and the second is a small perfect square, check (sum of digits)^2 or (difference of digits)^2.

### Q086 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
34 : 25 :: 56 : ?

A. 55
B. 65
C. 61
D. 59

**Answer:** C. 61
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 61 (Correct):** The second number equals the sum of the squares of the digits of the first number: 3^2 + 4^2 = 9 + 16 = 25. Thus 5^2 + 6^2 = 25 + 36 = 61.
- **A — 55:** The value 55 equals 5 * 11 and is 6 less than 25 + 36 = 61.
- **B — 65:** The value 65 equals 5^2 + 6^2 + 4 (off by +4 from 61).
- **D — 59:** The value 59 is 2 less than 61 and does not equal 25 + 36.
- **Exam Trick:** Classic digit-square pattern: 3^2 + 4^2 = 25 is a Pythagorean pair, making 34 : 25 a frequent SSC trap.

### Q087 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
43 : 19 :: 65 : ?

A. 39
B. 43
C. 37
D. 41

**Answer:** D. 41
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 41 (Correct):** The rule is ab : (a * b) + (a + b). For 43: (4 * 3) + (4 + 3) = 12 + 7 = 19. For 65: (6 * 5) + (6 + 5) = 30 + 11 = 41.
- **A — 39:** The value 39 is 2 less than 41 (missing 2 from the digit sum 6 + 5 = 11).
- **B — 43:** The value 43 is 2 more than 41.
- **C — 37:** The value 37 equals 6 * 5 + 7, using the digit sum of 43 instead of 65.
- **Exam Trick:** Note that ab + a + b = (a + 1)(b + 1) - 1; here (6 + 1)(5 + 1) - 1 = 42 - 1 = 41.

### Q088 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
58 : 40 :: 76 : ?

A. 44
B. 42
C. 38
D. 48

**Answer:** B. 42
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 42 (Correct):** The second number is the product of the digits of the first number: 5 * 8 = 40. Similarly, 7 * 6 = 42.
- **A — 44:** The value 44 equals 7 * 6 + 2 (off by +2).
- **C — 38:** The value 38 equals 7 * 6 - 4 (off by -4).
- **D — 48:** The value 48 equals 6 * 8 instead of 7 * 6.
- **Exam Trick:** When a two-digit number maps to a smaller number around 20-70, always test digit product (a * b) early.

### Q089 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
123 : 14 :: 234 : ?

A. 29
B. 27
C. 31
D. 25

**Answer:** A. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 29 (Correct):** The second number is the sum of the squares of the digits: 1^2 + 2^2 + 3^2 = 1 + 4 + 9 = 14. For 234: 2^2 + 3^2 + 4^2 = 4 + 9 + 16 = 29.
- **B — 27:** The value 27 equals 3^3 and is 2 less than 4 + 9 + 16 = 29.
- **C — 31:** The value 31 is 2 more than 29.
- **D — 25:** The value 25 equals 3^2 + 4^2, omitting 2^2 (+4).
- **Exam Trick:** For 3-digit numbers paired with small two-digit numbers, check sum of digits, product of digits, and sum of squares of digits.

### Q090 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
15 : 90 :: 18 : ?

A. 144
B. 152
C. 162
D. 171

**Answer:** C. 162
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 162 (Correct):** Each number is multiplied by the sum of its digits: 15 * (1 + 5) = 15 * 6 = 90. For 18: 18 * (1 + 8) = 18 * 9 = 162.
- **A — 144:** The value 144 equals 18 * 8, multiplying by 8 instead of 1 + 8 = 9.
- **B — 152:** The value 152 is not a multiple of 18 (18 * 8 = 144, 18 * 9 = 162).
- **D — 171:** The value 171 equals 19 * 9 instead of 18 * 9.
- **Exam Trick:** If n * k gives the second term, check whether k is fixed or equals the digit sum of n.

### Q091 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
11 : 120 :: 15 : ?

A. 226
B. 220
C. 228
D. 224

**Answer:** D. 224
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 224 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 - 1. For 11: 11^2 - 1 = 121 - 1 = 120. For 15: 15^2 - 1 = 225 - 1 = 224.
- **A — 226:** The value 226 equals 15^2 + 1 (n^2 + 1) instead of n^2 - 1.
- **B — 220:** The value 220 equals 15^2 - 5, subtracting 5 instead of 1.
- **C — 228:** The value 228 equals 15^2 + 3, adding 3 instead of subtracting 1.
- **Exam Trick:** Recall that n^2 - 1 = (n - 1)(n + 1); for n = 15, 14 * 16 = 224.

### Q092 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
12 : 157 :: 14 : ?

A. 209
B. 211
C. 213
D. 197

**Answer:** B. 211
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 211 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 + n + 1. For 12: 12^2 + 12 + 1 = 144 + 13 = 157. For 14: 14^2 + 14 + 1 = 196 + 15 = 211.
- **A — 209:** The value 209 equals 14^2 + 14 - 1 (n^2 + n - 1) instead of n^2 + n + 1.
- **C — 213:** The value 213 equals 14^2 + 17, which is 2 more than 211.
- **D — 197:** The value 197 equals 14^2 + 1, missing the +14 term.
- **Exam Trick:** When n^2 + n is off by 1, test n^2 + n + 1 or n^2 + n - 1.

### Q093 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
9 : 243 :: 12 : ?

A. 576
B. 512
C. 432
D. 648

**Answer:** A. 576
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 576 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^3 / 3. Specifically, 9^3 / 3 = 729 / 3 = 243, and 12^3 / 3 = 1728 / 3 = 576.
- **B — 512:** The value 512 is 8^3 and does not equal 12^3 / 3.
- **C — 432:** The value 432 equals 12^3 / 4 (3 * 144), dividing by 4 instead of 3.
- **D — 648:** The value 648 equals 18 * 36 and is 72 above 576.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that n^3 / 3 = n^2 * (n / 3); for multiples of 3 this is always an integer (144 * 4 = 576).

### Q094 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
16 : 56 :: 32 : ?

A. 96
B. 108
C. 112
D. 128

**Answer:** C. 112
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 112 (Correct):** The second number is 3.5 times the first number (i.e., n * 7 / 2). Here 16 * 3.5 = 56, and 32 * 3.5 = 112.
- **A — 96:** The value 96 equals 32 * 3, using a multiplier of 3 instead of 3.5.
- **B — 108:** The value 108 equals 32 * 3 + 12, which is 4 less than 112.
- **D — 128:** The value 128 equals 32 * 4, using a multiplier of 4 instead of 3.5.
- **Exam Trick:** Half-integer multipliers (1.5, 2.5, 3.5) are common in SSC CGL/CHSL when both numbers are even.

### Q095 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
12 : 149 :: 15 : ?

A. 220
B. 235
C. 240
D. 230

**Answer:** D. 230
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 230 (Correct):** The pattern is n : n^2 + 5. For 12: 12^2 + 5 = 144 + 5 = 149. For 15: 15^2 + 5 = 225 + 5 = 230.
- **A — 220:** The value 220 equals 15^2 - 5, subtracting 5 instead of adding 5.
- **B — 235:** The value 235 equals 15^2 + 10, adding 10 instead of 5.
- **C — 240:** The value 240 equals 15^2 + 15 (n^2 + n) rather than n^2 + 5.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check constant offsets from n^2 (such as n^2 + k) when 149 sits 5 above 144.

### Q096 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
3 : 243 :: 5 : ?

A. 625
B. 3125
C. 1250
D. 2500

**Answer:** B. 3125
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 3125 (Correct):** The relationship is n : n^5. Here 3^5 = 243, so 5^5 = 3125.
- **A — 625:** The value 625 is 5^4, one power short of 5^5.
- **C — 1250:** The value 1250 equals 2 * 5^4 instead of 5 * 5^4.
- **D — 2500:** The value 2500 equals 4 * 5^4 (50^2) rather than 5^5.
- **Exam Trick:** Knowing powers of small integers (2^5 = 32, 3^5 = 243, 4^5 = 1024, 5^5 = 3125) saves valuable exam time.

### Q097 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
121 : 12 :: 169 : ?

A. 14
B. 13
C. 15
D. 16

**Answer:** A. 14
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 14 (Correct):** The pattern is n^2 : n + 1. Since 121 = 11^2, the second term is 11 + 1 = 12. Since 169 = 13^2, the missing term is 13 + 1 = 14.
- **B — 13:** The value 13 is just sqrt(169) without adding 1.
- **C — 15:** The value 15 equals sqrt(169) + 2, adding 2 instead of 1.
- **D — 16:** The value 16 equals sqrt(169) + 3, adding 3 instead of 1.
- **Exam Trick:** In reverse-square analogies (large : small), take the square root of the first term first.

### Q098 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
216 : 36 :: 343 : ?

A. 42
B. 56
C. 49
D. 64

**Answer:** C. 49
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 49 (Correct):** The pattern is n^3 : n^2. Since 216 = 6^3 and 36 = 6^2, for 343 = 7^3 the related number is 7^2 = 49.
- **A — 42:** The value 42 equals 7 * 6 instead of 7^2.
- **B — 56:** The value 56 equals 7 * 8 instead of 7^2.
- **D — 64:** The value 64 is 8^2 (or 4^3), not 7^2.
- **Exam Trick:** Whenever both terms are powers of the same base (216 = 6^3, 36 = 6^2), express them as n^3 : n^2.

### Q099 · Number-Pair Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third number in the same way as the second number is related to the first number.
11 : 169 :: 17 : ?

A. 324
B. 343
C. 400
D. 361

**Answer:** D. 361
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 361 (Correct):** The second number is the square of the next prime number after the first. After 11, the next prime is 13 (13^2 = 169). After 17, the next prime is 19 (19^2 = 361).
- **A — 324:** The value 324 is 18^2, and 18 is composite rather than the next prime after 17.
- **B — 343:** The value 343 is 7^3 and is not a square of any prime.
- **C — 400:** The value 400 is 20^2, and 20 is composite.
- **Exam Trick:** Prime-number analogies often use (next prime)^2; 17 -> 19 -> 361 is a classic CGL/CHSL pattern.

### Q100 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(4, 12, 16)

A. (5, 11, 18)
B. (7, 15, 22)
C. (6, 14, 19)
D. (8, 13, 23)

**Answer:** B. (7, 15, 22)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (7, 15, 22) (Correct):** The third number is the sum of the first two numbers: 4 + 12 = 16. Similarly, 7 + 15 = 22.
- **A — (5, 11, 18):** The set (5, 11, 18) gives 5 + 11 = 16 instead of 18.
- **C — (6, 14, 19):** The set (6, 14, 19) gives 6 + 14 = 20 instead of 19.
- **D — (8, 13, 23):** The set (8, 13, 23) gives 8 + 13 = 21 instead of 23.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check the simplest arithmetic relation c = a + b or c = b - a first before testing products.

### Q101 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(6, 9, 45)

A. (8, 11, 57)
B. (7, 10, 50)
C. (5, 12, 48)
D. (9, 13, 64)

**Answer:** A. (8, 11, 57)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (8, 11, 57) (Correct):** The third number equals 3 times the sum of the first two: (6 + 9) * 3 = 15 * 3 = 45. For (8, 11, 57): (8 + 11) * 3 = 19 * 3 = 57.
- **B — (7, 10, 50):** The set (7, 10, 50) gives (7 + 10) * 3 = 51 instead of 50.
- **C — (5, 12, 48):** The set (5, 12, 48) gives (5 + 12) * 3 = 51 instead of 48.
- **D — (9, 13, 64):** The set (9, 13, 64) gives (9 + 13) * 3 = 66 instead of 64.
- **Exam Trick:** When the third number is a multiple of (a + b), divide c by (a + b) to find the constant multiplier.

### Q102 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(5, 7, 74)

A. (6, 8, 98)
B. (3, 10, 107)
C. (4, 9, 97)
D. (5, 8, 87)

**Answer:** C. (4, 9, 97)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — (4, 9, 97) (Correct):** The third number is the sum of the squares of the first two: 5^2 + 7^2 = 25 + 49 = 74. Likewise, 4^2 + 9^2 = 16 + 81 = 97.
- **A — (6, 8, 98):** The set (6, 8, 98) gives 6^2 + 8^2 = 36 + 64 = 100 instead of 98.
- **B — (3, 10, 107):** The set (3, 10, 107) gives 3^2 + 10^2 = 9 + 100 = 109 instead of 107.
- **D — (5, 8, 87):** The set (5, 8, 87) gives 5^2 + 8^2 = 25 + 64 = 89 instead of 87.
- **Exam Trick:** In triplet questions, if c is near a^2 + b^2, compute a^2 + b^2 mentally using unit digits first (16 + 81 ends in 7).

### Q103 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(12, 18, 15)

A. (14, 24, 20)
B. (18, 30, 23)
C. (10, 22, 17)
D. (16, 28, 22)

**Answer:** D. (16, 28, 22)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — (16, 28, 22) (Correct):** The third number is the average of the first two: (12 + 18) / 2 = 15. Similarly, (16 + 28) / 2 = 44 / 2 = 22.
- **A — (14, 24, 20):** The set (14, 24, 20) has average (14 + 24) / 2 = 19 instead of 20.
- **B — (18, 30, 23):** The set (18, 30, 23) has average (18 + 30) / 2 = 24 instead of 23.
- **C — (10, 22, 17):** The set (10, 22, 17) has average (10 + 22) / 2 = 16 instead of 17.
- **Exam Trick:** When the third number lies halfway between the first two, test c = (a + b) / 2.

### Q104 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(7, 13, 36)

A. (8, 14, 32)
B. (9, 16, 49)
C. (6, 15, 72)
D. (11, 18, 42)

**Answer:** B. (9, 16, 49)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (9, 16, 49) (Correct):** The third number is the square of the difference of the first two: (13 - 7)^2 = 6^2 = 36. Similarly, (16 - 9)^2 = 7^2 = 49.
- **A — (8, 14, 32):** The set (8, 14, 32) gives (14 - 8)^2 = 36 instead of 32.
- **C — (6, 15, 72):** The set (6, 15, 72) gives (15 - 6)^2 = 81 instead of 72.
- **D — (11, 18, 42):** The set (11, 18, 42) gives (18 - 11)^2 = 49 instead of 42.
- **Exam Trick:** If the third number in a triplet is a perfect square, immediately check (b - a)^2 or (a + b)^2.

### Q105 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(8, 12, 48)

A. (10, 14, 70)
B. (6, 16, 44)
C. (12, 15, 85)
D. (9, 12, 52)

**Answer:** A. (10, 14, 70)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (10, 14, 70) (Correct):** The third number is half the product of the first two: (8 * 12) / 2 = 48. Likewise, (10 * 14) / 2 = 70.
- **B — (6, 16, 44):** The set (6, 16, 44) gives (6 * 16) / 2 = 48 instead of 44.
- **C — (12, 15, 85):** The set (12, 15, 85) gives (12 * 15) / 2 = 90 instead of 85.
- **D — (9, 12, 52):** The set (9, 12, 52) gives (9 * 12) / 2 = 54 instead of 52.
- **Exam Trick:** Half-product c = (a * b) / 2 is a staple SSC triplet rule—easy to spot when c is smaller than a * b.

### Q106 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(3, 12, 30)

A. (4, 20, 64)
B. (6, 42, 216)
C. (5, 30, 130)
D. (2, 6, 12)

**Answer:** C. (5, 30, 130)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — (5, 30, 130) (Correct):** The pattern is (a, a^2 + a, a^3 + a). For a = 3: 3^2 + 3 = 12 and 3^3 + 3 = 30. For a = 5: 5^2 + 5 = 30 and 5^3 + 5 = 130.
- **A — (4, 20, 64):** The set (4, 20, 64) has 4^3 + 4 = 68 instead of 64.
- **B — (6, 42, 216):** The set (6, 42, 216) has 6^3 + 6 = 222 instead of 216.
- **D — (2, 6, 12):** The set (2, 6, 12) has 2^3 + 2 = 10 instead of 12.
- **Exam Trick:** When b and c grow like square and cube of a, test (a, a^2 + a, a^3 + a) or (a, a^2 - a, a^3 - a).

### Q107 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(9, 15, 25)

A. (8, 16, 30)
B. (6, 12, 20)
C. (9, 18, 32)
D. (4, 12, 36)

**Answer:** D. (4, 12, 36)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — (4, 12, 36) (Correct):** The three numbers form a geometric progression where a * c = b^2: 9 * 25 = 225 = 15^2. Similarly, 4 * 36 = 144 = 12^2.
- **A — (8, 16, 30):** The set (8, 16, 30) gives 8 * 30 = 240, which does not equal 16^2 = 256.
- **B — (6, 12, 20):** The set (6, 12, 20) gives 6 * 20 = 120, which does not equal 12^2 = 144.
- **C — (9, 18, 32):** The set (9, 18, 32) gives 9 * 32 = 288, which does not equal 18^2 = 324.
- **Exam Trick:** In a geometric progression (a, b, c), the middle term is the geometric mean: b = sqrt(a * c).

### Q108 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(11, 13, 48)

A. (5, 8, 36)
B. (7, 9, 32)
C. (6, 10, 60)
D. (8, 11, 55)

**Answer:** B. (7, 9, 32)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (7, 9, 32) (Correct):** The third number is the difference of squares: b^2 - a^2. Here 13^2 - 11^2 = 169 - 121 = 48. Similarly, 9^2 - 7^2 = 81 - 49 = 32.
- **A — (5, 8, 36):** The set (5, 8, 36) gives 8^2 - 5^2 = 64 - 25 = 39 instead of 36.
- **C — (6, 10, 60):** The set (6, 10, 60) gives 10^2 - 6^2 = 100 - 36 = 64 instead of 60.
- **D — (8, 11, 55):** The set (8, 11, 55) gives 11^2 - 8^2 = 121 - 64 = 57 instead of 55.
- **Exam Trick:** Use b^2 - a^2 = (b - a)(b + a) for instant calculation: (9 - 7)(9 + 7) = 2 * 16 = 32.

### Q109 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(6, 11, 28)

A. (8, 14, 36)
B. (7, 12, 30)
C. (9, 15, 40)
D. (5, 13, 33)

**Answer:** A. (8, 14, 36)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (8, 14, 36) (Correct):** The third number follows c = 2b + a: 2(11) + 6 = 22 + 6 = 28. Applying this to (8, 14, 36): 2(14) + 8 = 28 + 8 = 36.
- **B — (7, 12, 30):** The set (7, 12, 30) gives 2(12) + 7 = 31 instead of 30.
- **C — (9, 15, 40):** The set (9, 15, 40) gives 2(15) + 9 = 39 instead of 40.
- **D — (5, 13, 33):** The set (5, 13, 33) gives 2(13) + 5 = 31 instead of 33.
- **Exam Trick:** Linear combinations like 2b + a or 2a + b are very common when c is roughly 2 to 3 times b.

### Q110 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(10, 15, 35)

A. (12, 18, 40)
B. (16, 24, 52)
C. (14, 21, 49)
D. (8, 12, 30)

**Answer:** C. (14, 21, 49)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — (14, 21, 49) (Correct):** The three numbers are in the ratio 2 : 3 : 7 (10 = 5*2, 15 = 5*3, 35 = 5*7). Similarly, (14, 21, 49) = (7*2, 7*3, 7*7).
- **A — (12, 18, 40):** The set (12, 18, 40) has 2 : 3 for the first two (6*2, 6*3), but the third should be 6 * 7 = 42 instead of 40.
- **B — (16, 24, 52):** The set (16, 24, 52) has 8 * 2 = 16 and 8 * 3 = 24, so the third should be 8 * 7 = 56 instead of 52.
- **D — (8, 12, 30):** The set (8, 12, 30) has 4 * 2 = 8 and 4 * 3 = 12, so the third should be 4 * 7 = 28 instead of 30.
- **Exam Trick:** Divide all three numbers by their HCF to spot fixed-ratio triplets like 2 : 3 : 7 immediately.

### Q111 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(6, 8, 62)

A. (4, 9, 45)
B. (7, 8, 69)
C. (3, 11, 43)
D. (5, 7, 47)

**Answer:** D. (5, 7, 47)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — (5, 7, 47) (Correct):** The third number is a * b + a + b: 6 * 8 + 6 + 8 = 48 + 14 = 62. Similarly, 5 * 7 + 5 + 7 = 35 + 12 = 47.
- **A — (4, 9, 45):** The set (4, 9, 45) gives 4 * 9 + 4 + 9 = 36 + 13 = 49 instead of 45.
- **B — (7, 8, 69):** The set (7, 8, 69) gives 7 * 8 + 7 + 8 = 56 + 15 = 71 instead of 69.
- **C — (3, 11, 43):** The set (3, 11, 43) gives 3 * 11 + 3 + 11 = 33 + 14 = 47 instead of 43.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that a * b + a + b = (a + 1)(b + 1) - 1; here 6 * 8 - 1 = 47.

### Q112 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(16, 24, 12)

A. (12, 18, 10)
B. (20, 30, 15)
C. (24, 36, 16)
D. (8, 12, 8)

**Answer:** B. (20, 30, 15)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
