- **B — (20, 30, 15) (Correct):** The numbers are in the ratio 4 : 6 : 3 (b = 1.5a and c = 0.75a, or b/2 = c). For 16: 16 * 1.5 = 24 and 24 / 2 = 12. For 20: 20 * 1.5 = 30 and 30 / 2 = 15.
- **A — (12, 18, 10):** The set (12, 18, 10) has 18 / 2 = 9 instead of 10.
- **C — (24, 36, 16):** The set (24, 36, 16) has 36 / 2 = 18 instead of 16.
- **D — (8, 12, 8):** The set (8, 12, 8) has 12 / 2 = 6 instead of 8.
- **Exam Trick:** When numbers are not monotonic, check pairwise ratios like b = 1.5a and c = b / 2.

### Q113 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(7, 21, 84)

A. (5, 15, 60)
B. (6, 18, 64)
C. (8, 24, 88)
D. (9, 27, 98)

**Answer:** A. (5, 15, 60)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (5, 15, 60) (Correct):** The second number is 3 times the first, and the third is 4 times the second: 7 * 3 = 21 and 21 * 4 = 84. Likewise, 5 * 3 = 15 and 15 * 4 = 60.
- **B — (6, 18, 64):** The set (6, 18, 64) has 18 * 4 = 72 instead of 64.
- **C — (8, 24, 88):** The set (8, 24, 88) has 24 * 4 = 96 instead of 88.
- **D — (9, 27, 98):** The set (9, 27, 98) has 27 * 4 = 108 instead of 98.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check consecutive multipliers (b / a and c / b) when each number divides the next cleanly.

### Q114 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(13, 29, 61)

A. (15, 25, 43)
B. (9, 19, 37)
C. (11, 23, 47)
D. (12, 22, 40)

**Answer:** C. (11, 23, 47)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — (11, 23, 47) (Correct):** The difference between consecutive terms doubles: 29 - 13 = 16 and 61 - 29 = 32 (2 * 16). In (11, 23, 47), 23 - 11 = 12 and 47 - 23 = 24 (2 * 12).
- **A — (15, 25, 43):** The set (15, 25, 43) has differences +10 and +18 instead of +10 and +20.
- **B — (9, 19, 37):** The set (9, 19, 37) has differences +10 and +18 instead of +10 and +20.
- **D — (12, 22, 40):** The set (12, 22, 40) has differences +10 and +18 instead of +10 and +20.
- **Exam Trick:** Check (b - a) vs (c - b); a 1 : 2 difference ratio (c - b = 2(b - a)) is equivalent to c = 3b - 2a.

### Q115 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(5, 3, 98)

A. (4, 2, 52)
B. (7, 5, 212)
C. (5, 2, 115)
D. (6, 4, 152)

**Answer:** D. (6, 4, 152)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — (6, 4, 152) (Correct):** The third number equals a^3 - b^3: 5^3 - 3^3 = 125 - 27 = 98. Similarly, 6^3 - 4^3 = 216 - 64 = 152.
- **A — (4, 2, 52):** The set (4, 2, 52) gives 4^3 - 2^3 = 64 - 8 = 56 instead of 52.
- **B — (7, 5, 212):** The set (7, 5, 212) gives 7^3 - 5^3 = 343 - 125 = 218 instead of 212.
- **C — (5, 2, 115):** The set (5, 2, 115) gives 5^3 - 2^3 = 125 - 8 = 117 instead of 115.
- **Exam Trick:** When c is large and a > b, test a^3 - b^3 and a^3 + b^3.

### Q116 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(7, 5, 84)

A. (6, 8, 80)
B. (9, 4, 117)
C. (8, 3, 86)
D. (5, 7, 55)

**Answer:** B. (9, 4, 117)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (9, 4, 117) (Correct):** The third number is (a + b) * a: (7 + 5) * 7 = 12 * 7 = 84. Similarly, (9 + 4) * 9 = 13 * 9 = 117.
- **A — (6, 8, 80):** The set (6, 8, 80) gives (6 + 8) * 6 = 84 instead of 80.
- **C — (8, 3, 86):** The set (8, 3, 86) gives (8 + 3) * 8 = 88 instead of 86.
- **D — (5, 7, 55):** The set (5, 7, 55) gives (5 + 7) * 5 = 60 instead of 55.
- **Exam Trick:** If c is divisible by a, divide c by a first—here 84 / 7 = 12 = 7 + 5 reveals the rule immediately.

### Q117 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(4, 5, 40)

A. (6, 7, 84)
B. (3, 8, 44)
C. (5, 6, 54)
D. (7, 4, 52)

**Answer:** A. (6, 7, 84)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (6, 7, 84) (Correct):** The third number is twice the product of the first two: 2 * 4 * 5 = 40. Similarly, 2 * 6 * 7 = 84.
- **B — (3, 8, 44):** The set (3, 8, 44) gives 2 * 3 * 8 = 48 instead of 44.
- **C — (5, 6, 54):** The set (5, 6, 54) gives 2 * 5 * 6 = 60 instead of 54.
- **D — (7, 4, 52):** The set (7, 4, 52) gives 2 * 7 * 4 = 56 instead of 52.
- **Exam Trick:** Compare c against a * b: if c is 2ab or 3ab, the ratio c / (ab) stays constant across the correct option.

### Q118 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(5, 12, 13)

A. (7, 24, 26)
B. (9, 12, 16)
C. (8, 15, 17)
D. (6, 8, 12)

**Answer:** C. (8, 15, 17)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — (8, 15, 17) (Correct):** The numbers form a Pythagorean triplet (a^2 + b^2 = c^2): 5^2 + 12^2 = 25 + 144 = 169 = 13^2. Similarly, 8^2 + 15^2 = 64 + 225 = 289 = 17^2.
- **A — (7, 24, 26):** The set (7, 24, 26) gives 7^2 + 24^2 = 625 = 25^2, not 26^2 = 676.
- **B — (9, 12, 16):** The set (9, 12, 16) gives 9^2 + 12^2 = 225 = 15^2, not 16^2 = 256.
- **D — (6, 8, 12):** The set (6, 8, 12) gives 6^2 + 8^2 = 100 = 10^2, not 12^2 = 144.
- **Exam Trick:** Memorize standard Pythagorean triplets: (3,4,5), (5,12,13), (7,24,25), (8,15,17), (9,40,41).

### Q119 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(2, 6, 14)

A. (4, 10, 20)
B. (3, 8, 16)
C. (6, 14, 28)
D. (5, 12, 26)

**Answer:** D. (5, 12, 26)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — (5, 12, 26) (Correct):** Each subsequent term is twice the previous term plus 2: 2*2 + 2 = 6 and 6*2 + 2 = 14. Similarly, 5*2 + 2 = 12 and 12*2 + 2 = 26.
- **A — (4, 10, 20):** The set (4, 10, 20) has 10*2 + 2 = 22 instead of 20.
- **B — (3, 8, 16):** The set (3, 8, 16) has 8*2 + 2 = 18 instead of 16.
- **C — (6, 14, 28):** The set (6, 14, 28) has 14*2 + 2 = 30 instead of 28.
- **Exam Trick:** When terms roughly double at each step, test *2 + k or *2 - k.

### Q120 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(12, 36, 72)

A. (14, 42, 80)
B. (15, 45, 90)
C. (16, 48, 92)
D. (11, 33, 62)

**Answer:** B. (15, 45, 90)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — (15, 45, 90) (Correct):** The second number is 3 times the first, and the third is 2 times the second: 12 * 3 = 36, 36 * 2 = 72. Likewise, 15 * 3 = 45, 45 * 2 = 90.
- **A — (14, 42, 80):** The set (14, 42, 80) has 42 * 2 = 84 instead of 80.
- **C — (16, 48, 92):** The set (16, 48, 92) has 48 * 2 = 96 instead of 92.
- **D — (11, 33, 62):** The set (11, 33, 62) has 33 * 2 = 66 instead of 62.
- **Exam Trick:** Even when all distractors match b = 3a, verify the second step (c = 2b) carefully.

### Q121 · Triplet Set Analogy

Select the set in which the numbers are related in the same way as the numbers of the following set.
(12, 18, 20)

A. (14, 19, 17)
B. (15, 16, 18)
C. (11, 20, 21)
D. (13, 17, 19)

**Answer:** A. (14, 19, 17)
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — (14, 19, 17) (Correct):** The sum of all three numbers in the set is 50: 12 + 18 + 20 = 50. Similarly, 14 + 19 + 17 = 50.
- **B — (15, 16, 18):** The set (15, 16, 18) sums to 49 instead of 50.
- **C — (11, 20, 21):** The set (11, 20, 21) sums to 52 instead of 50.
- **D — (13, 17, 19):** The set (13, 17, 19) sums to 49 instead of 50.
- **Exam Trick:** If no clear pairwise formula works in a triplet, always check the total sum a + b + c.

### Q122 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
CARD : FDUG :: MIST : ?

A. PLWV
B. PLUV
C. PLVW
D. QLVW

**Answer:** C. PLVW
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — PLVW (Correct):** Each letter shifts +3 forward: C(3)->F(6), A(1)->D(4), R(18)->U(21), D(4)->G(7). Applying +3 to MIST: M(13)->P(16), I(9)->L(12), S(19)->V(22), T(20)->W(23), giving PLVW.
- **A — PLWV:** Cluster PLWV swaps the last two shifted letters (W is +4 from S, V is +2 from T).
- **B — PLUV:** Cluster PLUV has U (+2 from S) and V (+2 from T) instead of V and W (+3).
- **D — QLVW:** Cluster QLVW shifts M by +4 to Q instead of +3 to P.
- **Exam Trick:** Write letter positions (EJOTY = 5, 10, 15, 20, 25) to verify constant shifts without counting on fingers.

### Q123 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
GOLD : CKHZ :: MINT : ?

A. IEJQ
B. IFJP
C. HEJP
D. IEJP

**Answer:** D. IEJP
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — IEJP (Correct):** Each letter shifts -4 backward (with wrap-around A->Z): G(7)->C(3), O(15)->K(11), L(12)->H(8), D(4)->Z(26). Applying -4 to MINT: M(13)->I(9), I(9)->E(5), N(14)->J(10), T(20)->P(16), giving IEJP.
- **A — IEJQ:** Cluster IEJQ shifts T by -3 to Q instead of -4 to P.
- **B — IFJP:** Cluster IFJP shifts I by -3 to F instead of -4 to E.
- **C — HEJP:** Cluster HEJP shifts M by -5 to H instead of -4 to I.
- **Exam Trick:** For backward wrap-around past A, add 26 before subtracting: D(4) - 4 = 0 -> 26 (Z).

### Q124 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
BEAR : CGDV :: LION : ?

A. MLRR
B. MKRR
C. MKQR
D. MKRQ

**Answer:** B. MKRR
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — MKRR (Correct):** The shifts increase progressively by +1, +2, +3, +4: B+1=C, E+2=G, A+3=D, R+4=V. For LION: L+1=M, I+2=K, O+3=R, N+4=R, giving MKRR.
- **A — MLRR:** Cluster MLRR shifts I by +3 to L instead of +2 to K.
- **C — MKQR:** Cluster MKQR shifts O by +2 to Q instead of +3 to R.
- **D — MKRQ:** Cluster MKRQ shifts N by +3 to Q instead of +4 to R.
- **Exam Trick:** Progressive shifts (+1, +2, +3, +4) are among the top 3 most frequent SSC letter-cluster patterns.

### Q125 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
STAR : RRXN :: MOON : ?

A. LMLJ
B. LMKJ
C. LNLJ
D. LMLK

**Answer:** A. LMLJ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — LMLJ (Correct):** The shifts are -1, -2, -3, -4: S-1=R, T-2=R, A-3=X, R-4=N. For MOON: M-1=L, O-2=M, O-3=L, N-4=J, giving LMLJ.
- **B — LMKJ:** Cluster LMKJ shifts the second O by -4 to K instead of -3 to L.
- **C — LNLJ:** Cluster LNLJ shifts the first O by -1 to N instead of -2 to M.
- **D — LMLK:** Cluster LMLK shifts N by -3 to K instead of -4 to J.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that identical letters in the input (like OO in MOON) map to different letters (M and L) under progressive shifts.

### Q126 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
WORK : YMTI :: PLAY : ?

A. RJCV
B. RJCX
C. RJCW
D. RKCW

**Answer:** C. RJCW
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — RJCW (Correct):** The pattern alternates +2, -2, +2, -2: W+2=Y, O-2=M, R+2=T, K-2=I. For PLAY: P+2=R, L-2=J, A+2=C, Y-2=W, giving RJCW.
- **A — RJCV:** Cluster RJCV shifts Y by -3 to V instead of -2 to W.
- **B — RJCX:** Cluster RJCX shifts Y by -1 to X instead of -2 to W.
- **D — RKCW:** Cluster RKCW shifts L by -1 to K instead of -2 to J.
- **Exam Trick:** Alternating +/- shifts are easy to spot when the first letter moves forward and the second moves backward.

### Q127 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
LAMP : OZPO :: DESK : ?

A. GDWK
B. GCVJ
C. HDVJ
D. GDVJ

**Answer:** D. GDVJ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — GDVJ (Correct):** The pattern alternates +3, -1, +3, -1: L+3=O, A-1=Z, M+3=P, P-1=O. Applying this to DESK: D+3=G, E-1=D, S+3=V, K-1=J, giving GDVJ.
- **A — GDWK:** Cluster GDWK shifts S by +4 to W and K by 0 to K instead of +3 and -1.
- **B — GCVJ:** Cluster GCVJ shifts E by -2 to C instead of -1 to D.
- **C — HDVJ:** Cluster HDVJ shifts D by +4 to H instead of +3 to G.
- **Exam Trick:** When A maps to Z, check -1 backward wrap-around (1 - 1 = 0 -> 26 = Z) rather than opposite pairs.

### Q128 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
KING : PRMT :: GOLD : ?

A. TLOW
B. TLOX
C. TMOW
D. SLOW

**Answer:** A. TLOW
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — TLOW (Correct):** Each letter is replaced by its opposite A-Z pair (sum of positions = 27): K(11)-P(16), I(9)-R(18), N(14)-M(13), G(7)-T(20). For GOLD: G(7)-T(20), O(15)-L(12), L(12)-O(15), D(4)-W(23), giving TLOW.
- **B — TLOX:** Cluster TLOX pairs D(4) with X(24), summing to 28 instead of 27 (W is 23).
- **C — TMOW:** Cluster TMOW pairs L(12) with M(13), summing to 25 instead of 27 (O is 15).
- **D — SLOW:** Cluster SLOW pairs G(7) with S(19), summing to 26 instead of 27 (T is 20).
- **Exam Trick:** Opposite letter pairs always sum to 27 (e.g., G=7 and T=20 -> 27; D=4 and W=23 -> 27).

### Q129 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
MATH : SGZN :: BOOK : ?

A. YLLP
B. PLLY
C. PLMY
D. QLLY

**Answer:** B. PLLY
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — PLLY (Correct):** Each letter is replaced by its opposite A-Z pair and the result is reversed: MATH -> NZGS -> reversed SGZN. For BOOK: B-Y, O-L, O-L, K-P gives YLLP, which reversed is PLLY.
- **A — YLLP:** Cluster YLLP forgets to reverse the opposite-pair string.
- **C — PLMY:** Cluster PLMY uses M(13) instead of L(12) as the opposite of O(15).
- **D — QLLY:** Cluster QLLY pairs K(11) with Q(17), summing to 28 instead of 27 (P is 16).
- **Exam Trick:** If neither direct shift nor direct opposite matches, check the last letter of the word against the first letter of the code (reverse order).

### Q130 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
CARE : YAJW :: HOME : ?

A. SLNV
B. TMOV
C. TMOW
D. UMOW

**Answer:** C. TMOW
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — TMOW (Correct):** Each letter is replaced by its opposite A-Z pair and then shifted +1: C->X+1=Y, A->Z+1=A, R->I+1=J, E->V+1=W. For HOME: H->S+1=T, O->L+1=M, M->N+1=O, E->V+1=W, giving TMOW.
- **A — SLNV:** Cluster SLNV uses the exact opposite pairs of HOME without the +1 shift.
- **B — TMOV:** Cluster TMOV forgets to apply +1 to the last letter V (should be W).
- **D — UMOW:** Cluster UMOW shifts S by +2 to U instead of +1 to T.
- **Exam Trick:** Opposite-plus-1 means the position sum of original and coded letter is 28 (except Z->A wrap-around).

### Q131 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
TIME : FNJU :: WORK : ?

A. XPSM
B. LRPX
C. LSPW
D. LSPX

**Answer:** D. LSPX
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — LSPX (Correct):** The word is reversed and each letter is shifted +1: TIME reversed is EMIT -> F, N, J, U. WORK reversed is KROW -> K+1=L, R+1=S, O+1=P, W+1=X, giving LSPX.
- **A — XPSM:** Cluster XPSM applies +1 in forward order (W->X, O->P, R->S, K->L/M) and reverses/mis-shifts.
- **B — LRPX:** Cluster LRPX fails to shift R by +1 (leaves R instead of S).
- **C — LSPW:** Cluster LSPW leaves W unshifted instead of +1 to X.
- **Exam Trick:** Reversing before shifting (+1) is a classic CGL Tier-1/Tier-2 trick—always check first-to-last correspondence.

### Q132 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
STAR : BSTU :: PLAN : ?

A. BOQM
B. QMBP
C. ANPL
D. BOPM

**Answer:** A. BOQM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — BOQM (Correct):** The 4-letter cluster swaps its two halves (ABCD -> CDAB) and shifts each letter +1: STAR -> ARST -> +1 gives BSTU. For PLAN -> ANPL -> +1 gives BOQM.
- **B — QMBP:** Cluster QMBP shifts PLAN +1 first (QMBO) and misplaces the swap.
- **C — ANPL:** Cluster ANPL swaps the halves of PLAN without applying the +1 shift.
- **D — BOPM:** Cluster BOPM shifts P by 0 instead of +1 to Q.
- **Exam Trick:** When letters appear scrambled with small shifts, test half-swaps (CDAB) or pair-swaps (BADC).

### Q133 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
MIND : KOFP :: TAPE : ?

A. VCGR
B. CVGR
C. CVRG
D. CWGR

**Answer:** B. CVGR
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — CVGR (Correct):** Adjacent pairs are swapped (ABCD -> BADC) and each letter shifts +2: MIND -> IMDN -> +2 gives KOFP. For TAPE -> ATPE -> A+2=C, T+2=V, E+2=G, P+2=R -> CVGR.
- **A — VCGR:** Cluster VCGR shifts T+2=V and A+2=C without swapping the first pair.
- **C — CVRG:** Cluster CVRG forgets to swap the second pair (P and E) before shifting +2.
- **D — CWGR:** Cluster CWGR shifts T by +3 to W instead of +2 to V.
- **Exam Trick:** Pair-swapping (1-2, 3-4) combined with a constant +2 shift is easily spotted by checking diagonal letter pairs.

### Q134 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
ACE : FHJ :: BDF : ?

A. GIJ
B. HIK
C. GIK
D. GIL

**Answer:** C. GIK
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — GIK (Correct):** Each letter shifts +5 forward: A(1)->F(6), C(3)->H(8), E(5)->J(10). For BDF: B(2)+5=G(7), D(4)+5=I(9), F(6)+5=K(11), giving GIK.
- **A — GIJ:** Cluster GIJ shifts F by +4 to J instead of +5 to K.
- **B — HIK:** Cluster HIK shifts B by +6 to H instead of +5 to G.
- **D — GIL:** Cluster GIL shifts F by +6 to L instead of +5 to K.
- **Exam Trick:** In 3-letter clusters with +2 internal spacing (B-D-F), a constant shift preserves the +2 internal spacing (G-I-K).

### Q135 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
CAT : DEC :: DOG : ?

A. ESQ
B. ERP
C. FSP
D. ESP

**Answer:** D. ESP
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — ESP (Correct):** The shifts are consecutive squares (+1, +4, +9): C+1=D, A+4=E, T+9=C (20+9=29 -> 3=C). For DOG: D(4)+1=E(5), O(15)+4=S(19), G(7)+9=P(16), giving ESP.
- **A — ESQ:** Cluster ESQ shifts G by +10 to Q instead of +9 to P.
- **B — ERP:** Cluster ERP shifts O by +3 to R instead of +4 to S.
- **C — FSP:** Cluster FSP shifts D by +2 to F instead of +1 to E.
- **Exam Trick:** When shifts look like +1, +4, +9 (or +1, +4, +9, +16), recognize the n^2 shift progression.

### Q136 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
BRAIN : DTCKP :: HEART : ?

A. JGCTV
B. JGCUV
C. JFCTV
D. KGCTV

**Answer:** A. JGCTV
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — JGCTV (Correct):** Each letter shifts +2 forward: B->D, R->T, A->C, I->K, N->P. Applying +2 to HEART: H->J, E->G, A->C, R->T, T->V, giving JGCTV.
- **B — JGCUV:** Cluster JGCUV shifts R by +3 to U instead of +2 to T.
- **C — JFCTV:** Cluster JFCTV shifts E by +1 to F instead of +2 to G.
- **D — KGCTV:** Cluster KGCTV shifts H by +3 to K instead of +2 to J.
- **Exam Trick:** For 5-letter constant shifts, verify the middle and last letters first to eliminate distractors fast.

### Q137 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
PLANT : KOAMG :: TRAIN : ?

A. GIARN
B. GIARM
C. GJARM
D. HIARM

**Answer:** B. GIARM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — GIARM (Correct):** The middle letter stays unchanged (A->A), while all other letters are replaced by their opposite A-Z pairs: P-K, L-O, A-A, N-M, T-G. For TRAIN: T-G, R-I, A-A, I-R, N-M, giving GIARM.
- **A — GIARN:** Cluster GIARN leaves N unchanged instead of pairing it with its opposite M.
- **C — GJARM:** Cluster GJARM pairs R(18) with J(10), summing to 28 instead of 27 (I is 9).
- **D — HIARM:** Cluster HIARM pairs T(20) with H(8), summing to 28 instead of 27 (G is 7).
- **Exam Trick:** In 5-letter analogies where the middle letter is identical in both terms, check opposite pairs for the outer four letters.

### Q138 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
SMART : SQZLR :: POWER : ?

A. QDWNO
B. QDVNP
C. QDVNO
D. RDVNO

**Answer:** C. QDVNO
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — QDVNO (Correct):** The word is reversed and each letter shifts -1: SMART reversed is TRAMS -> T-1=S, R-1=Q, A-1=Z, M-1=L, S-1=R. POWER reversed is REWOP -> R-1=Q, E-1=D, W-1=V, O-1=N, P-1=O, giving QDVNO.
- **A — QDWNO:** Cluster QDWNO leaves W unshifted instead of -1 to V.
- **B — QDVNP:** Cluster QDVNP shifts P by 0 (or +0) to P instead of -1 to O.
- **D — RDVNO:** Cluster RDVNO leaves R unshifted instead of -1 to Q.
- **Exam Trick:** Check the last letter of the word against the first letter of the code: T(20)->S(19) immediately signals reverse + (-1).

### Q139 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
APPLE : BNSHJ :: MANGO : ?

A. NYQCU
B. NYRCT
C. NZQCT
D. NYQCT

**Answer:** D. NYQCT
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — NYQCT (Correct):** The shifts alternate in sign and increase by 1 (+1, -2, +3, -4, +5): A+1=B, P-2=N, P+3=S, L-4=H, E+5=J. For MANGO: M+1=N, A-2=Y, N+3=Q, G-4=C, O+5=T, giving NYQCT.
- **A — NYQCU:** Cluster NYQCU shifts O by +6 to U instead of +5 to T.
- **B — NYRCT:** Cluster NYRCT shifts N by +4 to R instead of +3 to Q.
- **C — NZQCT:** Cluster NZQCT shifts A by -1 to Z instead of -2 to Y.
- **Exam Trick:** Alternating-sign progressive shifts (+1, -2, +3, -4, +5) frequently appear in 5-letter SSC CGL questions.

### Q140 · Letter-Cluster Analogy

Select the option that is related to the third letter-cluster in the same way as the second letter-cluster is related to the first letter-cluster.
MASTER : SAMRET :: GARDEN : ?

A. RAGNED
B. GARNED
C. RAGDEN
D. AGREND

**Answer:** A. RAGNED
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — RAGNED (Correct):** The 6-letter cluster is split into two 3-letter halves and each half is reversed: MAS->SAM and TER->RET, giving SAMRET. For GARDEN: GAR->RAG and DEN->NED, giving RAGNED.
- **B — GARNED:** Cluster GARNED reverses only the second half (DEN->NED) while leaving GAR unchanged.
- **C — RAGDEN:** Cluster RAGDEN reverses only the first half (GAR->RAG) while leaving DEN unchanged.
- **D — AGREND:** Cluster AGREND scrambles the letters without reversing each 3-letter half.
- **Exam Trick:** When the coded cluster contains the exact same letters as the original, check half-reversals or fixed-position swaps.

---

## Chapter 3: Number, Alphabet & Mixed Series

### Q141 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the following series? 11, 21, 31, 41, ?

A. 51
B. 49
C. 50
D. 52

**Answer:** A. 51
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 51 (Correct):** Each consecutive term increases by a constant difference of +10: 11 + 10 = 21, 21 + 10 = 31, 31 + 10 = 41, so 41 + 10 = 51.
- **B — 49:** Adding 8 to 41 gives 49, which breaks the uniform +10 progression.
- **C — 50:** Adding 9 to 41 gives 50, whereas every prior step adds 10.
- **D — 52:** Adding 11 to 41 gives 52, which exceeds the constant +10 difference.
- **Exam Trick:** In an arithmetic progression (constant difference), verify the difference between at least three consecutive pairs before predicting the next term.

### Q142 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Select the number that will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 19, 31, 43, 55, ?

A. 65
B. 67
C. 68
D. 69

**Answer:** B. 67
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 67 (Correct):** The series follows a constant difference of +12: 19 + 12 = 31, 31 + 12 = 43, 43 + 12 = 55, and 55 + 12 = 67.
- **A — 65:** Adding 10 to 55 gives 65, underestimating the constant +12 step.
- **C — 68:** Adding 13 to 55 gives 68, which does not match the +12 pattern.
- **D — 69:** Adding 14 to 55 gives 69, which is 2 more than the required +12 increment.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice the units digits here (9, 1, 3, 5, 7) advance by +2 each step because +12 ends in 2.

### Q143 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Find the missing number in the given series: 16, 24, 32, 40, ?

A. 44
B. 46
C. 48
D. 52

**Answer:** C. 48
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 48 (Correct):** Each term increases by +8 (consecutive multiples of 8 starting from 16): 40 + 8 = 48.
- **A — 44:** Adding 4 to 40 gives 44, which is half the required +8 step.
- **B — 46:** Adding 6 to 40 gives 46, which is not a multiple of 8.
- **D — 52:** Adding 12 to 40 gives 52, which skips the next multiple of 8.
- **Exam Trick:** Recognizing multiples of common numbers (8, 9, 11, 12) lets you solve constant-difference series in under 5 seconds.

### Q144 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Which number completes the series? 7, 16, 25, 34, ?

A. 41
B. 45
C. 47
D. 43

**Answer:** D. 43
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 43 (Correct):** The difference between consecutive terms is constantly +9: 7 + 9 = 16, 16 + 9 = 25, 25 + 9 = 34, so 34 + 9 = 43.
- **A — 41:** Adding 7 to 34 gives 41, failing the constant +9 rule.
- **B — 45:** Adding 11 to 34 gives 45, which overshoots the +9 step by 2.
- **C — 47:** Adding 13 to 34 gives 47, which does not match the +9 arithmetic progression.
- **Exam Trick:** For any +9 series, the tens digit increases by 1 while the units digit decreases by 1 (7 -> 16 -> 25 -> 34 -> 43).

### Q145 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Find the next number in the series: 15, 26, 37, 48, ?

A. 59
B. 57
C. 60
D. 61

**Answer:** A. 59
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 59 (Correct):** Each term increases by +11: 15 + 11 = 26, 26 + 11 = 37, 37 + 11 = 48, and 48 + 11 = 59.
- **B — 57:** Adding 9 to 48 gives 57, which breaks the +11 pattern.
- **C — 60:** Adding 12 to 48 gives 60, which is 1 more than the +11 increment.
- **D — 61:** Adding 13 to 48 gives 61, which does not preserve the constant difference.
- **Exam Trick:** In a +11 series, both the tens and units digits increase by 1 at each step (unless a carry occurs).

### Q146 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Select the number that completes the following series: 12, 19, 26, 33, ?

A. 38
B. 40
C. 39
D. 42

**Answer:** B. 40
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40 (Correct):** The series increases by +7 at every step: 12 + 7 = 19, 19 + 7 = 26, 26 + 7 = 33, and 33 + 7 = 40.
- **A — 38:** Adding 5 to 33 gives 38, which is 2 short of the +7 difference.
- **C — 39:** Adding 6 to 33 gives 39, which breaks the uniform +7 rule.
- **D — 42:** Adding 9 to 33 gives 42, which exceeds the +7 increment.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check all given differences (19-12=7, 26-19=7, 33-26=7) to confirm the difference is constant rather than growing.

### Q147 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 6, 18, 30, 42, ?

A. 50
B. 52
C. 54
D. 56

**Answer:** C. 54
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 54 (Correct):** Every consecutive term increases by +12: 6 + 12 = 18, 18 + 12 = 30, 30 + 12 = 42, so 42 + 12 = 54.
- **A — 50:** Adding 8 to 42 gives 50, which does not match the +12 step.
- **B — 52:** Adding 10 to 42 gives 52, falling short of the +12 increment.
- **D — 56:** Adding 14 to 42 gives 56, overshooting the constant +12 difference.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice all terms are odd multiples of 6 (6×1, 6×3, 6×5, 6×7, 6×9 = 54).

### Q148 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Find the missing term in the series: 20, 31, 42, 53, ?

A. 62
B. 63
C. 65
D. 64

**Answer:** D. 64
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 64 (Correct):** The constant difference between terms is +11: 20 + 11 = 31, 31 + 11 = 42, 42 + 11 = 53, and 53 + 11 = 64.
- **A — 62:** Adding 9 to 53 gives 62, which breaks the +11 progression.
- **B — 63:** Adding 10 to 53 gives 63, failing the +11 pattern.
- **C — 65:** Adding 12 to 53 gives 65, exceeding the constant +11 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Check the last digits: 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 — a +11 step always shifts the units digit by +1.

### Q149 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Which number comes next in the series: 35, 41, 47, 53, ?

A. 59
B. 57
C. 61
D. 63

**Answer:** A. 59
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 59 (Correct):** Each term increases by +6: 35 + 6 = 41, 41 + 6 = 47, 47 + 6 = 53, and 53 + 6 = 59.
- **B — 57:** Adding 4 to 53 gives 57, which undercuts the +6 difference.
- **C — 61:** Adding 8 to 53 gives 61, which assumes an increasing step rather than constant +6.
- **D — 63:** Adding 10 to 53 gives 63, which does not follow the +6 rule.
- **Exam Trick:** All terms in this series are odd numbers separated by 6, so the next term must also be odd.

### Q150 · Number Series - Constant Difference

Find the next number in the series: 30, 38, 46, 54, ?

A. 60
B. 62
C. 64
D. 66

**Answer:** B. 62
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 62 (Correct):** The series progresses by adding +8 each time: 30 + 8 = 38, 38 + 8 = 46, 46 + 8 = 54, and 54 + 8 = 62.
- **A — 60:** Adding 6 to 54 gives 60, which is 2 less than the +8 step.
- **C — 64:** Adding 10 to 54 gives 64, which assumes an increasing difference (+8, +8, +8, +10).
- **D — 66:** Adding 12 to 54 gives 66, breaking the constant +8 pattern.
- **Exam Trick:** When differences are small and uniform, write them explicitly above the gaps to avoid careless addition errors.

### Q151 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 9, 13, 19, ?

A. 25
B. 26
C. 27
D. 29

**Answer:** C. 27
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 27 (Correct):** The differences increase by +2 at each step: 9 + 4 = 13, 13 + 6 = 19, so the next difference is +8: 19 + 8 = 27.
- **A — 25:** Adding 6 again to 19 gives 25, ignoring the +2 growth in differences.
- **B — 26:** Adding 7 to 19 gives 26, which uses an odd increment instead of +8.
- **D — 29:** Adding 10 to 19 gives 29, which skips +8 and jumps straight to +10.
- **Exam Trick:** When first differences grow steadily (4, 6, 8), the second-order difference is constant (+2).

### Q152 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Find the next number in the series: 9, 15, 23, ?

A. 31
B. 32
C. 35
D. 33

**Answer:** D. 33
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 33 (Correct):** The differences are consecutive even numbers (+6, +8, +10): 9 + 6 = 15, 15 + 8 = 23, and 23 + 10 = 33.
- **A — 31:** Adding 8 again gives 31, failing to increase the difference by +2.
- **B — 32:** Adding 9 gives 32, which breaks the even-number difference sequence.
- **C — 35:** Adding 12 gives 35, skipping the +10 step.
- **Exam Trick:** In 3-term increasing-difference series, check whether the differences are +d, +(d+2), +(d+4).

### Q153 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Select the missing number in the series: 2, 9, 18, ?

A. 29
B. 27
C. 28
D. 30

**Answer:** A. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 29 (Correct):** The differences increase by +2 each time (+7, +9, +11): 2 + 7 = 9, 9 + 9 = 18, and 18 + 11 = 29.
- **B — 27:** Adding 9 again gives 27, which keeps the difference constant instead of increasing it.
- **C — 28:** Adding 10 gives 28, which increases the difference by +1 rather than +2.
- **D — 30:** Adding 12 gives 30, overshooting the required +11 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Even when differences are odd numbers (7, 9, 11), their step-to-step growth is still +2.

### Q154 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number completes the series? 3, 12, 23, ?

A. 34
B. 36
C. 35
D. 38

**Answer:** B. 36
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 36 (Correct):** The differences are +9, +11, +13: 3 + 9 = 12, 12 + 11 = 23, and 23 + 13 = 36.
- **A — 34:** Adding 11 again gives 34, ignoring the +2 growth in the difference.
- **C — 35:** Adding 12 gives 35, which uses a +1 increment instead of +2.
- **D — 38:** Adding 15 gives 38, skipping +13.
- **Exam Trick:** Alternatively, notice 1×3=3, 2×6=12, 3×7.66 fails, so difference-of-differences (+2) is the cleanest rule.

### Q155 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Find the missing number in the series: 4, 7, 12, ?

A. 17
B. 18
C. 19
D. 21

**Answer:** C. 19
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 19 (Correct):** The differences are consecutive odd numbers (+3, +5, +7): 4 + 3 = 7, 7 + 5 = 12, and 12 + 7 = 19.
- **A — 17:** Adding 5 again gives 17, failing to advance the difference to +7.
- **B — 18:** Adding 6 gives 18, which does not follow the odd-number difference pattern.
- **D — 21:** Adding 9 gives 21, skipping the +7 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Also note the closed-form pattern: n² + 3 for n = 1, 2, 3, 4 gives 4, 7, 12, 19!

### Q156 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 5, 11, 19, ?

A. 27
B. 28
C. 31
D. 29

**Answer:** D. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 29 (Correct):** The differences increase by +2 each step (+6, +8, +10): 5 + 6 = 11, 11 + 8 = 19, so 19 + 10 = 29.
- **A — 27:** Adding 8 again gives 27, which keeps the difference static.
- **B — 28:** Adding 9 gives 28, which breaks the even-difference sequence.
- **C — 31:** Adding 12 gives 31, skipping +10.
- **Exam Trick:** This series also equals n² + 3n + 1 for n = 1, 2, 3, 4 (or simply differences +6, +8, +10).

### Q157 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Find the next number in the series: 6, 15, 26, ?

A. 39
B. 37
C. 38
D. 41

**Answer:** A. 39
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 39 (Correct):** The differences are +9, +11, +13: 6 + 9 = 15, 15 + 11 = 26, and 26 + 13 = 39.
- **B — 37:** Adding 11 again gives 37, missing the +2 step growth.
- **C — 38:** Adding 12 gives 38, which breaks the odd-number difference pattern.
- **D — 41:** Adding 15 gives 41, skipping the +13 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Both differences (9, 11, 13) and second differences (+2, +2) confirm 39 immediately.

### Q158 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Select the number that will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 7, 16, 27, ?

A. 38
B. 40
C. 39
D. 42

**Answer:** B. 40
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 40 (Correct):** The differences follow +9, +11, +13: 7 + 9 = 16, 16 + 11 = 27, and 27 + 13 = 40.
- **A — 38:** Adding 11 again gives 38, failing to increase the difference.
- **C — 39:** Adding 12 gives 39, which breaks the +2 second-difference rule.
- **D — 42:** Adding 15 gives 42, overshooting +13.
- **Exam Trick:** Compare with 6, 15, 26, 39: every term here is simply +1 greater, so 39 + 1 = 40.

### Q159 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number completes the series? 8, 13, 20, ?

A. 27
B. 28
C. 29
D. 31

**Answer:** C. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 29 (Correct):** The differences are +5, +7, +9: 8 + 5 = 13, 13 + 7 = 20, and 20 + 9 = 29.
- **A — 27:** Adding 7 again gives 27, ignoring the +2 growth in differences.
- **B — 28:** Adding 8 gives 28, which breaks the odd-difference sequence.
- **D — 31:** Adding 11 gives 31, skipping +9.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice 8 = 2²+4, 13 = 3²+4, 20 = 4²+4, and 29 = 5²+4 — squares plus 4!

### Q160 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Find the next term in the series: 26, 30, 36, 44, ?

A. 52
B. 56
C. 58
D. 54

**Answer:** D. 54
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 54 (Correct):** The differences increase by +2 at each step (+4, +6, +8, +10): 26 + 4 = 30, 30 + 6 = 36, 36 + 8 = 44, and 44 + 10 = 54.
- **A — 52:** Adding 8 again gives 52, failing to increment the difference to +10.
- **B — 56:** Adding 12 gives 56, skipping +10.
- **C — 58:** Adding 14 gives 58, which is 4 more than the required +10 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Four given terms give three differences (4, 6, 8), which firmly locks in +10 as the next step.

### Q161 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 16, 22, 30, 40, ?

A. 52
B. 48
C. 50
D. 54

**Answer:** A. 52
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 52 (Correct):** The differences are +6, +8, +10, +12: 16 + 6 = 22, 22 + 8 = 30, 30 + 10 = 40, so 40 + 12 = 52.
- **B — 48:** Adding 8 gives 48, which reverses the growing difference.
- **C — 50:** Adding 10 again gives 50, ignoring the +2 growth from 10 to 12.
- **D — 54:** Adding 14 gives 54, skipping +12.
- **Exam Trick:** Even numbers added to an even starting term always keep every term in the series even.

### Q162 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Find the next term in the series: 11, 14, 19, 26, ?

A. 33
B. 35
C. 37
D. 39

**Answer:** B. 35
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 35 (Correct):** The differences are consecutive odd numbers (+3, +5, +7, +9): 11 + 3 = 14, 14 + 5 = 19, 19 + 7 = 26, and 26 + 9 = 35.
- **A — 33:** Adding 7 again gives 33, failing to advance the difference to +9.
- **C — 37:** Adding 11 gives 37, skipping +9.
- **D — 39:** Adding 13 gives 39, which jumps two odd steps ahead.
- **Exam Trick:** Also note: 1²+10=11, 2²+10=14, 3²+10=19, 4²+10=26, 5²+10=35.

### Q163 · Number Series - Decreasing Difference

Select the number that will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 100, 98, 94, 88, 80, ?

A. 72
B. 68
C. 70
D. 74

**Answer:** C. 70
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 70 (Correct):** The series decreases by consecutive even numbers (-2, -4, -6, -8, -10): 80 - 10 = 70.
- **A — 72:** Subtracting 8 again gives 72, failing to increase the subtraction step to 10.
- **B — 68:** Subtracting 12 gives 68, skipping -10.
- **D — 74:** Subtracting 6 gives 74, reversing the growing subtraction pattern.
- **Exam Trick:** Decreasing series with growing negative differences (-2, -4, -6, -8, -10) are common in SSC CGL Tier-1.

### Q164 · Number Series - Increasing Difference

Which number completes the series? 12, 14, 18, 26, 42, ?

A. 58
B. 66
C. 82
D. 74

**Answer:** D. 74
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 74 (Correct):** The differences double at each step (+2, +4, +8, +16, +32): 42 + 32 = 74.
- **A — 58:** Adding 16 again gives 58, ignoring the doubling of differences.
- **B — 66:** Adding 24 gives 66, assuming linear +8 growth in differences instead of doubling.
- **C — 82:** Adding 40 gives 82, which does not follow powers of 2.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check whether differences grow by addition (+2) or multiplication (×2) when the jump accelerates rapidly.

### Q165 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Find the next number in the series: 4, 9, 16, 25, ?

A. 36
B. 32
C. 35
D. 49

**Answer:** A. 36
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 36 (Correct):** The terms are squares of consecutive natural numbers: 2² = 4, 3² = 9, 4² = 16, 5² = 25, so 6² = 36.
- **B — 32:** 32 is a power of 2 (2⁵), not a square number.
- **C — 35:** 35 is 6² - 1, whereas the series uses exact squares.
- **D — 49:** 49 is 7², which skips 6² = 36.
- **Exam Trick:** Memorizing squares up to 30² and cubes up to 15³ saves crucial seconds in SSC reasoning.

### Q166 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 25, 36, 49, 64, ?

A. 72
B. 81
C. 79
D. 100

**Answer:** B. 81
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 81 (Correct):** The series consists of consecutive squares starting from 5²: 5² = 25, 6² = 36, 7² = 49, 8² = 64, so 9² = 81.
- **A — 72:** 72 is not a perfect square (it is 8 × 9).
- **C — 79:** 79 is 2 less than 81 and is a prime number, not a square.
- **D — 100:** 100 is 10², which skips 9² = 81.
- **Exam Trick:** You can also cross-check using differences: +11, +13, +15, +17 -> 64 + 17 = 81.

### Q167 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Find the next term in the series: 64, 81, 100, 121, ?

A. 132
B. 156
C. 144
D. 169

**Answer:** C. 144
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 144 (Correct):** These are consecutive squares starting from 8²: 8² = 64, 9² = 81, 10² = 100, 11² = 121, so 12² = 144.
- **A — 132:** 132 is 11 × 12, not a perfect square.
- **B — 156:** 156 is 12 × 13, not a perfect square.
- **D — 169:** 169 is 13², which skips 12² = 144.
- **Exam Trick:** The difference between any two consecutive squares n² and (n+1)² is always 2n + 1 (here +17, +19, +21, +23).

### Q168 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Select the number that completes the series: 100, 121, 144, 169, ?

A. 184
B. 192
C. 225
D. 196

**Answer:** D. 196
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 196 (Correct):** The terms are squares of consecutive integers from 10 to 14: 10² = 100, 11² = 121, 12² = 144, 13² = 169, so 14² = 196.
- **A — 184:** 184 is not a perfect square.
- **B — 192:** 192 is 3 × 64, not a square.
- **C — 225:** 225 is 15², skipping 14² = 196.
- **Exam Trick:** Don't confuse 14² = 196 with 13² = 169; their last two digits are reversed (69 vs 96).

### Q169 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Find the next number in the series: 2, 5, 10, 17, ?

A. 26
B. 24
C. 25
D. 28

**Answer:** A. 26
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 26 (Correct):** Each term follows n² + 1 for n = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5: 1²+1 = 2, 2²+1 = 5, 3²+1 = 10, 4²+1 = 17, so 5²+1 = 26.
- **B — 24:** 24 is 5² - 1 instead of 5² + 1.
- **C — 25:** 25 is 5², forgetting the +1 offset.
- **D — 28:** 28 is 5² + 3, breaking the +1 offset rule.
- **Exam Trick:** n² + 1 and n² - 1 are two of the most frequently tested patterns in SSC CHSL and CGL.

### Q170 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 0, 3, 8, 15, 24, ?

A. 33
B. 35
C. 36
D. 37

**Answer:** B. 35
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 35 (Correct):** Each term follows n² - 1 for n = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6: 1²-1=0, 2²-1=3, 3²-1=8, 4²-1=15, 5²-1=24, so 6²-1 = 35.
- **A — 33:** 33 adds 9 instead of 11 to 24.
- **C — 36:** 36 is 6², forgetting to subtract 1.
- **D — 37:** 37 is 6² + 1 instead of 6² - 1.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that n² - 1 also equals (n - 1)(n + 1): 0×2, 1×3, 2×4, 3×5, 4×6, 5×7 = 35.

### Q171 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Find the missing number in the series: 2, 9, 28, 65, ?

A. 124
B. 125
C. 126
D. 128

**Answer:** C. 126
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 126 (Correct):** Each term follows n³ + 1 for n = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5: 1³+1=2, 2³+1=9, 3³+1=28, 4³+1=65, so 5³+1 = 126.
- **A — 124:** 124 is 5³ - 1 instead of 5³ + 1.
- **B — 125:** 125 is 5³, omitting the +1 offset.
- **D — 128:** 128 is 2⁷, which does not fit the cubic pattern.
- **Exam Trick:** Whenever you see numbers just 1 above 8, 27, 64, 125, 216, immediately test n³ + 1.

### Q172 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Select the number that completes the series: 0, 7, 26, 63, 124, ?

A. 214
B. 216
C. 217
D. 215

**Answer:** D. 215
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 215 (Correct):** Each term is n³ - 1 for n = 1 to 6: 1³-1=0, 2³-1=7, 3³-1=26, 4³-1=63, 5³-1=124, so 6³-1 = 216 - 1 = 215.
- **A — 214:** 214 is 6³ - 2, subtracting 2 instead of 1.
- **B — 216:** 216 is 6³, forgetting the -1 offset.
- **C — 217:** 217 is 6³ + 1 instead of 6³ - 1.
- **Exam Trick:** Memorize key cubes: 6³=216, 7³=343, 8³=512, 9³=729, 10³=1000.

### Q173 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 2, 6, 12, 20, 30, ?

A. 42
B. 40
C. 44
D. 36

**Answer:** A. 42
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 42 (Correct):** Each term follows n² + n = n(n + 1) for n = 1..6: 1×2=2, 2×3=6, 3×4=12, 4×5=20, 5×6=30, so 6×7 = 42.
- **B — 40:** 40 adds 10 again instead of 12 (differences are +4, +6, +8, +10, +12).
- **C — 44:** 44 adds 14 to 30, skipping the +12 step.
- **D — 36:** 36 is 6² instead of 6² + 6.
- **Exam Trick:** The pattern n² + n (or n(n+1)) is a staple PYQ; its differences are always consecutive even numbers.

### Q174 · Number Series - Squares & Cubes

Find the next term in the series: 2, 10, 30, 68, ?

A. 125
B. 130
C. 132
D. 135

**Answer:** B. 130
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 130 (Correct):** Each term follows n³ + n for n = 1..5: 1³+1=2, 2³+2=10, 3³+3=30, 4³+4=68, so 5³+5 = 125 + 5 = 130.
- **A — 125:** 125 is 5³ without adding n = 5.
- **C — 132:** 132 is 11 × 12, not 5³ + 5.
- **D — 135:** 135 is 5³ + 10, adding double the index.
- **Exam Trick:** For rapid growth like 2, 10, 30, 68, test n³ + n or n³ - n before taking triple differences.

### Q175 · Number Series - Multiplication

Find the next term in the series: 3, 9, 27, 81, ?

A. 162
B. 324
C. 243
D. 729

**Answer:** C. 243
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 243 (Correct):** Each term is multiplied by 3: 3×3=9, 9×3=27, 27×3=81, so 81×3 = 243.
- **A — 162:** 162 is 81 × 2 instead of 81 × 3.
- **B — 324:** 324 is 81 × 4, using the wrong multiplier.
- **D — 729:** 729 is 3⁶ (243 × 3), skipping 3⁵ = 243.
- **Exam Trick:** Powers of 3 up to 3⁶ (3, 9, 27, 81, 243, 729) appear frequently in both number series and analogy questions.

### Q176 · Number Series - Multiplication

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 8, 16, 32, 64, ?

A. 96
B. 120
C. 256
D. 128

**Answer:** D. 128
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 128 (Correct):** Each term is doubled (×2): 8×2=16, 16×2=32, 32×2=64, so 64×2 = 128.
- **A — 96:** 96 adds 32 to 64 instead of doubling 64.
- **B — 120:** 120 is not a power of 2.
- **C — 256:** 256 is 64 × 4, skipping 128.
- **Exam Trick:** Powers of 2 up to 2¹⁰ = 1024 are essential for quick mental checks.

### Q177 · Number Series - Multiplication

Find the next term in the series: 5, 20, 80, 320, ?

A. 1280
B. 640
C. 960
D. 1600

**Answer:** A. 1280
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 1280 (Correct):** Each term is multiplied by 4: 5×4=20, 20×4=80, 80×4=320, so 320×4 = 1280.
- **B — 640:** 640 is 320 × 2 instead of 320 × 4.
- **C — 960:** 960 is 320 × 3, using a ×3 multiplier.
- **D — 1600:** 1600 is 320 × 5, overshooting the ×4 ratio.
- **Exam Trick:** In geometric progressions, check the ratio of the first two terms (20/5 = 4) and verify it on the next pair.

### Q178 · Number Series - Multiplication

Select the number that completes the series: 2, 6, 18, 54, ?

A. 108
B. 162
C. 144
D. 216

**Answer:** B. 162
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 162 (Correct):** Each term is multiplied by 3: 2×3=6, 6×3=18, 18×3=54, so 54×3 = 162.
- **A — 108:** 108 is 54 × 2 instead of 54 × 3.
- **C — 144:** 144 is 12², not a multiple of 27.
- **D — 216:** 216 is 54 × 4, using the wrong factor.
- **Exam Trick:** Check the units digit: 54 × 3 ends in 2 (4 × 3 = 12), which immediately narrows down 162.

### Q179 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 3, 7, 15, 31, 63, ?

A. 125
B. 126
C. 127
D. 129

**Answer:** C. 127
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 127 (Correct):** Each term follows (previous × 2) + 1: 3×2+1=7, 7×2+1=15, 15×2+1=31, 31×2+1=63, so 63×2+1 = 127.
- **A — 125:** 125 is 63×2 - 1 instead of +1.
- **B — 126:** 126 is 63×2 without adding 1.
- **D — 129:** 129 is 63×2 + 3, using the wrong offset.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice every term is also 2ⁿ - 1 for n = 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 (3, 7, 15, 31, 63, 127).

### Q180 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Find the missing number in the series: 4, 7, 13, 25, 49, ?

A. 95
B. 98
C. 99
D. 97

**Answer:** D. 97
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 97 (Correct):** Each term follows (previous × 2) - 1: 4×2-1=7, 7×2-1=13, 13×2-1=25, 25×2-1=49, so 49×2-1 = 97.
- **A — 95:** 95 is 49×2 - 3, subtracting too much.
- **B — 98:** 98 is 49×2 without subtracting 1.
- **C — 99:** 99 is 49×2 + 1 instead of -1.
- **Exam Trick:** The differences (+3, +6, +12, +24, +48) also double at every step, confirming 49 + 48 = 97.

### Q181 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Select the number that completes the series: 2, 5, 11, 23, 47, ?

A. 95
B. 93
C. 94
D. 96

**Answer:** A. 95
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 95 (Correct):** Each term follows (previous × 2) + 1: 2×2+1=5, 5×2+1=11, 11×2+1=23, 23×2+1=47, so 47×2+1 = 95.
- **B — 93:** 93 is 47×2 - 1 instead of +1.
- **C — 94:** 94 is 47×2 without adding 1.
- **D — 96:** 96 is 47×2 + 2, which breaks the +1 rule.
- **Exam Trick:** You can also verify via doubling differences: +3, +6, +12, +24, +48 -> 47 + 48 = 95.

### Q182 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 5, 11, 24, 51, 106, ?

A. 212
B. 217
C. 215
D. 221

**Answer:** B. 217
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 217 (Correct):** The rule is ×2 + 1, ×2 + 2, ×2 + 3, ×2 + 4, ×2 + 5: 5×2+1=11, 11×2+2=24, 24×2+3=51, 51×2+4=106, so 106×2+5 = 217.
- **A — 212:** 212 is 106×2 with no added term.
- **C — 215:** 215 is 106×2 + 3, failing to increment the added constant.
- **D — 221:** 221 is 106×2 + 9, adding too large an offset.
- **Exam Trick:** When ×2 ± c has a changing offset, check if c increases by +1 at each step.

### Q183 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Find the next number in the series: 1, 2, 6, 24, 120, ?

A. 600
B. 480
C. 720
D. 840

**Answer:** C. 720
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 720 (Correct):** Each term is multiplied by consecutive integers (×2, ×3, ×4, ×5, ×6): 120 × 6 = 720.
- **A — 600:** 600 is 120 × 5, repeating the previous multiplier.
- **B — 480:** 480 is 120 × 4, stepping backward.
- **D — 840:** 840 is 120 × 7, skipping ×6.
- **Exam Trick:** These are factorials (1!, 2!, 3!, 4!, 5!, 6! = 720) — a classic SSC favorite.

### Q184 · Number Series - Mixed Multiplication

Which number completes the series? 12, 6, 6, 9, 18, ?

A. 36
B. 54
C. 27
D. 45

**Answer:** D. 45
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 45 (Correct):** The multipliers increase by +0.5 at each step (×0.5, ×1, ×1.5, ×2, ×2.5): 18 × 2.5 = 45.
- **A — 36:** 36 is 18 × 2, repeating the ×2 multiplier instead of ×2.5.
- **B — 54:** 54 is 18 × 3, jumping to ×3 and skipping ×2.5.
- **C — 27:** 27 is 18 × 1.5, using an earlier multiplier.
- **Exam Trick:** Whenever a series dips once at the start (12 -> 6 -> 6 -> 9), immediately suspect ×0.5, ×1, ×1.5, ×2...

### Q185 · Number Series - Prime Series

Find the next number in the series: 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, ?

A. 29
B. 25
C. 27
D. 31

**Answer:** A. 29
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 29 (Correct):** The series lists consecutive prime numbers starting from 11: 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, so the next prime is 29.
- **B — 25:** 25 is composite (5 × 5), not a prime number.
- **C — 27:** 27 is composite (3 × 9), not a prime number.
- **D — 31:** 31 is the prime after 29, skipping 29.
- **Exam Trick:** Always test prime numbers when differences fluctuate irregularly like +2, +4, +2, +4.

### Q186 · Number Series - Prime Series

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 10, 12, 15, 20, 27, 38, ?

A. 49
B. 51
C. 53
D. 55

**Answer:** B. 51
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 51 (Correct):** The differences are consecutive prime numbers (+2, +3, +5, +7, +11, +13): 38 + 13 = 51.
- **A — 49:** 49 adds 11 again instead of the next prime 13.
- **C — 53:** 53 adds 15, which is not a prime number.
- **D — 55:** 55 adds 17, skipping the prime 13.
- **Exam Trick:** If differences start +2, +3, +5, +7, check whether the next difference is +11 (primes) or +9 (odd numbers).

### Q187 · Number Series - Prime Series

Select the number that completes the series: 4, 9, 25, 49, 121, ?

A. 144
B. 196
C. 169
D. 225

**Answer:** C. 169
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 169 (Correct):** The terms are squares of consecutive prime numbers: 2²=4, 3²=9, 5²=25, 7²=49, 11²=121, so 13² = 169.
- **A — 144:** 144 is 12², and 12 is not prime.
- **B — 196:** 196 is 14², skipping 13² and using a composite base.
- **D — 225:** 225 is 15², and 15 is composite.
- **Exam Trick:** Squares of primes (4, 9, 25, 49, 121, 169, 289, 361) are a classic trap when 9² = 81 is missing.

### Q188 · Number Series - Special Series

Find the missing number in the series: 3, 5, 8, 13, 21, ?

A. 31
B. 33
C. 35
D. 34

**Answer:** D. 34
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 34 (Correct):** Each term from the third onward is the sum of the two preceding terms (Fibonacci-style): 3+5=8, 5+8=13, 8+13=21, so 13+21 = 34.
- **A — 31:** 31 adds 10 to 21, assuming +2, +3, +5, +8, +10.
- **B — 33:** 33 is 1 less than 13 + 21.
- **C — 35:** 35 is 1 more than 13 + 21.
- **Exam Trick:** In a Fibonacci-like series, the differences replicate the series itself (+2, +3, +5, +8, +13).

### Q189 · Number Series - Special Series

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 14, 18, 26, 38, 62, ?

A. 74
B. 86
C. 92
D. 98

**Answer:** A. 74
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 74 (Correct):** Each term adds the product of its own digits to itself: 14+(1×4)=18, 18+(1×8)=26, 26+(2×6)=38, 38+(3×8)=62, so 62+(6×2) = 74.
- **B — 86:** 86 adds 24 (repeating +24 from 38 to 62) instead of 6×2 = 12.
- **C — 92:** 92 adds 30, which does not match any digit-based rule.
- **D — 98:** 98 adds 36, misreading 6×2 as 6².
- **Exam Trick:** When differences jump unpredictably (4, 8, 12, 24), test digit-sum or digit-product additions.

### Q190 · Number Series - Alternating

Find the next term in the series: 6, 7, 12, 14, 24, ?

A. 32
B. 28
C. 36
D. 48

**Answer:** B. 28
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 28 (Correct):** This is an alternating series of two ×2 progressions: odd positions (6, 12, 24) and even positions (7, 14, 28).
- **A — 32:** 32 does not belong to the 7, 14, 28 doubling sequence.
- **C — 36:** 36 multiplies 12 by 3 instead of continuing the even-position ×2 pattern.
- **D — 48:** 48 is the 7th term (24 × 2), not the 6th term.
- **Exam Trick:** Whenever numbers rise and dip or pair up cleanly (6,7 | 12,14 | 24,28), split odd and even positions.

### Q191 · Number Series - Alternating

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 5, 6, 10, 12, 20, ?

A. 22
B. 28
C. 24
D. 40

**Answer:** C. 24
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 24 (Correct):** Two alternating ×2 series run together: odd positions (5, 10, 20) and even positions (6, 12, 24).
- **A — 22:** 22 adds 2 to 20 instead of doubling 12.
- **B — 28:** 28 adds 16 to 12, breaking the ×2 rule.
- **D — 40:** 40 is the 7th term (20 × 2), not the 6th term.
- **Exam Trick:** In paired series (5,6), (10,12), (20,24), each pair is also double the previous pair.

### Q192 · Number Series - Alternating

Find the next term in the series: 4, 5, 8, 10, 16, ?

A. 18
B. 24
C. 32
D. 20

**Answer:** D. 20
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 20 (Correct):** Odd-positioned terms double (4, 8, 16) and even-positioned terms double (5, 10, 20).
- **A — 18:** 18 adds 2 to 16 instead of doubling 10.
- **B — 24:** 24 does not follow the 5, 10, 20 geometric progression.
- **C — 32:** 32 is the 7th term (16 × 2), not the 6th term.
- **Exam Trick:** Always verify which position the question mark occupies (here, 6th = even position) before applying the sub-series.

### Q193 · Number Series - Alternating

Select the number that completes the series: 15, 22, 17, 24, 19, ?

A. 26
B. 21
C. 25
D. 28

**Answer:** A. 26
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 26 (Correct):** Odd positions increase by +2 (15, 17, 19) and even positions increase by +2 (22, 24, 26).
- **B — 21:** 21 is the 7th term (19 + 2), not the 6th term.
- **C — 25:** 25 adds 1 instead of 2 to 24.
- **D — 28:** 28 adds 4 to 24, breaking the +2 step.
- **Exam Trick:** Alternating +7, -5 (+7, -5, +7, -5, +7 -> 19 + 7 = 26) also gives the exact same answer!

### Q194 · Number Series - Alternating

Which number will replace the question mark (?) in the series: 12, 25, 15, 22, 18, 19, ?

A. 16
B. 21
C. 20
D. 22

**Answer:** B. 21
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 21 (Correct):** Odd positions increase by +3 (12, 15, 18, 21) while even positions decrease by -3 (25, 22, 19).
- **A — 16:** 16 subtracts 3 from 19, which belongs to the even-position sub-series.
- **C — 20:** 20 adds 2 instead of 3 to 18.
- **D — 22:** 22 adds 4 to 18, breaking the +3 rule.
- **Exam Trick:** When one sub-series rises and the other falls, the overall series oscillates up and down.

### Q195 · Number Series - Wrong Term

Find the wrong number in the given series: 4, 9, 19, 39, 79, 160

A. 9
B. 19
C. 160
D. 79

**Answer:** C. 160
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 160 (Correct):** Each term follows (previous × 2) + 1: 4×2+1=9, 9×2+1=19, 19×2+1=39, 39×2+1=79, so the last term should be 79×2+1 = 159, not 160.
- **A — 9:** 9 correctly equals 4×2 + 1.
- **B — 19:** 19 correctly equals 9×2 + 1.
- **D — 79:** 79 correctly equals 39×2 + 1.
- **Exam Trick:** In wrong-term questions, always check the majority pattern across all pairs before blaming an early term.

### Q196 · Number Series - Wrong Term

Identify the incorrect term in the series: 7, 10, 15, 22, 31, 44

A. 10
B. 22
C. 31
D. 44

**Answer:** D. 44
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 44 (Correct):** The differences should be consecutive odd numbers (+3, +5, +7, +9, +11): 7+3=10, 10+5=15, 15+7=22, 22+9=31, so 31+11 should be 42, not 44.
- **A — 10:** 10 fits the +3 first difference (7 + 3 = 10).
- **B — 22:** 22 fits the +7 difference (15 + 7 = 22).
- **C — 31:** 31 fits the +9 difference (22 + 9 = 31).
- **Exam Trick:** Once you spot the wrong term at the end, verify that the earlier 4 transitions are 100% consistent.

### Q197 · Number Series - Wrong Term

Which number is wrong in the following series? 2, 6, 12, 20, 32, 42

A. 32
B. 6
C. 12
D. 20

**Answer:** A. 32
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 32 (Correct):** The differences should be +4, +6, +8, +10, +12 (or n² + n): 20 + 10 = 30, and 30 + 12 = 42. Thus 32 is wrong and should be 30.
- **B — 6:** 6 is 2 + 4 (or 2×3), which fits the pattern.
- **C — 12:** 12 is 6 + 6 (or 3×4), which fits the pattern.
- **D — 20:** 20 is 12 + 8 (or 4×5), which fits the pattern.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that a wrong term in the middle breaks TWO consecutive differences (+12 and +10 instead of +10 and +12).

### Q198 · Number Series - Wrong Term

Find the wrong term in the series: 8, 27, 64, 125, 218, 343

A. 27
B. 218
C. 64
D. 343

**Answer:** B. 218
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 218 (Correct):** The series consists of cubes of consecutive integers starting from 2³: 2³=8, 3³=27, 4³=64, 5³=125, 6³=216 (not 218), and 7³=343.
- **A — 27:** 27 is the exact cube of 3.
- **C — 64:** 64 is the exact cube of 4.
- **D — 343:** 343 is the exact cube of 7.
- **Exam Trick:** In SSC CGL wrong-term questions, a single digit off in a familiar cube (218 instead of 216) is a very common trap.

### Q199 · Letter Series - Single Letter

Which letter will replace the question mark (?) in the series: B, E, H, K, N, ?

A. P
B. R
C. Q
D. S

**Answer:** C. Q
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Q (Correct):** Using alphabet positions (B=2, E=5, H=8, K=11, N=14), each letter advances by +3: 14 + 3 = 17, which is Q.
- **A — P:** P (16) is only +2 from N (14).
- **B — R:** R (18) is +4 from N (14).
- **D — S:** S (19) is +5 from N (14).
- **Exam Trick:** Convert letters to numbers (EJOTY: E=5, J=10, O=15, T=20, Y=25) to turn any letter series into a simple number series.

### Q200 · Letter Series - Single Letter

Find the next letter in the series: Z, X, U, Q, L, ?

A. E
B. G
C. H
D. F

**Answer:** D. F
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — F (Correct):** Positions are Z=26, X=24, U=21, Q=17, L=12. The steps are -2, -3, -4, -5, so next is 12 - 6 = 6, which is F.
- **A — E:** E (5) subtracts 7 from L (12) instead of 6.
- **B — G:** G (7) subtracts 5 again instead of 6.
- **C — H:** H (8) subtracts 4 from L (12).
- **Exam Trick:** When letters move backward, write their numeric positions and subtract carefully.

### Q201 · Letter-Cluster Series - 2 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: AZ, BY, CX, DW, ?

A. EV
B. EU
C. FU
D. FV

**Answer:** A. EV
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — EV (Correct):** The first letter advances by +1 (A, B, C, D, E) and the second letter moves backward by -1 (Z, Y, X, W, V), giving EV.
- **B — EU:** EU pairs E with U instead of its opposite letter V.
- **C — FU:** FU advances the first letter by +2 instead of +1.
- **D — FV:** FV uses F instead of E for the first letter.
- **Exam Trick:** Opposite-letter pairs always sum to 27 (A=1 + Z=26 = 27, E=5 + V=22 = 27).

### Q202 · Letter-Cluster Series - 2 Letters

Select the letter cluster that completes the series: CE, FH, IK, LN, ?

A. OR
B. OQ
C. PQ
D. PR

**Answer:** B. OQ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — OQ (Correct):** Each letter shifts by +3: C(3)->F(6)->I(9)->L(12)->O(15), and E(5)->H(8)->K(11)->N(14)->Q(17), giving OQ.
- **A — OR:** OR shifts the second letter by +4 instead of +3.
- **C — PQ:** PQ shifts the first letter by +4 instead of +3.
- **D — PR:** PR shifts both letters by +4 instead of +3.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice each pair also has a constant internal gap of +2 (C+2=E, F+2=H, O+2=Q).

### Q203 · Letter-Cluster Series - 2 Letters

Find the missing letter cluster in the series: JP, LR, NT, PV, ?

A. RY
B. SX
C. RX
D. RW

**Answer:** C. RX
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — RX (Correct):** Both letters advance by +2: J(10)->L(12)->N(14)->P(16)->R(18) and P(16)->R(18)->T(20)->V(22)->X(24), giving RX.
- **A — RY:** RY advances the second letter by +3 instead of +2.
- **B — SX:** SX advances the first letter by +3 instead of +2.
- **D — RW:** RW advances the second letter by only +1.
- **Exam Trick:** Check the first letter across all clusters first; if it eliminates 2-3 options, you may only need one more check.

### Q204 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: ABC, EFG, IJK, MNO, ?

A. PQR
B. RST
C. OPQ
D. QRS

**Answer:** D. QRS
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — QRS (Correct):** The starting letter of each cluster advances by +4 (A=1, E=5, I=9, M=13, Q=17), followed by consecutive letters: QRS.
- **A — PQR:** PQR starts at P(16), which is only +3 from M(13).
- **B — RST:** RST starts at R(18), which is +5 from M(13).
- **C — OPQ:** OPQ starts at O(15), which is +2 from M(13).
- **Exam Trick:** For consecutive-letter blocks, track only the first letter of each cluster to find the answer in seconds.

### Q205 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Select the letter cluster that replaces the question mark (?): BDF, HJL, NPR, TVX, ?

A. ZBD
B. YAC
C. ZAC
D. ACE

**Answer:** A. ZBD
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — ZBD (Correct):** Each letter shifts by +6 across clusters (wrapping after Z=26): T(20)+6=26(Z), V(22)+6=28->2(B), X(24)+6=30->4(D), giving ZBD.
- **B — YAC:** YAC shifts by +5 instead of +6.
- **C — ZAC:** ZAC mixes +6 on the first letter with +5 on the others.
- **D — ACE:** ACE shifts by +7 instead of +6.
- **Exam Trick:** When wrapping past Z (26), subtract 26 from the sum: 28 - 26 = 2 (B).

### Q206 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Find the next letter cluster in the series: ZWT, WUQ, TRN, QOK, ?

A. MLH
B. NLH
C. NKH
D. OMH

**Answer:** B. NLH
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — NLH (Correct):** Every position decreases by -3: Q(17)-3=14(N), O(15)-3=12(L), K(11)-3=8(H), giving NLH.
- **A — MLH:** MLH subtracts 4 from Q(17) instead of 3.
- **C — NKH:** NKH subtracts 4 from O(15) instead of 3.
- **D — OMH:** OMH subtracts 2 from both Q and O.
- **Exam Trick:** All clusters here have internal gaps of -3 (Z->W->T, N->L->H); checking internal gaps is a great verification shortcut.

### Q207 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Which letter cluster completes the series? ACE, GIK, MOQ, SUW, ?

A. ZBD
B. XAC
C. YAC
D. YAD

**Answer:** C. YAC
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — YAC (Correct):** Each letter advances by +6 (with wrap-around): S(19)+6=25(Y), U(21)+6=27->1(A), W(23)+6=29->3(C), giving YAC.
- **A — ZBD:** ZBD shifts by +7 instead of +6.
- **B — XAC:** XAC shifts the first letter by +5 instead of +6.
- **D — YAD:** YAD shifts the third letter by +7 instead of +6.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice the last letter of one cluster + 2 equals the first letter of the next: E+2=G, K+2=M, Q+2=S, W+2=Y.

### Q208 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Select the missing letter cluster in the series: KMO, NQT, QUY, TYD, ?

A. WCH
B. VCI
C. XDI
D. WCI

**Answer:** D. WCI
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — WCI (Correct):** Position 1 shifts by +3 (K, N, Q, T, W), Position 2 by +4 (M, Q, U, Y, C), and Position 3 by +5 (O, T, Y, D, I), giving WCI.
- **A — WCH:** WCH shifts the third letter by +4 instead of +5.
- **B — VCI:** VCI shifts the first letter by +2 instead of +3.
- **C — XDI:** XDI shifts the first two letters by +4 and +5 instead of +3 and +4.
- **Exam Trick:** When shifts differ by position (+3, +4, +5), test the first letter to narrow options and then the last letter.

### Q209 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: DKY, FJW, HIU, JHS, ?

A. LGQ
B. LGP
C. KGQ
D. MGQ

**Answer:** A. LGQ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — LGQ (Correct):** Position 1 advances by +2 (D, F, H, J, L), Position 2 decreases by -1 (K, J, I, H, G), and Position 3 decreases by -2 (Y, W, U, S, Q), giving LGQ.
- **B — LGP:** LGP subtracts 3 from S instead of 2.
- **C — KGQ:** KGQ advances J by +1 instead of +2.
- **D — MGQ:** MGQ advances J by +3 instead of +2.
- **Exam Trick:** Mixed-direction letter clusters (+2, -1, -2) are a staple of SSC CHSL Tier-1.

### Q210 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Find the next letter cluster in the series: PQR, TUV, XYZ, BCD, ?

A. EFG
B. FGH
C. GHI
D. HIJ

**Answer:** B. FGH
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — FGH (Correct):** Each cluster consists of 3 consecutive letters, and the starting letter shifts by +4 (P=16, T=20, X=24, B=2, F=6), giving FGH.
- **A — EFG:** EFG starts at E(5), which is +3 from B(2) instead of +4.
- **C — GHI:** GHI starts at G(7), which is +5 from B(2).
- **D — HIJ:** HIJ starts at H(8), which is +6 from B(2).
- **Exam Trick:** Notice there is always a gap of 1 skipped letter between clusters (S, W, A, E).

### Q211 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Select the letter cluster that completes the series: AZB, CXD, EVF, GTH, ?

A. IQJ
B. ISJ
C. IRJ
D. HRJ

**Answer:** C. IRJ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — IRJ (Correct):** Position 1: +2 (A, C, E, G, I). Position 2: -2 (Z, X, V, T, R). Position 3: +2 (B, D, F, H, J). Together: IRJ.
- **A — IQJ:** IQJ subtracts 3 from T instead of 2.
- **B — ISJ:** ISJ subtracts 1 from T instead of 2.
- **D — HRJ:** HRJ advances G by +1 instead of +2.
- **Exam Trick:** Look at the first two letters: AZ, CX, EV, GT, IR — every pair is an opposite-letter pair (sum = 27)!

### Q212 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: MNO, KLM, IJK, GHI, ?

A. DEF
B. FGH
C. CDE
D. EFG

**Answer:** D. EFG
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — EFG (Correct):** Each cluster is 3 consecutive letters, and the starting letter steps backward by -2 (M=13, K=11, I=9, G=7, E=5), giving EFG.
- **A — DEF:** DEF steps backward by -3 from G(7) instead of -2.
- **B — FGH:** FGH steps backward by only -1 from G(7).
- **C — CDE:** CDE steps backward by -4 from G(7).
- **Exam Trick:** Even when clusters overlap (KLM shares L,M with MNO), tracking the first letter alone solves it cleanly.

### Q213 · Letter-Cluster Series - 3 Letters

Find the missing letter cluster in the series: BDF, CFI, DHL, EJO, ?

A. FLR
B. FKR
C. FLQ
D. GLR

**Answer:** A. FLR
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — FLR (Correct):** Position 1: +1 (B, C, D, E, F). Position 2: +2 (D, F, H, J, L). Position 3: +3 (F, I, L, O, R), giving FLR.
- **B — FKR:** FKR advances J by +1 instead of +2.
- **C — FLQ:** FLQ advances O by +2 instead of +3.
- **D — GLR:** GLR advances E by +2 instead of +1.
- **Exam Trick:** Here each cluster's internal gap also grows by +1: BDF (+2), CFI (+3), DHL (+4), EJO (+5), FLR (+6).

### Q214 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: BDFH, JLNP, RTVX, ZBDF, ?

A. IKMO
B. HJLN
C. HJLM
D. GJLN

**Answer:** B. HJLN
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — HJLN (Correct):** Each letter shifts by +8 across clusters (B=2->J=10->R=18->Z=26->H=8; D->L->T->B->J; F->N->V->D->L; H->P->X->F->N), giving HJLN.
- **A — IKMO:** IKMO shifts by +9 from ZBDF instead of +8.
- **C — HJLM:** HJLM shifts the fourth letter by +7 instead of +8.
- **D — GJLN:** GJLN shifts the first letter by +7 instead of +8.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice each cluster itself consists of 4 consecutive alternate letters (+2 internal gap): H, J, L, N.

### Q215 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Select the missing letter cluster in the series: AZBY, CXDW, EVFU, GTHS, ?

A. IQJR
B. IRJP
C. IRJQ
D. HSJQ

**Answer:** C. IRJQ
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — IRJQ (Correct):** Positions 1 and 3 advance by +2 (A,C,E,G,I and B,D,F,H,J) while positions 2 and 4 decrease by -2 (Z,X,V,T,R and Y,W,U,S,Q), giving IRJQ.
- **A — IQJR:** IQJR swaps the +2/-2 directions on the middle two letters.
- **B — IRJP:** IRJP subtracts 3 from S instead of 2.
- **D — HSJQ:** HSJQ advances G by +1 instead of +2.
- **Exam Trick:** Each 4-letter cluster is two consecutive opposite-letter pairs: IR (9+18=27) and JQ (10+17=27).

### Q216 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Find the next letter cluster in the series: PQRS, TUVW, XYZA, BCDE, ?

A. EFGH
B. GHIJ
C. FGHJ
D. FGHI

**Answer:** D. FGHI
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — FGHI (Correct):** The starting letter of each cluster advances by +4 (P=16, T=20, X=24, B=2, F=6), and each cluster consists of 4 consecutive letters: FGHI.
- **A — EFGH:** EFGH starts at E(5), which is +3 from B(2) instead of +4.
- **B — GHIJ:** GHIJ starts at G(7), which is +5 from B(2).
- **C — FGHJ:** FGHJ skips I between H and J.
- **Exam Trick:** Track only the first letter of each consecutive-letter block: P(16), T(20), X(24), B(2), F(6).

### Q217 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Which letter cluster completes the series? ZYXW, VUTS, RQPO, NMLK, ?

A. JIHG
B. KJIH
C. IHGF
D. JIHF

**Answer:** A. JIHG
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — JIHG (Correct):** The series lists 4-letter blocks in reverse alphabetical order, stepping backward by -4 each time: N(14)-4=10(J), giving JIHG.
- **B — KJIH:** KJIH overlaps K with the previous block instead of stepping by -4.
- **C — IHGF:** IHGF steps backward by -5 from N(14) instead of -4.
- **D — JIHF:** JIHF skips G at the end.
- **Exam Trick:** Reading backward from Z in blocks of 4: ZYXW (26-23), VUTS (22-19), RQPO (18-15), NMLK (14-11), JIHG (10-7).

### Q218 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Select the letter cluster that will replace the question mark (?): ACEG, DFHJ, GIKM, JLNP, ?

A. MOQT
B. MOQS
C. LNPR
D. NPQT

**Answer:** B. MOQS
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — MOQS (Correct):** Every position advances by +3: J(10)+3=M(13), L(12)+3=O(15), N(14)+3=Q(17), P(16)+3=S(19), giving MOQS.
- **A — MOQT:** MOQT shifts the fourth letter by +4 instead of +3.
- **C — LNPR:** LNPR shifts by +2 instead of +3.
- **D — NPQT:** NPQT shifts the first two letters by +4 instead of +3.
- **Exam Trick:** Each cluster maintains an internal +2 gap (M-O-Q-S), so any option without uniform +2 gaps can be eliminated immediately.

### Q219 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Find the missing letter cluster in the series: BDFH, DGJM, FJNR, HMRW, ?

A. JPVA
B. IPVB
C. JPVB
D. KQVB

**Answer:** C. JPVB
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — JPVB (Correct):** Position 1 shifts by +2 (B, D, F, H, J), Position 2 by +3 (D, G, J, M, P), Position 3 by +4 (F, J, N, R, V), and Position 4 by +5 (H, M, R, W, B), giving JPVB.
- **A — JPVA:** JPVA shifts the fourth letter by +4 instead of +5.
- **B — IPVB:** IPVB shifts the first letter by +1 instead of +2.
- **D — KQVB:** KQVB shifts the first two letters by +3 and +4 instead of +2 and +3.
- **Exam Trick:** For 4-letter clusters with progressive shifts (+2, +3, +4, +5), verify the first and last letters first to eliminate distractors rapidly.

### Q220 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Which letter cluster will replace the question mark (?) in the series: ZWTQ, WTQN, TQNK, QNKH, ?

A. MKHE
B. NKGD
C. NLHE
D. NKHE

**Answer:** D. NKHE
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — NKHE (Correct):** Every position steps backward by -3: Q(17)-3=N(14), N(14)-3=K(11), K(11)-3=H(8), H(8)-3=E(5), giving NKHE.
- **A — MKHE:** MKHE steps backward by -4 from Q(17) instead of -3.
- **B — NKGD:** NKGD steps backward by -4 on the last two letters.
- **C — NLHE:** NLHE steps backward by only -2 from N(14).
- **Exam Trick:** Each cluster also has a constant -3 internal gap (N-K-H-E), making it easy to spot invalid options.

### Q221 · Letter-Cluster Series - 4 Letters

Select the letter cluster that completes the series: CFLT, EHOU, GJRV, ILUW, ?

A. KNXX
B. KMXW
C. KNXY
D. LNXX

**Answer:** A. KNXX
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — KNXX (Correct):** Position 1: +2 (C,E,G,I,K); Position 2: +2 (F,H,J,L,N); Position 3: +3 (L,O,R,U,X); Position 4: +1 (T,U,V,W,X), giving KNXX.
- **B — KMXW:** KMXW shifts the second letter by +1 and the fourth by 0.
- **C — KNXY:** KNXY shifts the fourth letter by +2 instead of +1.
- **D — LNXX:** LNXX shifts the first letter by +3 instead of +2.
- **Exam Trick:** Repeated letters at the end (like XX in KNXX) are completely valid when different positions use independent shifts (+3 and +1).

### Q222 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Which term will replace the question mark (?) in the series: B2D, E4G, H8J, K16M, ?

A. N24P
B. N32P
C. M32O
D. N32O

**Answer:** B. N32P
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — N32P (Correct):** First letter: +3 (B, E, H, K, N). Middle number: ×2 (2, 4, 8, 16, 32). Last letter: +3 (D, G, J, M, P), giving N32P.
- **A — N24P:** N24P adds 8 instead of doubling 16.
- **C — M32O:** M32O advances the letters by +2 instead of +3.
- **D — N32O:** N32O advances the last letter by +2 instead of +3.
- **Exam Trick:** In alphanumeric clusters, solve the number component first — it usually eliminates half the options immediately.

### Q223 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Find the missing term in the series: A1Z, C4X, F9U, J16Q, ?

A. O25M
B. N25L
C. O25L
D. P25K

**Answer:** C. O25L
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — O25L (Correct):** First letter shifts by +2, +3, +4, +5 (A, C, F, J, O). Number is n² (1, 4, 9, 16, 25). Last letter is the opposite of the first (O=15 -> L=12, sum=27), giving O25L.
- **A — O25M:** O25M pairs O(15) with M(13), whose positions sum to 28 instead of 27.
- **B — N25L:** N25L shifts J(10) by +4 instead of +5.
- **D — P25K:** P25K shifts J(10) by +6 instead of +5.
- **Exam Trick:** Check whether the two letters in an alphanumeric cluster are opposites (sum to 27) or have a fixed gap.

### Q224 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Select the term that completes the series: D4, G9, J16, M25, ?

A. O36
B. P34
C. Q36
D. P36

**Answer:** D. P36
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — P36 (Correct):** Letters advance by +3 (D, G, J, M, P) and numbers are consecutive squares starting from 2² (4, 9, 16, 25, 36), giving P36.
- **A — O36:** O36 advances M(13) by +2 instead of +3.
- **B — P34:** P34 uses 34 instead of 6² = 36.
- **C — Q36:** Q36 advances M(13) by +4 instead of +3.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice the number here is (letter index/3 + 1)² rather than the direct letter position, so always verify both parts independently.

### Q225 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Which term will replace the question mark (?) in the series: Z26, X24, U21, Q17, ?

A. L12
B. M13
C. K11
D. L13

**Answer:** A. L12
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — L12 (Correct):** Each term pairs a letter with its exact alphabetical position, stepping back by -2, -3, -4, -5: Q(17) - 5 = 12, which is L12.
- **B — M13:** M13 subtracts 4 again instead of 5.
- **C — K11:** K11 subtracts 6 from 17 instead of 5.
- **D — L13:** L13 mismatches L(12) with 13.
- **Exam Trick:** When numbers equal the letter's alphabetical rank, any option with a mismatched letter-number pair is automatically wrong.

### Q226 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Find the next term in the series: 3F, 6G, 11I, 18L, ?

A. 27O
B. 27P
C. 25P
D. 29Q

**Answer:** B. 27P
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 27P (Correct):** Numbers increase by consecutive odd steps (+3, +5, +7, +9): 18 + 9 = 27. Letters shift by +1, +2, +3, +4: L(12) + 4 = P(16), giving 27P.
- **A — 27O:** 27O shifts L(12) by +3 again instead of +4.
- **C — 25P:** 25P adds 7 again instead of 9 to 18.
- **D — 29Q:** 29Q adds 11 to 18 and shifts L by +5.
- **Exam Trick:** Progressive shifts (+1, +2, +3, +4) in letters paired with (+3, +5, +7, +9) in numbers are classic SSC CHSL Tier-1 questions.

### Q227 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Select the missing term in the series: C3E, F6H, I9K, L12N, ?

A. O15P
B. N15Q
C. O15Q
D. O14Q

**Answer:** C. O15Q
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — O15Q (Correct):** First letter: +3 (C, F, I, L, O). Number: +3 (3, 6, 9, 12, 15, matching the first letter's position). Last letter: +3 (E, H, K, N, Q), giving O15Q.
- **A — O15P:** O15P shifts N(14) by +2 instead of +3.
- **B — N15Q:** N15Q shifts L(12) by +2 instead of +3.
- **D — O14Q:** O14Q adds 2 to 12 instead of 3.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice the middle number equals the alphabetical position of the first letter (C=3, F=6, I=9, L=12, O=15).

### Q228 · Mixed Alphanumeric Series

Which term completes the series? P5Q, R10T, T20W, V40Z, ?

A. X60C
B. W80C
C. X80B
D. X80C

**Answer:** D. X80C
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — X80C (Correct):** First letter: +2 (P, R, T, V, X). Number: ×2 (5, 10, 20, 40, 80). Last letter: +3 (Q, T, W, Z, C), giving X80C.
- **A — X60C:** X60C adds 20 instead of doubling 40.
- **B — W80C:** W80C shifts V(22) by +1 instead of +2.
- **C — X80B:** X80B shifts Z(26) by +2 instead of +3.
- **Exam Trick:** Wrap-around from Z(26) + 3 = 29 -> 29 - 26 = 3 (C).

### Q229 · Continuous Repeating Pattern Series

Which sequence of letters will complete the given pattern? a _ c a b _ a _ c _ b c

A. bcba
B. bcab
C. cbba
D. bbca

**Answer:** A. bcba
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — bcba (Correct):** The repeating block is 'abc' (a b c | a b c | a b c | a b c). Filling the blanks in order gives b, c, b, a -> bcba.
- **B — bcab:** bcab places 'a' in the 3rd blank and 'b' in the 4th, producing 'aacbbc'.
- **C — cbba:** cbba places 'c' in the 1st blank, producing 'acc'.
- **D — bbca:** bbca places 'c' in the 3rd blank, producing 'acc'.
- **Exam Trick:** Count total characters including blanks (here 12) and test divisors (3 or 4) to split the string into equal blocks.

### Q230 · Continuous Repeating Pattern Series

Select the correct combination of letters to fill the blanks: a _ b b _ a b _ a a _ b a _ b b

A. ababa
B. aabba
C. aabaa
D. babba

**Answer:** B. aabba
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — aabba (Correct):** The 16-character sequence repeats the 4-letter block 'aabb' (a a b b | a a b b | a a b b | a a b b). The blanks take a, a, b, b, a -> aabba.
- **A — ababa:** ababa places 'b' in the 2nd blank, creating 'babb'.
- **C — aabaa:** aabaa places 'a' in the 4th blank, creating 'aaab'.
- **D — babba:** babba starts with 'b', creating 'babb'.
- **Exam Trick:** Look for double-letter anchors like 'bb' and 'aa' in the prompt — they immediately reveal the 4-letter block 'aabb'.

### Q231 · Continuous Repeating Pattern Series

Which letters will sequentially replace the blanks to complete the series? a b _ d _ b c _ a _ c d a b _ d

A. cabdc
B. cadcb
C. cadbc
D. cdabc

**Answer:** C. cadbc
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
