- **Exam Trick:** In production-to-consumption sequences, editing/proofreading always precedes printing, and publishing precedes reading by the public.

### Q386 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Adult  2. Child  3. Infant  4. Adolescent  5. Elderly

A. 4, 5, 3, 2, 1
B. 4, 3, 2, 5, 1
C. 4, 3, 5, 2, 1
D. 4, 3, 5, 1, 2

**Answer:** C. 4, 3, 5, 2, 1
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 4, 3, 5, 2, 1 (Correct):** The water cycle proceeds as: Ocean (4) -> Evaporation (3) -> Condensation (5) -> Cloud (2) -> Rain (1).
- **A — 4, 5, 3, 2, 1:** Placing Condensation (5) before Evaporation (3) reverses the water cycle.
- **B — 4, 3, 2, 5, 1:** Placing Cloud (2) before Condensation (5) ignores that condensation forms clouds.
- **D — 4, 3, 5, 1, 2:** Placing Rain (1) before Cloud (2) is physically backwards, as rain falls from clouds.
- **Exam Trick:** In natural cycle questions, remember the physics sequence: heating/evaporation -> cooling/condensation -> cloud formation -> precipitation.

### Q387 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Cooking  2. Market  3. Eating  4. Cutting  5. Washing

A. 3, 2, 4, 1, 5
B. 2, 3, 4, 1, 5
C. 3, 2, 1, 4, 5
D. 3, 2, 4, 5, 1

**Answer:** A. 3, 2, 4, 1, 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 3, 2, 4, 1, 5 (Correct):** Arranging human life stages from youngest to oldest: Infant (3) -> Child (2) -> Adolescent (4) -> Adult (1) -> Elderly (5).
- **B — 2, 3, 4, 1, 5:** Starting with Child (2) before Infant (3) reverses the earliest two stages of life.
- **C — 3, 2, 1, 4, 5:** Placing Adult (1) before Adolescent (4) reverses the transition from teenager to adult.
- **D — 3, 2, 4, 5, 1:** Ending with Adult (1) instead of Elderly (5) breaks the ascending age order.
- **Exam Trick:** Adolescent represents the transitional teenage stage between Child and Adult.

### Q388 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Trial  2. Punishment  3. Crime  4. Conviction  5. Arrest

A. 2, 4, 5, 1, 3
B. 2, 5, 4, 1, 3
C. 2, 5, 1, 4, 3
D. 2, 5, 4, 3, 1

**Answer:** B. 2, 5, 4, 1, 3
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2, 5, 4, 1, 3 (Correct):** The logical sequence of preparing a meal is: Market (2) -> Washing (5) -> Cutting (4) -> Cooking (1) -> Eating (3).
- **A — 2, 4, 5, 1, 3:** Placing Cutting (4) before Washing (5) reverses standard food preparation hygiene.
- **C — 2, 5, 1, 4, 3:** Placing Cooking (1) before Cutting (4) reverses the preparation steps.
- **D — 2, 5, 4, 3, 1:** Placing Eating (3) before Cooking (1) is illogical for a cooked meal.
- **Exam Trick:** In domestic process questions, raw ingredients are bought, cleaned/washed, cut, cooked, and finally eaten.

### Q389 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Elephant  2. Ant  3. Cat  4. Whale  5. Dog

A. 3, 1, 5, 4, 2
B. 3, 5, 1, 2, 4
C. 3, 5, 1, 4, 2
D. 5, 3, 1, 4, 2

**Answer:** C. 3, 5, 1, 4, 2
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 3, 5, 1, 4, 2 (Correct):** The legal sequence from offence to sentence is: Crime (3) -> Arrest (5) -> Trial (1) -> Conviction (4) -> Punishment (2).
- **A — 3, 1, 5, 4, 2:** Placing Trial (1) before Arrest (5) reverses the legal procedure.
- **B — 3, 5, 1, 2, 4:** Placing Punishment (2) before Conviction (4) implies punishing before a guilty verdict.
- **D — 5, 3, 1, 4, 2:** Placing Arrest (5) before Crime (3) is chronologically backwards.
- **Exam Trick:** In criminal justice sequences, Conviction (finding guilty) always occurs after the Trial and before Punishment/Sentencing.

### Q390 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Hexagon  2. Triangle  3. Pentagon  4. Square  5. Octagon

A. 2, 5, 3, 1, 4
B. 2, 3, 5, 4, 1
C. 3, 2, 5, 1, 4
D. 2, 3, 5, 1, 4

**Answer:** D. 2, 3, 5, 1, 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 2, 3, 5, 1, 4 (Correct):** Arranging the animals in ascending order of physical size: Ant (2) -> Cat (3) -> Dog (5) -> Elephant (1) -> Whale (4).
- **A — 2, 5, 3, 1, 4:** Placing Dog (5) before Cat (3) reverses the ascending size order.
- **B — 2, 3, 5, 4, 1:** Placing Whale (4) before Elephant (1) puts the larger marine mammal before the land mammal.
- **C — 3, 2, 5, 1, 4:** Starting with Cat (3) instead of Ant (2) ignores that an ant is the smallest.
- **Exam Trick:** The Blue Whale is the largest animal on Earth, making Whale larger than Elephant in size-ordering questions.

### Q391 · Logical Word Sequence

Arrange the following words in a logical and meaningful order:
1. Doctor  2. Illness  3. Medicine  4. Diagnosis  5. Recovery

A. 2, 4, 3, 1, 5
B. 2, 3, 4, 1, 5
C. 2, 4, 1, 3, 5
D. 2, 4, 3, 5, 1

**Answer:** A. 2, 4, 3, 1, 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 2, 4, 3, 1, 5 (Correct):** Arranging polygons by increasing number of sides: Triangle (2, 3 sides) -> Square (4, 4 sides) -> Pentagon (3, 5 sides) -> Hexagon (1, 6 sides) -> Octagon (5, 8 sides).
- **B — 2, 3, 4, 1, 5:** Placing Pentagon (3) before Square (4) puts 5 sides before 4 sides.
- **C — 2, 4, 1, 3, 5:** Placing Hexagon (1) before Pentagon (3) puts 6 sides before 5 sides.
- **D — 2, 4, 3, 5, 1:** Placing Octagon (5) before Hexagon (1) puts 8 sides before 6 sides.
- **Exam Trick:** Greek prefixes help recall side counts: Tri (3), Penta (5), Hexa (6), Hepta (7), Octa (8), Nona (9), Deca (10).

### Q392 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
INTERNATIONAL

A. 2, 4, 1, 3, 5
B. 2, 1, 4, 3, 5
C. 2, 1, 3, 4, 5
D. 1, 2, 4, 3, 5

**Answer:** B. 2, 1, 4, 3, 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 2, 1, 4, 3, 5 (Correct):** The medical sequence is: Illness (2) -> Doctor (1) -> Diagnosis (4) -> Medicine (3) -> Recovery (5).
- **A — 2, 4, 1, 3, 5:** Placing Diagnosis (4) before Doctor (1) skips consulting the doctor first.
- **C — 2, 1, 3, 4, 5:** Placing Medicine (3) before Diagnosis (4) reverses diagnosis and prescription.
- **D — 1, 2, 4, 3, 5:** Placing Doctor (1) before Illness (2) reverses the cause for visiting a doctor.
- **Exam Trick:** Medical sequences always follow: Symptom/Illness -> Consultation (Doctor) -> Diagnosis -> Treatment/Medicine -> Recovery.

### Q393 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
ENVIRONMENT

A. TRAIN
B. RATION
C. LETTER
D. LATER

**Answer:** C. LETTER
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — LETTER (Correct):** LETTER cannot be formed because it requires two 'E's, whereas INTERNATIONAL contains only one 'E'.
- **A — TRAIN:** TRAIN can be formed using T, R, A, I, and N from INTERNATIONAL.
- **B — RATION:** RATION can be formed using R, A, T, I, O, and N from INTERNATIONAL.
- **D — LATER:** LATER can be formed using L, A, T, E, and R from INTERNATIONAL.
- **Exam Trick:** Always check letter frequencies (especially repeated vowels like E, A, I, O) rather than just checking if a letter exists once.

### Q394 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
CONSTITUTION

A. MENTOR
B. MOVER
C. ENTER
D. NATION

**Answer:** D. NATION
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — NATION (Correct):** NATION cannot be formed because the letter 'A' is not present in ENVIRONMENT.
- **A — MENTOR:** MENTOR can be formed using M, E, N, T, O, and R from ENVIRONMENT.
- **B — MOVER:** MOVER can be formed using M, O, V, E, and R from ENVIRONMENT.
- **C — ENTER:** ENTER can be formed using both E's, N, T, and R from ENVIRONMENT.
- **Exam Trick:** Scan for completely absent letters first—if an option has a letter missing from the parent word, you have the answer immediately.

### Q395 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
COMMUNICATION

A. TUITION
B. CONSULT
C. NOTION
D. STOUT

**Answer:** B. CONSULT
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — CONSULT (Correct):** CONSULT cannot be formed because the letter 'L' is not present in CONSTITUTION.
- **A — TUITION:** TUITION can be formed using T(2), U, I(2), O, and N from CONSTITUTION.
- **C — NOTION:** NOTION can be formed using N(2), O(2), T, and I from CONSTITUTION.
- **D — STOUT:** STOUT can be formed using S, T(2), O, and U from CONSTITUTION.
- **Exam Trick:** Do not assume a plausible-sounding word like CONSULT uses the same root letters; verify every consonant.

### Q396 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
DEMOGRAPHY

A. MUSIC
B. ACTION
C. UNION
D. COUNT

**Answer:** A. MUSIC
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — MUSIC (Correct):** MUSIC cannot be formed because the letter 'S' is not present in COMMUNICATION.
- **B — ACTION:** ACTION can be formed using A, C, T, I, O, and N from COMMUNICATION.
- **C — UNION:** UNION can be formed using U, N(2), I, and O from COMMUNICATION.
- **D — COUNT:** COUNT can be formed using C, O, U, N, and T from COMMUNICATION.
- **Exam Trick:** Common distractors in word formation include letters like S, D, or R that feel natural with the theme of the parent word.

### Q397 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
ADMINISTRATION

A. GRADE
B. DREAM
C. GROOM
D. GRAPH

**Answer:** C. GROOM
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — GROOM (Correct):** GROOM cannot be formed because it requires two 'O's, whereas DEMOGRAPHY contains only one 'O'.
- **A — GRADE:** GRADE can be formed using G, R, A, D, and E from DEMOGRAPHY.
- **B — DREAM:** DREAM can be formed using D, R, E, A, and M from DEMOGRAPHY.
- **D — GRAPH:** GRAPH can be formed using G, R, A, P, and H from DEMOGRAPHY.
- **Exam Trick:** In words like DEMOGRAPHY where every letter appears only once, any option with a doubled letter is automatically impossible to form.

### Q398 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CANNOT be formed using the letters of the given word:
PERPENDICULAR

A. STATION
B. STRAIN
C. RATION
D. MINISTER

**Answer:** D. MINISTER
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — MINISTER (Correct):** MINISTER cannot be formed because the letter 'E' is not present in ADMINISTRATION.
- **A — STATION:** STATION can be formed using S, T(2), A, I, O, and N from ADMINISTRATION.
- **B — STRAIN:** STRAIN can be formed using S, T, R, A, I, and N from ADMINISTRATION.
- **C — RATION:** RATION can be formed using R, A, T, I, O, and N from ADMINISTRATION.
- **Exam Trick:** Checking for vowels (A, E, I, O, U) absent from the parent word is the fastest elimination trick.

### Q399 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CAN be formed using the letters of the given word:
ESTABLISHMENT

A. PENINSULA
B. DECLINE
C. REPUBLIC
D. CALENDAR

**Answer:** B. DECLINE
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — DECLINE (Correct):** DECLINE can be formed using D, E(2), C, L, I, and N, all of which are available in PERPENDICULAR.
- **A — PENINSULA:** PENINSULA cannot be formed because 'S' is not in PERPENDICULAR.
- **C — REPUBLIC:** REPUBLIC cannot be formed because 'B' is not in PERPENDICULAR.
- **D — CALENDAR:** CALENDAR cannot be formed because it needs two 'A's, while PERPENDICULAR has only one 'A'.
- **Exam Trick:** When the question asks which word CAN be formed, three options will fail either due to a missing letter or an exceeded letter count.

### Q400 · Word Formation

From the given alternatives, select the word which CAN be formed using the letters of the given word:
CONCENTRATION

A. TABLE
B. BLUNT
C. TEACH
D. BRAVE

**Answer:** A. TABLE
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — TABLE (Correct):** TABLE can be formed using T, A, B, L, and E, all of which are present in ESTABLISHMENT.
- **B — BLUNT:** BLUNT cannot be formed because 'U' is not in ESTABLISHMENT.
- **C — TEACH:** TEACH cannot be formed because 'C' is not in ESTABLISHMENT.
- **D — BRAVE:** BRAVE cannot be formed because 'R' and 'V' are not in ESTABLISHMENT.
- **Exam Trick:** Read carefully whether the prompt asks for 'CAN be formed' or 'CANNOT be formed'—this single word flips the entire logic.

---

## Chapter 6: Blood Relations, Ordering & Seating Arrangement

### Q401 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a photograph of a man, Priya said, "He is the only son of my mother's father." How is the man in the photograph related to Priya?

A. Maternal Uncle
B. Father
C. Brother
D. Cousin

**Answer:** A. Maternal Uncle
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Maternal Uncle (Correct):** "My mother's father" refers to Priya's maternal grandfather. The only son of Priya's maternal grandfather is Priya's mother's brother, who is Priya's maternal uncle. Therefore, the man in the photograph is Priya's maternal uncle.
- **B — Father:** Father would be the husband of Priya's mother, not her mother's brother.
- **C — Brother:** Brother would be the son of Priya's own parents, not her maternal grandfather.
- **D — Cousin:** Cousin would be the child of Priya's uncle or aunt, one generation lower than the maternal grandfather's son.
- **Exam Trick:** In pointing questions, always break the possessive chain backwards from "my" (the speaker) step by step.

### Q402 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a woman in a portrait, Rohit (a man) said, "She is the only daughter of my father's only son." How is the woman related to Rohit?

A. Daughter
B. Sister
C. Niece
D. Mother

**Answer:** A. Daughter
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Daughter (Correct):** Since Rohit is a man, "my father's only son" refers to Rohit himself. The only daughter of Rohit is Rohit's own daughter. Therefore, the woman in the portrait is Rohit's daughter.
- **B — Sister:** Sister would be another daughter of Rohit's father, whereas the clue specifies the daughter of his father's only son.
- **C — Niece:** Niece would be the daughter of Rohit's sibling, which contradicts "my father's only son".
- **D — Mother:** Mother is two generations above the daughter described in the statement.
- **Exam Trick:** When a male speaker says "my father's only son" or a female speaker says "my mother's only daughter", the phrase always refers to the speaker themselves.

### Q403 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a boy, Meera said, "He is the son of my husband's only sister." How is the boy related to Meera?

A. Son
B. Nephew
C. Brother-in-law
D. Cousin

**Answer:** B. Nephew
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Nephew (Correct):** Meera's husband's only sister is Meera's sister-in-law. The son of one's sister-in-law is one's nephew. Therefore, the boy is Meera's nephew.
- **A — Son:** Son would be Meera's own child, not her husband's sister's child.
- **C — Brother-in-law:** Brother-in-law is the husband's brother or sister's husband, which is in the same generation as Meera.
- **D — Cousin:** Cousin would be the child of Meera's uncle or aunt, not her husband's sister.
- **Exam Trick:** A sibling's child or spouse's sibling's child is always a nephew (male) or niece (female) in SSC reasoning.

### Q404 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a photograph, Karan said, "The woman in this photo is the mother of my son's wife." How is the woman related to Karan's son?

A. Mother
B. Aunt
C. Mother-in-law
D. Sister-in-law

**Answer:** C. Mother-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Mother-in-law (Correct):** "My son's wife" refers to Karan's daughter-in-law. The mother of Karan's son's wife is the mother-in-law of Karan's son. Therefore, the woman is the mother-in-law of Karan's son.
- **A — Mother:** Mother of Karan's son would be Karan's wife, not his son's wife's mother.
- **B — Aunt:** Aunt is a parent's sister, which does not match the wife's mother.
- **D — Sister-in-law:** Sister-in-law would be the wife's sister, whereas the woman is the wife's mother.
- **Exam Trick:** Read the target of the question carefully ("related to Karan's son" vs "related to Karan") to avoid answering for the wrong person.

### Q405 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a man on the stage, Anjali said, "He is the brother of the daughter of the wife of my husband." How is the man on the stage related to Anjali?

A. Nephew
B. Brother
C. Husband
D. Son

**Answer:** D. Son
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Son (Correct):** Since Anjali is a woman, "the wife of my husband" refers to Anjali herself. The daughter of Anjali is Anjali's daughter, and the brother of Anjali's daughter is Anjali's son. Therefore, the man on the stage is Anjali's son.
- **A — Nephew:** Nephew would be the son of Anjali's sibling, not her own child.
- **B — Brother:** Brother would be in Anjali's own generation, whereas the man is her daughter's brother.
- **C — Husband:** Husband is Anjali's spouse, one generation above her daughter's brother.
- **Exam Trick:** Substitute "the wife of my husband" directly with "me" when the speaker is female to simplify the chain instantly.

### Q406 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a girl, Sneha said, "She is the only daughter of my mother's only son." If Sneha has only one brother, how is the girl related to Sneha?

A. Daughter
B. Niece
C. Sister
D. Cousin

**Answer:** B. Niece
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Niece (Correct):** Since Sneha has only one brother, "my mother's only son" refers to Sneha's brother. The only daughter of Sneha's brother is Sneha's niece. Therefore, the girl is Sneha's niece.
- **A — Daughter:** Daughter would be Sneha's own child, not her brother's child.
- **C — Sister:** Sister is in the same generation as Sneha, whereas the girl is one generation below.
- **D — Cousin:** Cousin would be the daughter of Sneha's uncle or aunt, not her brother.
- **Exam Trick:** Always verify whether the speaker is male or female before interpreting "mother's only son" or "father's only daughter".

### Q407 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to an old man, Vikram said, "His son is my son's uncle." If Vikram has only one brother and no sisters, how is the old man related to Vikram?

A. Father
B. Grandfather
C. Uncle
D. Father-in-law

**Answer:** A. Father
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Father (Correct):** Since Vikram has only one brother and no sisters, "my son's uncle" refers to Vikram's brother. The old man's son is Vikram's brother, which means the old man is the father of both Vikram's brother and Vikram. Therefore, the old man is Vikram's father.
- **B — Grandfather:** Grandfather would make Vikram's brother the grandson of the old man, not his son.
- **C — Uncle:** Uncle would make Vikram's brother the cousin of Vikram, not his brother.
- **D — Father-in-law:** Father-in-law would apply only if the uncle were Vikram's wife's brother, which is ruled out by the given condition.
- **Exam Trick:** Equate the two halves of "His X is my Y" by finding the common person linking both speakers.

### Q408 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a woman, Suresh said, "Her mother is the only daughter of my mother-in-law." How is Suresh related to the woman?

A. Brother
B. Uncle
C. Father
D. Grandfather

**Answer:** C. Father
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Father (Correct):** "The only daughter of my mother-in-law" refers to Suresh's wife. Since the woman's mother is Suresh's wife, the woman is Suresh's daughter, making Suresh the woman's father.
- **A — Brother:** Brother would mean Suresh and the woman share the same parents, which is false since Suresh is married to her mother.
- **B — Uncle:** Uncle would mean Suresh is the brother of one of the woman's parents, not her mother's husband.
- **D — Grandfather:** Grandfather is one generation too high, as Suresh is married to the woman's mother.
- **Exam Trick:** For a married man, "my mother-in-law's only daughter" always points to his wife.

### Q409 · Blood Relations - Pointing

Pointing to a man in a photograph, Kavita said, "He is the father of my brother's only sister's son." If Kavita is the only daughter of her parents, how is the man related to Kavita?

A. Brother
B. Father
C. Son
D. Husband

**Answer:** D. Husband
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Husband (Correct):** Since Kavita is the only daughter of her parents, "my brother's only sister" refers to Kavita herself. Thus, "my brother's only sister's son" is Kavita's son, and the father of Kavita's son is Kavita's husband. Therefore, the man is Kavita's husband.
- **A — Brother:** Brother cannot be the father of Kavita's son.
- **B — Father:** Father would be Kavita's parent, one generation above Kavita's husband.
- **C — Son:** Son is one generation below, whereas the man is the father of Kavita's son.
- **Exam Trick:** When a female speaker is the only daughter of her parents, "my brother's only sister" reduces directly to the speaker.

### Q410 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

A is the father of B. C is the sister of A. D is the mother of C. E is the brother of D. How is D related to B?

A. Mother
B. Aunt
C. Grandmother
D. Sister

**Answer:** C. Grandmother
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Grandmother (Correct):** A is the father of B, and C is the sister of A, so A and C are siblings. D is the mother of C, which means D is also the mother of A. Since A is B's father, D is B's paternal grandmother.
- **A — Mother:** Mother is one generation above B, whereas D is A's mother (two generations above B).
- **B — Aunt:** Aunt would be a sibling of A, whereas D is A's mother.
- **D — Sister:** Sister is in the same generation as B, not two generations above.
- **Exam Trick:** Draw a vertical family tree with generations on separate levels to see grandparent-grandchild links at a glance.

### Q411 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

P is the mother of Q. Q is the sister of R. R is the father of S. T is the wife of R. How is P related to T?

A. Mother-in-law
B. Aunt
C. Mother
D. Sister-in-law

**Answer:** A. Mother-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Mother-in-law (Correct):** P is the mother of Q, and Q is the sister of R, which means P is also the mother of R. Since T is the wife of R, P is the mother of T's husband, making P the mother-in-law of T.
- **B — Aunt:** Aunt is a parent's sister, not a spouse's mother.
- **C — Mother:** Mother would be T's own mother, whereas P is R's mother.
- **D — Sister-in-law:** Sister-in-law would be in the same generation as T, not one generation above.
- **Exam Trick:** Use standard symbols (+ for male, - for female, = for marriage) on rough paper to avoid mixing up in-law relationships.

### Q412 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

M is the brother of N. N is the wife of O. O is the father of P. Q is the only sister of P. How is M related to Q?

A. Paternal Uncle
B. Maternal Uncle
C. Father
D. Brother

**Answer:** B. Maternal Uncle
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Maternal Uncle (Correct):** N is the wife of O, O is the father of P, and Q is the only sister of P, which means N is the mother of Q. Since M is the brother of N, M is Q's mother's brother, i.e., Q's maternal uncle.
- **A — Paternal Uncle:** Paternal Uncle would be O's brother, whereas M is N's (the mother's) brother.
- **C — Father:** Father of Q is O, not M.
- **D — Brother:** Brother is in Q's generation, whereas M is one generation above Q.
- **Exam Trick:** Always distinguish paternal (father's side) from maternal (mother's side) when both options appear in SSC exams.

### Q413 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

X and Y are brothers. Z is the father of X. W is the sister of Z. V is the son of W. How is V related to Y?

A. Brother
B. Nephew
C. Uncle
D. Cousin

**Answer:** D. Cousin
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Cousin (Correct):** X and Y are brothers, and Z is the father of X, so Z is also the father of Y. W is the sister of Z, making W the paternal aunt of Y. Since V is the son of W, V is Y's cousin.
- **A — Brother:** Brother would require V to be Z's son, not W's son.
- **B — Nephew:** Nephew would be one generation below Y, whereas V is in Y's generation.
- **C — Uncle:** Uncle would be one generation above Y, whereas V is the son of Y's aunt.
- **Exam Trick:** Children of siblings (uncle's or aunt's children) are always cousins, regardless of whether they are on the paternal or maternal side.

### Q414 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

R is the daughter of S. S is the husband of T. U is the brother of T. V is the mother of U. How is V related to R?

A. Maternal Grandmother
B. Paternal Grandmother
C. Mother
D. Aunt

**Answer:** A. Maternal Grandmother
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Maternal Grandmother (Correct):** R is the daughter of S, and S is the husband of T, so T is R's mother. U is the brother of T, and V is the mother of U, which means V is also T's mother. Therefore, V is R's maternal grandmother.
- **B — Paternal Grandmother:** Paternal Grandmother would be S's mother, whereas V is T's (the mother's) mother.
- **C — Mother:** Mother of R is T, one generation below V.
- **D — Aunt:** Aunt is a parent's sister, whereas V is T's mother.
- **Exam Trick:** Trace marriage links first to identify the child's mother and father before moving up to grandparents.

### Q415 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

In a family of six persons A, B, C, D, E, and F, there are two married couples. D is the grandmother of A and mother of B. C is the wife of B and mother of F. F is the granddaughter of E. Who is E's son?

A. A
B. B
C. C
D. F

**Answer:** B. B
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — B (Correct):** D is the mother of B and grandmother of A, while C is the wife of B and mother of F. Since F is the granddaughter of E and there are two married couples in the six-person family, D and E must be the senior married couple. Therefore, E is B's father, making B the son of E.
- **A — A:** A is the grandchild of D and E, not E's son.
- **C — C:** C is the wife of B, making her E's daughter-in-law.
- **D — F:** F is explicitly stated to be E's granddaughter, not son.
- **Exam Trick:** In fixed-size family puzzles, count the couples and generations first to pair up grandparents and parents quickly.

### Q416 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

K is the son of L. L is the daughter of M. N is the husband of M. O is the only brother of L. How is K related to O?

A. Son
B. Cousin
C. Nephew
D. Brother

**Answer:** C. Nephew
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Nephew (Correct):** L is the daughter of M and N, and O is the only brother of L. K is the son of L, so O is K's maternal uncle. Since K is male (son of L), K is O's nephew.
- **A — Son:** Son would require K to be O's own child, whereas K is O's sister's child.
- **B — Cousin:** Cousin is in the same generation as O, whereas K is one generation below O.
- **D — Brother:** Brother is in the same generation as O, whereas K is L's son.
- **Exam Trick:** Pay attention to direction: "How is K related to O?" asks for K's role relative to O, not O's role relative to K.

### Q417 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

A is the sister of B. B is married to C. C is the son of D, and D is the father of C. E is the daughter of A. How is D related to B?

A. Father
B. Uncle
C. Brother-in-law
D. Father-in-law

**Answer:** D. Father-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Father-in-law (Correct):** C is the son of D, and D is the father of C, so C is male. Since B is married to C, B is C's wife. Therefore, D (C's father) is B's father-in-law.
- **A — Father:** Father would be B's own father, not her husband's father.
- **B — Uncle:** Uncle is a parent's brother, not a spouse's father.
- **C — Brother-in-law:** Brother-in-law is in the same generation as B, whereas D is C's father.
- **Exam Trick:** A spouse's father is always the father-in-law, regardless of whether the spouse is the husband or wife.

### Q418 · Blood Relations - Family Tree

G is the father of H. H is the only sister of J. J is married to K. L is the mother of K. If J is the father of M, how is G related to M?

A. Paternal Grandfather
B. Maternal Grandfather
C. Father
D. Uncle

**Answer:** A. Paternal Grandfather
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Paternal Grandfather (Correct):** G is the father of H, and H is the only sister of J, so G is also the father of J. Since J is the father of M, G is M's father's father, i.e., M's paternal grandfather.
- **B — Maternal Grandfather:** Maternal Grandfather would be M's mother's father, whereas J is M's father.
- **C — Father:** Father of M is J, one generation below G.
- **D — Uncle:** Uncle would be J's brother, whereas G is J's father.
- **Exam Trick:** When two people are siblings, any parent of one is automatically the parent of the other.

### Q419 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A + B' means 'A is the father of B', 'A - B' means 'A is the mother of B', 'A × B' means 'A is the brother of B', and 'A ÷ B' means 'A is the sister of B', then in the expression P + Q × R ÷ S, how is P related to S?

A. Father
B. Uncle
C. Grandfather
D. Brother

**Answer:** A. Father
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Father (Correct):** Decoding the expression: P + Q means P is the father of Q; Q × R means Q is the brother of R; R ÷ S means R is the sister of S. Since Q, R, and S are siblings and P is Q's father, P is also the father of S.
- **B — Uncle:** Uncle would be P's brother, whereas P is directly the parent of Q, R, and S.
- **C — Grandfather:** Grandfather is two generations above S, whereas P is only one generation above.
- **D — Brother:** Brother is in the same generation as S, whereas P is S's father.
- **Exam Trick:** In coded blood relations, first convert each symbol into a generation shift (+1, 0, -1) and gender marker before drawing the tree.

### Q420 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A @ B' means 'A is the wife of B', 'A # B' means 'A is the son of B', 'A $ B' means 'A is the sister of B', and 'A % B' means 'A is the father of B', then in the expression M % N @ O # P $ Q, how is P related to N?

A. Mother
B. Mother-in-law
C. Sister-in-law
D. Aunt

**Answer:** B. Mother-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Mother-in-law (Correct):** Decoding the expression: N @ O means N is the wife of O; O # P means O is the son of P; P $ Q means P is the sister of Q (so P is female). Thus P is O's mother, and since N is O's wife, P is N's mother-in-law.
- **A — Mother:** Mother would be N's own mother, whereas P is N's husband O's mother.
- **C — Sister-in-law:** Sister-in-law is in the same generation as N, whereas P is O's mother.
- **D — Aunt:** Aunt is a parent's sister, not a spouse's mother.
- **Exam Trick:** The last symbol in a chain (here P $ Q) is often included solely to fix the gender of the second-to-last person (P).

### Q421 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A * B' means 'A is the daughter of B', 'A & B' means 'A is the husband of B', 'A ^ B' means 'A is the brother of B', and 'A ! B' means 'A is the mother of B', then in the expression T * U & V ! W, how is T related to W?

A. Daughter
B. Mother
C. Sister
D. Niece

**Answer:** C. Sister
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Sister (Correct):** Decoding the expression: T * U means T is the daughter of U; U & V means U is the husband of V; V ! W means V is the mother of W. Thus U and V are a married couple and the parents of both T and W. Since T is female (daughter of U), T is W's sister.
- **A — Daughter:** Daughter would put T one generation below W, whereas both T and W are children of U and V.
- **B — Mother:** Mother would put T one generation above W.
- **D — Niece:** Niece would require T to be the daughter of W's sibling, not W's parent.
- **Exam Trick:** When A is the husband of B and B is the mother of C, A and B form a couple sharing all children in that branch.

### Q422 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A > B' means 'A is the brother of B', 'A < B' means 'A is the father of B', 'A = B' means 'A is the mother of B', and 'A ~ B' means 'A is the wife of B', which of the following expressions shows that 'K is the paternal grandmother of N'?

A. K ~ L < M > N
B. K = L ~ M < N
C. K < L > M < N
D. K = L < M > N

**Answer:** D. K = L < M > N
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — K = L < M > N (Correct):** For K to be the paternal grandmother of N, K must be female, two generations above N, and the mother of N's father. In 'K = L < M > N', K is the mother of L, L is the father of M, and M is the brother of N (so L is also the father of N), making K the paternal grandmother of N.
- **A — K ~ L < M > N:** In 'K ~ L < M > N', K is the wife of L (who is N's father), making K the mother of N, not the grandmother.
- **B — K = L ~ M < N:** In 'K = L ~ M < N', K is the mother of L and L is the wife of M (N's father), making K the maternal grandmother of N.
- **C — K < L > M < N:** In 'K < L > M < N', K is the father of L (who is the brother of M, N's father), making K the paternal grandfather of N.
- **Exam Trick:** Use gender and generation elimination first: any option where K is male (< or >) or only one generation above N can be rejected immediately.

### Q423 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A + B' means 'A is the sister of B', 'A - B' means 'A is the father of B', 'A × B' means 'A is the mother of B', and 'A ÷ B' means 'A is the son of B', then in the expression E ÷ F - G + H, how is E related to H?

A. Brother
B. Father
C. Son
D. Uncle

**Answer:** A. Brother
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Brother (Correct):** Decoding the expression: E ÷ F means E is the son of F; F - G means F is the father of G; G + H means G is the sister of H. Thus F is the father of E, G, and H, and E is male (son of F). Therefore, E is H's brother.
- **B — Father:** Father of H is F, not E.
- **C — Son:** Son would place E one generation below H, whereas both are children of F.
- **D — Uncle:** Uncle would place E one generation above H.
- **Exam Trick:** Even if H's gender is unknown, E's gender is fixed as male by 'E ÷ F', which uniquely determines E's relation to H as brother.

### Q424 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A $ B' means 'A is the father of B', 'A # B' means 'A is the mother of B', 'A @ B' means 'A is the wife of B', and 'A % B' means 'A is the brother of B', then in the expression P % Q $ R % S, how is P related to S?

A. Father
B. Paternal Uncle
C. Grandfather
D. Brother

**Answer:** B. Paternal Uncle
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Paternal Uncle (Correct):** Decoding the expression: P % Q means P is the brother of Q; Q $ R means Q is the father of R; R % S means R is the brother of S. Since Q is the father of R and R is S's brother, Q is also S's father. Because P is Q's brother, P is S's paternal uncle.
- **A — Father:** Father of S is Q, not P.
- **C — Grandfather:** Grandfather would be Q's father, whereas P is Q's brother.
- **D — Brother:** Brother would be in S's generation, whereas P is in Q's generation.
- **Exam Trick:** A father's brother is always a paternal uncle, while a mother's brother is a maternal uncle.

### Q425 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A © B' means 'A is the mother of B', 'A ® B' means 'A is the sister of B', 'A ™ B' means 'A is the husband of B', and 'A § B' means 'A is the daughter of B', then in the expression W § X © Y ® Z, how is W related to Z?

A. Mother
B. Aunt
C. Sister
D. Daughter

**Answer:** C. Sister
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Sister (Correct):** Decoding the expression: W § X means W is the daughter of X; X © Y means X is the mother of Y; Y ® Z means Y is the sister of Z. Thus X is the mother of W, Y, and Z. Since W is female (daughter of X), W is Z's sister.
- **A — Mother:** Mother of Z is X, not W.
- **B — Aunt:** Aunt would be X's sister, whereas W is X's daughter.
- **D — Daughter:** Daughter would place W one generation below Z, whereas both share the same mother X.
- **Exam Trick:** Watch out for operators that flip direction vs. keep forward order; here every operator is 'A is [relation] of B'.

### Q426 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A + B' means 'A is the son of B', 'A - B' means 'A is the wife of B', 'A × B' means 'A is the father of B', and 'A ÷ B' means 'A is the sister of B', then in the expression A + B - C × D ÷ E, how is A related to E?

A. Father
B. Son
C. Cousin
D. Brother

**Answer:** D. Brother
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Brother (Correct):** Decoding the expression: A + B means A is the son of B; B - C means B is the wife of C; C × D means C is the father of D; D ÷ E means D is the sister of E. Thus B and C are a married couple and the parents of A, D, and E. Since A is male (son of B), A is E's brother.
- **A — Father:** Father of E is C, not A.
- **B — Son:** Son would place A one generation below E, whereas both are children of B and C.
- **C — Cousin:** Cousin would require A and E to have different parents who are siblings.
- **Exam Trick:** Linking a couple (B - C) means children of B (like A) and children of C (like D and E) are all siblings.

### Q427 · Blood Relations - Coded

If 'A ? B' means 'A is the brother of B', 'A ! B' means 'A is the daughter of B', 'A : B' means 'A is the father of B', and 'A ; B' means 'A is the mother of B', which of the following expressions shows that 'R is the niece of T'?

A. R ! S ? T
B. T ? S ! R
C. R ? S : T
D. R ! S : T

**Answer:** A. R ! S ? T
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — R ! S ? T (Correct):** For R to be the niece of T, R must be female and the daughter of T's sibling. In 'R ! S ? T', R is the daughter of S and S is the brother of T, which directly makes R the niece of T.
- **B — T ? S ! R:** In 'T ? S ! R', T is the brother of S and S is the daughter of R, making T the son of R.
- **C — R ? S : T:** In 'R ? S : T', R is the brother of S and S is the father of T, making R the paternal uncle of T.
- **D — R ! S : T:** In 'R ! S : T', R is the daughter of S and S is the father of T, making R the sister of T.
- **Exam Trick:** Check the first symbol attached to R to immediately filter out options where R is male or in the wrong generation.

### Q428 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Rahul is the only son of Mohan, who is the husband of Sunita.\nStatement II: Sunita is the mother-in-law of Kavya, and Kavya has a son named Aarav.\nHow is Mohan related to Aarav?

A. Father
B. Paternal Grandfather
C. Maternal Grandfather
D. Uncle

**Answer:** B. Paternal Grandfather
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Paternal Grandfather (Correct):** From Statement I, Mohan is the husband of Sunita and Rahul is their only son. From Statement II, Sunita is the mother-in-law of Kavya, so Kavya is Rahul's wife, and Aarav (Kavya's son) is Rahul's son. Therefore, Mohan is Aarav's paternal grandfather.
- **A — Father:** Father of Aarav is Rahul, not Mohan.
- **C — Maternal Grandfather:** Maternal Grandfather would be Kavya's father, whereas Mohan is Rahul's (the father's) father.
- **D — Uncle:** Uncle is in Rahul's generation, whereas Mohan is two generations above Aarav.
- **Exam Trick:** In two-statement puzzles, combine the common person (here Sunita and Rahul) to merge both statements into one tree.

### Q429 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Deepa is the sister of Vivek, and Vivek is the father of Tanvi.\nStatement II: Tanvi is the only daughter of Meenakshi.\nHow is Deepa related to Meenakshi?

A. Sister
B. Aunt
C. Sister-in-law
D. Mother-in-law

**Answer:** C. Sister-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Sister-in-law (Correct):** From Statement I, Vivek is the father of Tanvi, and Deepa is Vivek's sister. From Statement II, Tanvi is the only daughter of Meenakshi, which means Meenakshi is Vivek's wife. Therefore, Deepa (Vivek's sister) is Meenakshi's sister-in-law.
- **A — Sister:** Sister would require Deepa and Meenakshi to have the same parents, whereas Deepa is Vivek's sister.
- **B — Aunt:** Aunt is one generation above Meenakshi, whereas Deepa is in the same generation.
- **D — Mother-in-law:** Mother-in-law would be Vivek's mother, not his sister.
- **Exam Trick:** When two different people are both parents of the same child (Tanvi), they form a married couple.

### Q430 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Harish is the brother of Ramesh's father.\nStatement II: Pooja is the only sister of Harish, and Suman is the daughter of Pooja.\nHow is Suman related to Ramesh?

A. Sister
B. Niece
C. Aunt
D. Cousin

**Answer:** D. Cousin
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Cousin (Correct):** From Statement I, Harish is the brother of Ramesh's father (Ramesh's paternal uncle). From Statement II, Pooja is the sister of Harish (so Pooja is Ramesh's paternal aunt) and Suman is Pooja's daughter. Therefore, Suman is Ramesh's cousin.
- **A — Sister:** Sister would require Suman to be the daughter of Ramesh's own parents.
- **B — Niece:** Niece would place Suman one generation below Ramesh.
- **C — Aunt:** Aunt is Pooja (one generation above Ramesh), not Suman.
- **Exam Trick:** Your father's sister's child is always your cousin.

### Q431 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Geeta is the mother of Sanjay, and Sanjay is the husband of Rekha.\nStatement II: Rekha is the only sister of Tarun, who is the son of Mahendra.\nHow is Mahendra related to Sanjay?

A. Father-in-law
B. Father
C. Uncle
D. Brother-in-law

**Answer:** A. Father-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Father-in-law (Correct):** From Statement I, Sanjay is the husband of Rekha. From Statement II, Rekha is the sister of Tarun, who is the son of Mahendra, which means Mahendra is Rekha's father. Therefore, Mahendra is Sanjay's father-in-law.
- **B — Father:** Father of Sanjay is not Mahendra; Sanjay is married to Mahendra's daughter.
- **C — Uncle:** Uncle is a parent's brother, not a wife's father.
- **D — Brother-in-law:** Brother-in-law is Tarun (Rekha's brother), not Mahendra (Rekha's father).
- **Exam Trick:** Notice that Geeta's information is extra context; focus on the chain connecting Mahendra to Sanjay via Rekha.

### Q432 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Aman is the son of Bhavna, who is the only daughter of Chetan.\nStatement II: Chetan is the husband of Devika, and Devika has no other children.\nHow is Devika related to Aman?

A. Paternal Grandmother
B. Maternal Grandmother
C. Mother
D. Aunt

**Answer:** B. Maternal Grandmother
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Maternal Grandmother (Correct):** From Statement I, Aman is the son of Bhavna, and Bhavna is the daughter of Chetan. From Statement II, Chetan is the husband of Devika, so Devika is Bhavna's mother. Therefore, Devika is Aman's maternal grandmother.
- **A — Paternal Grandmother:** Paternal Grandmother would be Aman's father's mother, whereas Devika is Aman's mother's mother.
- **C — Mother:** Mother of Aman is Bhavna, one generation below Devika.
- **D — Aunt:** Aunt is a parent's sister, not a grandparent.
- **Exam Trick:** Mother's mother is always the maternal grandmother.

### Q433 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Nitin is the father of Prateek, and Prateek is the brother of Shreya.\nStatement II: Shreya is the wife of Gaurav, and Gaurav is the only son of Alok.\nHow is Nitin related to Gaurav?

A. Father
B. Uncle
C. Father-in-law
D. Brother-in-law

**Answer:** C. Father-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — Father-in-law (Correct):** From Statement I, Nitin is the father of Prateek and Prateek is the brother of Shreya, so Nitin is Shreya's father. From Statement II, Shreya is the wife of Gaurav. Therefore, Nitin is Gaurav's father-in-law.
- **A — Father:** Father of Gaurav is Alok, not Nitin.
- **B — Uncle:** Uncle is a parent's brother, not a wife's father.
- **D — Brother-in-law:** Brother-in-law is Prateek (Shreya's brother), not Nitin (Shreya's father).
- **Exam Trick:** Identify the spouse link (Shreya–Gaurav) to see which parent becomes an in-law.

### Q434 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Komal is the daughter of Rajat, and Rajat is the only son of Vinod.\nStatement II: Vinod is the father of Priya, who is unmarried.\nHow is Priya related to Komal?

A. Mother
B. Sister
C. Cousin
D. Paternal Aunt

**Answer:** D. Paternal Aunt
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Paternal Aunt (Correct):** From Statement I, Rajat is the only son of Vinod, and Komal is Rajat's daughter. From Statement II, Vinod is the father of Priya; since Rajat is Vinod's only son, Priya must be Vinod's daughter (Rajat's sister). Therefore, Priya is Komal's paternal aunt.
- **A — Mother:** Mother of Komal is Rajat's wife, whereas Priya is Rajat's sister.
- **B — Sister:** Sister would place Priya in Komal's generation, whereas Priya is in Rajat's generation.
- **C — Cousin:** Cousin would be the child of Priya, not Priya herself.
- **Exam Trick:** The phrase "only son" is a key gender clue: any other child of that parent must be female.

### Q435 · Blood Relations - Two-Statement

Read the following two statements and answer the question:\nStatement I: Varun is the husband of Nidhi, and Nidhi is the sister of Pankaj.\nStatement II: Pankaj is the son of Ashok, and Ashok is the husband of Meena.\nHow is Varun related to Ashok?

A. Son-in-law
B. Son
C. Brother-in-law
D. Nephew

**Answer:** A. Son-in-law
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Son-in-law (Correct):** From Statement I, Varun is the husband of Nidhi, and Nidhi is Pankaj's sister. From Statement II, Pankaj is the son of Ashok, so Nidhi is Ashok's daughter. Since Varun is married to Ashok's daughter, Varun is Ashok's son-in-law.
- **B — Son:** Son of Ashok is Pankaj, not Varun.
- **C — Brother-in-law:** Brother-in-law is Varun's relation to Pankaj, not to Ashok.
- **D — Nephew:** Nephew is one generation below Ashok's sibling, whereas Varun is married to Ashok's daughter.
- **Exam Trick:** A daughter's husband is always the son-in-law.

### Q436 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a row of students, Rohan is 15th from the left end and 18th from the right end. How many students are there in the row?

A. 31
B. 32
C. 33
D. 34

**Answer:** B. 32
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 32 (Correct):** Using the formula Total = Left + Right - 1, we get Total = 15 + 18 - 1 = 32. We subtract 1 because Rohan is counted twice (once from each end).
- **A — 31:** 31 results from mistakenly subtracting 2 instead of 1.
- **C — 33:** 33 is 15 + 18 without subtracting the double-counted position of Rohan.
- **D — 34:** 34 results from adding 1 instead of subtracting 1.
- **Exam Trick:** Total = Left + Right - 1 whenever the position of the same person is known from both ends.

### Q437 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a class of 45 students, Aditi ranks 12th from the top. What is her rank from the bottom?

A. 33rd
B. 34th
C. 35th
D. 32nd

**Answer:** B. 34th
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 34th (Correct):** Using the formula Bottom = Total - Top + 1, we get Bottom = 45 - 12 + 1 = 34. Therefore, Aditi's rank from the bottom is 34th.
- **A — 33rd:** 33 forgets to add 1 back after subtracting Aditi's position from the total.
- **C — 35th:** 35 adds 2 instead of 1 after subtracting 12 from 45.
- **D — 32nd:** 32 subtracts 1 instead of adding 1.
- **Exam Trick:** To find rank from the opposite end: Opposite Rank = Total - Given Rank + 1.

### Q438 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a queue of 60 people, Mohit is 24th from the front. What is his position from the back?

A. 36th
B. 38th
C. 37th
D. 35th

**Answer:** C. 37th
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 37th (Correct):** Using Back = Total - Front + 1, we get Back = 60 - 24 + 1 = 37. Therefore, Mohit is 37th from the back.
- **A — 36th:** 36 misses adding 1 for Mohit's own position.
- **B — 38th:** 38 adds 2 instead of 1 to 60 - 24.
- **D — 35th:** 35 subtracts 1 instead of adding 1 to 60 - 24.
- **Exam Trick:** Front + Back = Total + 1 holds for every person in a single linear queue.

### Q439 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a row of trees, a mango tree is 9th from either end of the row. How many trees are there in the row?

A. 17
B. 18
C. 16
D. 19

**Answer:** A. 17
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 17 (Correct):** "9th from either end" means the mango tree is 9th from the left and 9th from the right. Using Total = Left + Right - 1, we get 9 + 9 - 1 = 17 trees.
- **B — 18:** 18 forgets to subtract 1 for the tree being counted from both ends.
- **C — 16:** 16 subtracts 2 instead of 1.
- **D — 19:** 19 adds 1 instead of subtracting 1.
- **Exam Trick:** When a position is 'n-th from either end', the total count is simply 2n - 1.

### Q440 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a class, Samarth ranked 11th from the top and 27th from the bottom among those who passed an examination. If 5 students failed the examination and 3 did not appear, how many students were there in the class?

A. 42
B. 44
C. 45
D. 46

**Answer:** C. 45
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 45 (Correct):** First find the number of students who passed: Passed = 11 + 27 - 1 = 37. Now add the 5 who failed and 3 who did not appear: Total = 37 + 5 + 3 = 45 students.
- **A — 42:** 42 omits the 3 students who did not appear.
- **B — 44:** 44 forgets to count Samarth properly or drops 1 student in the addition.
- **D — 46:** 46 forgets to subtract 1 when calculating the number of passed students (11 + 27 = 38; 38 + 5 + 3 = 46).
- **Exam Trick:** In class-strength problems, apply Left + Right - 1 only to the ranked subset (passed), then add unranked groups (failed/absent).

### Q441 · Ordering & Ranking - Position Formula

In a row of girls facing North, Sneha is 19th from the left end. If she shifts 4 places to her right, she becomes 16th from the right end. How many girls are there in the row?

A. 36
B. 37
C. 39
D. 38

**Answer:** D. 38
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 38 (Correct):** Sneha was 19th from the left and shifted 4 places to her right, so her new position from the left is 19 + 4 = 23rd. In this new position, she is 16th from the right. Thus, Total = 23 + 16 - 1 = 38 girls.
- **A — 36:** 36 is obtained by mistakenly shifting 4 places to the left (19 - 4 + 16 - 1 = 30) or miscalculating 23 + 16 - 3.
- **B — 37:** 37 subtracts 2 instead of 1 from 23 + 16.
- **C — 39:** 39 forgets to subtract 1 from 23 + 16.
- **Exam Trick:** When a person facing North shifts k places to the right, add k to their rank from the left end.

### Q442 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a row of children, A is 10th from the left and B is 14th from the right. When they interchange their positions, A becomes 18th from the left. What is B's new position from the right?

A. 21st
B. 22nd
C. 23rd
D. 20th

**Answer:** B. 22nd
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 22nd (Correct):** When A and B interchange positions, A moves from 10th to 18th from the left, a shift of +8. Therefore, B's position from the right also increases by +8: 14 + 8 = 22nd.
- **A — 21st:** 21st uses a shift of +7 instead of +8.
- **C — 23rd:** 23rd adds 9 instead of 8 to B's rank.
- **D — 20th:** 20th uses +6 instead of +8.
- **Exam Trick:** In position-interchange questions, both persons experience the exact same magnitude and direction of rank change!

### Q443 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a row of boys, P is 13th from the left and Q is 17th from the right. After interchanging their positions, P becomes 21st from the left. How many boys are there in the row?

A. 37
B. 38
C. 36
D. 35

**Answer:** A. 37
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 37 (Correct):** After interchanging, P occupies Q's old seat. Thus, that single seat is 21st from the left (P's new rank) and 17th from the right (Q's old rank). Total = 21 + 17 - 1 = 37 boys.
- **B — 38:** 38 forgets to subtract 1 from 21 + 17.
- **C — 36:** 36 subtracts 2 instead of 1.
- **D — 35:** 35 miscalculates 21 + 17 - 3.
- **Exam Trick:** Total in interchange = (New rank of 1st person) + (Old rank of 2nd person) - 1.

### Q444 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a row of girls, Kavita is 8th from the left and Sunita is 12th from the right. When they swap places, Sunita becomes 19th from the right. What is Kavita's new position from the left?

A. 14th
B. 16th
C. 15th
D. 17th

**Answer:** C. 15th
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 15th (Correct):** Sunita's rank from the right changes from 12th to 19th, which is an increase of +7. Applying the same +7 shift to Kavita's rank from the left gives 8 + 7 = 15th.
- **A — 14th:** 14th adds +6 instead of +7.
- **B — 16th:** 16th adds +8 instead of +7.
- **D — 17th:** 17th adds +9 instead of +7.
- **Exam Trick:** New rank of Person 1 = Old rank of Person 1 + (New rank of Person 2 - Old rank of Person 2).

### Q445 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a queue, Aman is 16th from the front and Bharat is 21st from the back. When they interchange positions, Aman becomes 25th from the front. How many people are sitting between Aman and Bharat?

A. 7
B. 9
C. 10
D. 8

**Answer:** D. 8
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 8 (Correct):** Aman's rank from the front changes from 16th to 25th, a difference of 25 - 16 = 9 steps. The number of people sitting strictly between two seats is always 1 less than the step difference: 9 - 1 = 8 people.
- **A — 7:** 7 subtracts 2 instead of 1 from the difference of 9.
- **B — 9:** 9 is the difference in ranks (25 - 16), forgetting to subtract 1 to exclude the endpoint seat.
- **C — 10:** 10 adds 1 instead of subtracting 1 from 9.
- **Exam Trick:** Number of persons between two interchanging people = |New Rank - Old Rank| - 1.

### Q446 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a row of students, Meera is 11th from the left and Priya is 15th from the right. After interchanging positions, Priya becomes 9th from the right. How many students are there in the row?

A. 19
B. 20
C. 18
D. 25

**Answer:** A. 19
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 19 (Correct):** When they interchange, Priya occupies Meera's old seat. Thus, that seat is 9th from the right (Priya's new rank) and 11th from the left (Meera's old rank). Total = 9 + 11 - 1 = 19 students.
- **B — 20:** 20 forgets to subtract 1 from 9 + 11.
- **C — 18:** 18 subtracts 2 instead of 1 from 9 + 11.
- **D — 25:** 25 mistakenly uses Priya's old rank (15 + 11 - 1 = 25) instead of her new rank.
- **Exam Trick:** Even in an overlapping interchange (where rank decreases), Total = (New rank of 1st) + (Old rank of 2nd) - 1 still works identically!

### Q447 · Ordering & Ranking - Interchanged Positions

In a row of 40 students, Ritu is 14th from the left and Sonal is 19th from the right. If they interchange their positions, what will be Ritu's new position from the left?

A. 21st
B. 22nd
C. 23rd
D. 20th

**Answer:** B. 22nd
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 22nd (Correct):** After interchanging, Ritu takes Sonal's original seat. Sonal's original position from the left is Total - Right + 1 = 40 - 19 + 1 = 22nd. Therefore, Ritu's new position from the left is 22nd.
- **A — 21st:** 21st is 40 - 19, forgetting to add 1.
- **C — 23rd:** 23rd adds 2 instead of 1 to 40 - 19.
- **D — 20th:** 20th subtracts 1 from 40 - 19 instead of adding 1.
- **Exam Trick:** After swapping, Person A's new position from the left is simply Person B's old position from the left.

### Q448 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a row of 50 students, A is 18th from the left and B is 22nd from the right. How many students are sitting between A and B?

A. 9
B. 11
C. 10
D. 12

**Answer:** C. 10
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 10 (Correct):** Here, 18 + 22 = 40, which is less than the total of 50 (non-overlapping case). The number of students between A and B is Total - (Left + Right) = 50 - 40 = 10.
- **A — 9:** 9 subtracts 1 unnecessarily from 50 - 40.
- **B — 11:** 11 adds 1 unnecessarily to 50 - 40.
- **D — 12:** 12 miscalculates 50 - 38.
- **Exam Trick:** Always compare (Left + Right) with Total first: if (Left + Right) < Total, Between = Total - (Left + Right).

### Q449 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a row of 30 students, X is 20th from the left and Y is 17th from the right. How many students are between X and Y?

A. 4
B. 6
C. 7
D. 5

**Answer:** D. 5
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 5 (Correct):** Here, 20 + 17 = 37, which is greater than the total of 30 (overlapping case). In an overlapping case, Between = (Left + Right) - Total - 2 = 37 - 30 - 2 = 5 students.
- **A — 4:** 4 subtracts 3 instead of 2 from 37 - 30.
- **B — 6:** 6 subtracts 1 instead of 2 from 37 - 30.
- **C — 7:** 7 is simply 37 - 30, forgetting to subtract 2 for X and Y themselves.
- **Exam Trick:** If (Left + Right) > Total + 1, it is an overlapping case and Between = (Left + Right) - Total - 2.

### Q450 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a row of students, P is 14th from the left, Q is 18th from the right, and there are 6 students between P and Q. What is the maximum possible number of students in the row?

A. 38
B. 24
C. 32
D. 40

**Answer:** A. 38
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — 38 (Correct):** The maximum possible number of students occurs in the non-overlapping arrangement: Max Total = Left + Right + Between = 14 + 18 + 6 = 38 students.
- **B — 24:** 24 is the minimum (overlapping) total, not the maximum.
- **C — 32:** 32 is 14 + 18, forgetting to add the 6 students between them.
- **D — 40:** 40 adds 2 extra students to 14 + 18 + 6.
- **Exam Trick:** Maximum Total = Left + Right + Between (no overlap).

### Q451 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a row of students, P is 14th from the left, Q is 18th from the right, and there are 6 students between P and Q. What is the minimum possible number of students in the row?

A. 26
B. 24
C. 25
D. 38

**Answer:** B. 24
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — 24 (Correct):** Since min(14, 18) = 14 > 6 + 1, an overlapping arrangement is possible. Using Min Total = Left + Right - Between - 2, we get 14 + 18 - 6 - 2 = 24 students.
- **A — 26:** 26 forgets to subtract 2 for P and Q themselves (14 + 18 - 6 = 26).
- **C — 25:** 25 subtracts 1 instead of 2.
- **D — 38:** 38 is the maximum (non-overlapping) total, not the minimum.
- **Exam Trick:** Minimum Total = Left + Right - Between - 2, provided min(Left, Right) > Between + 1.

### Q452 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a queue, Ankit is 12th from the front and Bala is 15th from the back. If there are 4 persons between them, what is the minimum possible number of persons in the queue?

A. 20
B. 22
C. 21
D. 31

**Answer:** C. 21
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — 21 (Correct):** Since min(12, 15) = 12 > 4 + 1, overlap is possible. Using Min Total = Front + Back - Between - 2, we get 12 + 15 - 4 - 2 = 21 persons.
- **A — 20:** 20 subtracts 3 instead of 2 from 12 + 15 - 4.
- **B — 22:** 22 subtracts 1 instead of 2 from 12 + 15 - 4.
- **D — 31:** 31 is the maximum total (12 + 15 + 4), not the minimum.
- **Exam Trick:** Always verify min(Front, Back) > Between + 1 before applying the overlap formula for minimum total.

### Q453 · Ordering & Ranking - Max/Min Persons

In a row of 42 boys, Amit is 25th from the left and Sumit is 23rd from the right. How many boys are between Amit and Sumit?

A. 5
B. 6
C. 3
D. 4

**Answer:** D. 4
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — 4 (Correct):** Here, 25 + 23 = 48 > 42, which is an overlapping case. Thus, Between = (Left + Right) - Total - 2 = 48 - 42 - 2 = 4 boys.
- **A — 5:** 5 subtracts 1 instead of 2 from 48 - 42.
- **B — 6:** 6 is 48 - 42, forgetting to exclude Amit and Sumit.
- **C — 3:** 3 subtracts 3 instead of 2 from 48 - 42.
- **Exam Trick:** Remember to subtract 2 in the overlap formula because both Amit and Sumit are counted in the sum (Left + Right) beyond the overlap.

### Q454 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Among five friends A, B, C, D, and E, A is taller than B but shorter than C. D is shorter than E but taller than C. Who is the tallest among them?

A. E
B. D
C. C
D. A

**Answer:** A. E
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — E (Correct):** From the first statement, B < A < C. From the second statement, C < D < E. Combining both chains gives B < A < C < D < E, so E is the tallest.
- **B — D:** D is shorter than E.
- **C — C:** C is shorter than both D and E.
- **D — A:** A is shorter than C, D, and E.
- **Exam Trick:** Write all comparison clues using a single direction (e.g., '<' or '>') so chains merge effortlessly.

### Q455 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Five boxes P, Q, R, S, and T have different weights. P is heavier than Q and R. S is heavier than P but lighter than T. R is not the lightest. Which box is the lightest?

A. R
B. Q
C. P
D. S

**Answer:** B. Q
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — Q (Correct):** P > Q and P > R, while T > S > P. Since R is not the lightest among the five boxes and Q and R are the only ones lighter than P, R must be heavier than Q. Thus the full order is T > S > P > R > Q, making Q the lightest.
- **A — R:** R is explicitly stated as not the lightest.
- **C — P:** P is heavier than both Q and R.
- **D — S:** S is heavier than P, Q, and R.
- **Exam Trick:** When an item is "not the lightest" among two remaining bottom candidates, it immediately fixes the bottom two positions.

### Q456 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Six students M, N, O, P, Q, and R scored different marks in a test. M scored more than only N and O. P scored less than only Q. R scored more than M. Who scored the third highest marks?

A. P
B. M
C. R
D. Q

**Answer:** C. R
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — R (Correct):** M scored more than only N and O, so M is 4th from the top (above N and O). P scored less than only Q, so Q is 1st and P is 2nd. R scored more than M, so R must take the remaining 3rd highest spot: Q > P > R > M > {N, O}.
- **A — P:** P is the second highest, as P scored less than only Q.
- **B — M:** M is the fourth highest, above only N and O.
- **D — Q:** Q is the highest scorer, above P.
- **Exam Trick:** "Scored more than only k people" fixes an item's exact rank from the bottom as (k + 1)th.

### Q457 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Among five brothers Aman, Bharat, Chirag, Deepak, and Eshan, Bharat is older than Aman but younger than Chirag. Deepak is older than Eshan and Chirag. If Eshan is younger than Aman, who is the second youngest?

A. Eshan
B. Bharat
C. Chirag
D. Aman

**Answer:** D. Aman
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — Aman (Correct):** From the clues: Chirag > Bharat > Aman, Deepak > Chirag, and Aman > Eshan (since Eshan is younger than Aman). Combining them gives Deepak > Chirag > Bharat > Aman > Eshan. Thus Eshan is the youngest and Aman is the second youngest.
- **A — Eshan:** Eshan is the youngest, not the second youngest.
- **B — Bharat:** Bharat is older than Aman and Eshan, making him the third youngest.
- **C — Chirag:** Chirag is older than Bharat, Aman, and Eshan, making him the second oldest.
- **Exam Trick:** Draw numbered slots 1 to N from highest to lowest and place fixed pairs before filling relative clues.

### Q458 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Five cars A, B, C, D, and E have different speeds. Car C is faster than only Car A. Car B is faster than Car D but slower than Car E. Which car is the second fastest?

A. Car B
B. Car E
C. Car D
D. Car C

**Answer:** A. Car B
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — Car B (Correct):** Car C is faster than only Car A, so C is 4th and A is 5th (slowest). Car B is faster than Car D but slower than Car E, giving E > B > D > C > A. Therefore, Car B is the second fastest.
- **B — Car E:** Car E is the fastest overall.
- **C — Car D:** Car D is slower than both Car E and Car B, making it third fastest.
- **D — Car C:** Car C is faster than only Car A, making it fourth fastest.
- **Exam Trick:** "Faster than only one" immediately locks the bottom two positions (4th and 5th out of 5).

### Q459 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Six friends K, L, M, N, O, and P have different heights. K is taller than L and M. N is shorter than O but taller than K. P is taller than O. Who is the third tallest?

A. O
B. N
C. K
D. P

**Answer:** B. N
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — N (Correct):** K is taller than L and M. N is taller than K and shorter than O, while P is taller than O. This gives P > O > N > K > {L, M}. Therefore, N is the third tallest.
- **A — O:** O is the second tallest, shorter only than P.
- **C — K:** K is fourth tallest, shorter than P, O, and N.
- **D — P:** P is the tallest overall.
- **Exam Trick:** Notice you don't need to know the relative order between L and M to answer who is third tallest!

### Q460 · Ordering & Ranking - Comparison

Among five sisters P, Q, R, S, and T, Q is younger than R and P. S is older than P. T is younger than Q. R is older than S. Who is the oldest?

A. S
B. P
C. R
D. Q

**Answer:** C. R
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — R (Correct):** From the clues: P > Q, R > Q, S > P, Q > T, and R > S. Combining these gives R > S > P > Q > T. Therefore, R is the oldest among the five sisters.
- **A — S:** S is younger than R.
- **B — P:** P is younger than both S and R.
- **D — Q:** Q is younger than R, S, and P.
- **Exam Trick:** To find the oldest/tallest quickly, eliminate anyone who is stated to be younger/shorter than at least one other person.

### Q461 · Linear Seating

Five friends A, B, C, D, and E are sitting in a row facing North. C is sitting in the middle. A is to the immediate left of C. D is to the immediate right of E, and B is at the extreme left end. Who is sitting at the extreme right end?

A. E
B. D
C. A
D. B

**Answer:** B. D
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — D (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 5 from left to right (facing North): B is at seat 1 (extreme left), C is at seat 3 (middle), and A is at seat 2 (immediate left of C). Since D is to the immediate right of E, E must be at seat 4 and D at seat 5. Thus the order is B, A, C, E, D, and D is at the extreme right end.
- **A — E:** E is at seat 4, to the immediate left of D.
- **C — A:** A is at seat 2, between B and C.
- **D — B:** B is explicitly placed at the extreme left end (seat 1).
- **Exam Trick:** When everyone faces North, your own left and right match the persons' left and right.

### Q462 · Linear Seating

Six persons P, Q, R, S, T, and U are sitting in a straight line facing North. P is sitting third to the left of Q. R is sitting at the extreme right end. S is to the immediate left of R and immediate right of Q. T is not sitting at any extreme end. Who is sitting at the extreme left end?

A. P
B. T
C. U
D. Q

**Answer:** A. P
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — P (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 6 from left to right (facing North): R is at seat 6 (extreme right). S is to the immediate left of R (seat 5) and immediate right of Q, so Q is at seat 4. P is third to the left of Q (4 - 3 = 1), placing P at seat 1, the extreme left end.
- **B — T:** T is explicitly stated as not sitting at any extreme end (occupies seat 2 or 3).
- **C — U:** U occupies the other middle seat (2 or 3), not seat 1.
- **D — Q:** Q is at seat 4, third from the right.
- **Exam Trick:** "3rd to the left" of position k (facing North) is simply position k - 3.

### Q463 · Linear Seating

Five girls K, L, M, N, and O are sitting in a row facing South. L is sitting in the middle. K is to the immediate right of L. M is to the immediate left of L. N is sitting to the immediate left of M. Who is sitting to the immediate right of K?

A. L
B. M
C. O
D. N

**Answer:** C. O
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — O (Correct):** Facing South reverses left and right: a person's right is toward the viewer's left, and their left is toward the viewer's right. From viewer's left to right (seats 1 to 5): L is at seat 3, K is at seat 2 (immediate right of L), M is at seat 4 (immediate left of L), and N is at seat 5 (immediate left of M). The remaining seat 1 belongs to O, who is to the immediate right of K.
- **A — L:** L is to the immediate left of K (seat 3), not immediate right.
- **B — M:** M is at seat 4, next to L and N.
- **D — N:** N is at seat 5, at the opposite end from O.
- **Exam Trick:** For South-facing rows, remember: Person's Left = Viewer's Right, and Person's Right = Viewer's Left.

### Q464 · Linear Seating

Six boys A, B, C, D, E, and F are sitting in a row facing North. B is between F and D. E is between A and C. A does not sit next to F or D. C does not sit next to D. If A is at the extreme left end, who is sitting to the immediate left of F?

A. B
B. E
C. C
D. D

**Answer:** C. C
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — C (Correct):** B is between F and D (forming F-B-D or D-B-F) and E is between A and C (forming A-E-C or C-E-A). Since A is not next to F or D, and C is not next to D, the only valid arrangement with A at the extreme left end is A, E, C, F, B, D. Therefore, C is sitting to the immediate left of F.
- **A — B:** B is to the immediate right of F, not immediate left.
- **B — E:** E is between A and C, two places to the left of F.
- **D — D:** D is at the extreme right end, two places to the right of F.
- **Exam Trick:** Group adjacent triplets like (A-E-C) and (F-B-D) into blocks first, then use negative adjacency clues to join them.

### Q465 · Linear Seating

Seven persons P, Q, R, S, T, U, and V are sitting in a row facing North. T is sitting exactly in the middle. P is sitting at the extreme left end. Q is sitting to the immediate right of P. R is sitting to the immediate left of T. U is sitting at the extreme right end. S is sitting to the immediate left of U. Who is sitting to the immediate right of T?

A. Q
B. R
C. S
D. V

**Answer:** D. V
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — V (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 7 from left to right: P is at 1, T is at 4 (middle), and U is at 7. Q is at 2 (immediate right of P), R is at 3 (immediate left of T), and S is at 6 (immediate left of U). The only remaining seat 5 is occupied by V, who is to the immediate right of T.
- **A — Q:** Q is at seat 2, between P and R.
- **B — R:** R is to the immediate left of T (seat 3), not immediate right.
- **C — S:** S is at seat 6, to the immediate left of U.
- **Exam Trick:** Fill all fixed-end and middle-seat clues first; the remaining single empty slot immediately identifies the last person.

### Q466 · Linear Seating

Five persons A, B, C, D, and E are sitting in a row from left to right. A is at the extreme left end and faces North. C is sitting second to the right of A and faces South. B is sitting to the immediate right of C (with respect to C's facing direction). D is sitting at the extreme right end and faces North. Who is sitting to the immediate left of D?

A. E
B. C
C. B
D. A

**Answer:** A. E
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — E (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 5 from left to right: A is at 1 (facing North), so 2nd to the right of A is seat 3, where C sits (facing South). Since C faces South, C's immediate right is seat 2, so B is at 2. D is at 5 (facing North), leaving seat 4 for E. Because D faces North, D's immediate left is seat 4, which is E.
- **B — C:** C is at seat 3, two places to the left of D.
- **C — B:** B is at seat 2, between A and C.
- **D — A:** A is at seat 1, at the opposite end from D.
- **Exam Trick:** In mixed-direction rows, draw a small arrow (↑ for North, ↓ for South) above each person before evaluating left/right clues.

### Q467 · Linear Seating

Six friends M, N, O, P, Q, and R are sitting in two parallel rows of three each, facing each other. Row 1 (M, N, O) faces South, and Row 2 (P, Q, R) faces North. M sits opposite P. N sits to the immediate right of M. R sits at the extreme right end of Row 2. Who sits opposite O?

A. P
B. R
C. Q
D. N

**Answer:** B. R
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — R (Correct):** In Row 2 (facing North), R is at the extreme right end (seat 3). In Row 1 (facing South), N is to the immediate right of M (viewer's left of M), so M cannot be at seat 1. Since M is opposite P and R is at seat 3 of Row 2, M cannot be at seat 3 either; thus M is at seat 2 (opposite P), N is at seat 1, and O is at seat 3. Therefore, O sits opposite R.
- **A — P:** P sits opposite M in the middle seat (seat 2).
- **C — Q:** Q sits at seat 1 of Row 2, opposite N.
- **D — N:** N sits at seat 1 of Row 1, in the same row as O.
- **Exam Trick:** In two-row facing-each-other puzzles, align both rows vertically by seat number (1, 2, 3) to see opposite pairs immediately.

### Q468 · Linear Seating

Five students P, Q, R, S, and T are sitting on a bench facing North. P is sitting next to Q, and R is sitting next to S. S is not sitting with T, who is on the extreme left end of the bench. R is in the second position from the right. P is to the right of Q and T. Who is sitting in the middle of the bench?

A. Q
B. R
C. P
D. S

**Answer:** C. P
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — P (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 5 from left to right: T is at 1, and R is 2nd from the right, so R is at 4. S is next to R and not next to T, which could be 3 or 5; however, P and Q must sit together (with P to the right of Q), leaving seats 2 and 3 for Q and P, and forcing S to seat 5. Thus the order is T, Q, P, R, S, and P is in the middle.
- **A — Q:** Q is at seat 2, to the immediate left of P.
- **B — R:** R is at seat 4, second from the right.
- **D — S:** S is at seat 5, at the extreme right end.
- **Exam Trick:** When two people must sit adjacent (like P and Q), look for consecutive empty slots to rule out placements that split the remaining seats.

### Q469 · Linear Seating

Six persons A, B, C, D, E, and F are sitting in a row facing North. D is sitting between F and B. A is sitting at the extreme left end. C is sitting to the immediate right of A. E is sitting to the immediate right of C and immediate left of F. Who is sitting at the extreme right end?

A. D
B. F
C. E
D. B

**Answer:** D. B
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — B (Correct):** Starting from the left end (seat 1): A is at 1, C is at 2 (immediate right of A), E is at 3 (immediate right of C), and F is at 4 (since E is to the immediate left of F). D is between F and B, which places D at 5 and B at 6 (the extreme right end).
- **A — D:** D is at seat 5, between F and B.
- **B — F:** F is at seat 4, between E and D.
- **C — E:** E is at seat 3, between C and F.
- **Exam Trick:** Chain immediate-neighbour clues together like dominoes from the fixed end to build the entire row in one pass.

### Q470 · Linear Seating

Seven friends K, L, M, N, O, P, and Q are sitting in a row facing South. K is at one of the extreme ends, and L is to the immediate left of K. M is sitting second to the left of L. N is sitting between M and O, where O is to the immediate left of N. P is sitting to the immediate left of O. Who is sitting at the other extreme end?

A. P
B. Q
C. O
D. N

**Answer:** A. P
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — P (Correct):** Facing South, a person's left is toward the viewer's right. Since L is to the immediate left of K and K is at an extreme end, K must be at viewer's seat 1 (leftmost), with L at 2. M is 2nd left of L (seat 4), N is at 5 and O at 6 (since N is between M and O with O to the immediate left of N), and P is at 7 (immediate left of O), with Q at 3. Thus P is at the other extreme end.
- **B — Q:** Q is at seat 3, between L and M.
- **C — O:** O is at seat 6, between N and P.
- **D — N:** N is at seat 5, between M and O.
- **Exam Trick:** If someone is at an extreme end and another person is to their left, that end must allow movement in the 'left' direction!

### Q471 · Circular Seating

Five friends A, B, C, D, and E are sitting around a circular table facing the centre. A is to the immediate right of B. E is sitting between A and D. C is to the immediate right of D. Who is sitting to the immediate left of B?

A. A
B. C
C. D
D. E

**Answer:** B. C
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **B — C (Correct):** Going counter-clockwise (right, when facing the centre) around the circle of 5 seats: place B at seat 1, so A is at seat 2 (immediate right of B). E is between A and D, placing E at 3 and D at 4. The remaining seat 5 is C (which is to the immediate right of D, i.e., +1 from 4). Thus, going clockwise (left) from B(1) leads to C(5), making C the immediate left of B.
- **A — A:** A is to the immediate right of B, not immediate left.
- **C — D:** D is two places to the left of B.
- **D — E:** E is two places to the right of B.
- **Exam Trick:** When facing the centre of a circle: Right = Counter-Clockwise (CCW), Left = Clockwise (CW).

### Q472 · Circular Seating

Six persons P, Q, R, S, T, and U are sitting around a circular table facing the centre. P is opposite Q. R is to the immediate right of P. S is to the immediate right of Q. T is sitting between P and S. Who is sitting opposite T?

A. R
B. S
C. U
D. P

**Answer:** C. U
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **C — U (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 6 counter-clockwise (so +1 is right, -1 is left): place P at 1 and Q at 4 (opposite). R is at 2 (immediate right of P), and S is at 5 (immediate right of Q). T is between P(1) and S(5), placing T at 6. The remaining seat 3 belongs to U, who sits directly opposite T(6).
- **A — R:** R is at seat 2, opposite S(5).
- **B — S:** S is at seat 5, opposite R(2).
- **D — P:** P is at seat 1, opposite Q(4).
- **Exam Trick:** In an even-numbered circle of N seats, the person opposite seat k is always at seat k + N/2.

### Q473 · Circular Seating

Six friends A, B, C, D, E, and F are sitting in a circle facing the centre. A is sitting second to the left of B. D is sitting opposite A. E is sitting to the immediate right of D. C is sitting between A and B. Who is sitting opposite E?

A. A
B. B
C. F
D. C

**Answer:** D. C
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **D — C (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 6 counter-clockwise (+1 = right, -1 = left): place B at 1. A is 2nd left of B (seat 5), D is opposite A (seat 2), and E is immediate right of D (seat 3). C is between A(5) and B(1), placing C at 6, and F takes the remaining seat 4. Therefore, C(6) sits directly opposite E(3).
- **A — A:** A is at seat 5, opposite D(2).
- **B — B:** B is at seat 1, opposite F(4).
- **C — F:** F is at seat 4, opposite B(1).
- **Exam Trick:** Draw a 6-spoke wheel and mark the first person at the bottom (6 o'clock) so 'left' and 'right' match your own hands!

### Q474 · Circular Seating

Eight friends P, Q, R, S, T, U, V, and W are sitting around a circular table facing the centre. P is sitting opposite T. Q is sitting to the immediate right of P. W is sitting to the immediate left of P. V is sitting second to the right of T. S is sitting between R and T, and R is sitting third to the left of U. Who is sitting opposite Q?

A. U
B. V
C. W
D. S

**Answer:** A. U
**Notes & Option Breakdown:**
- **A — U (Correct):** Numbering seats 1 to 8 counter-clockwise (+1 = right, -1 = left): place P at 1 and T at 5 (opposite). Q is at 2 (immediate right of P), W is at 8 (immediate left of P), and V is at 7 (2nd right of T). Since S is between R and T(5), S must be at 4 and R at 3, leaving seat 6 for U (confirming R is 3rd left of U). Thus U(6) sits opposite Q(2).
- **B — V:** V is at seat 7, opposite R(3).
- **C — W:** W is at seat 8, opposite S(4).
- **D — S:** S is at seat 4, opposite W(8).
- **Exam Trick:** In 8-person circular puzzles, start with an opposite pair (seats 1 and 5) to anchor half the table immediately.

### Q475 · Circular Seating
